id: work/sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare
lang: en
source_lang: ar
translators: []
---

## Summary
A series by Omar Abdelkafy in which he reads hadiths from Sahih al-Bukhari, explains them and draws lessons. This set holds episodes from the Assabile archive, and everything in it is the sheikh's speech as it stands in the machine transcript; what is not traced to a chain or is garbled in the transcript is marked as needing a source.

## Episode 01: introduction to the series and deeds are by intentions
- He opens by saying the series explains the hadiths of Sahih al-Bukhari, taking three or four hadiths in a session, and that these hadiths cover the whole religion, from revelation to prayer, worship, dealings and every side of life, all drawn from the Prophet's Sunna. See [[work/sahih-al-bukhari|Sahih al-Bukhari]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:03:00}
- He says Ibn Hajar spent twenty-five years explaining al-Bukhari's hadiths in Fath al-Bari, that al-Bukhari himself spent years gathering them and examining their narrators for soundness and trustworthiness, and that the community received the book with acceptance as the soundest book after the Book of God. See [[person/ibn-hajar-al-asqalani|Ibn Hajar al-Asqalani]], [[work/fath-al-bari|Fath al-Bari]] and [[person/al-bukhari|al-Bukhari]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the years of compilation and the community's acceptance are stated without a reference) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:29:00}
- He says that whoever questions a hadith must be a specialist in hadith, as one expects of a specialist in any other field. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:06:00}
- He says he will speak of the place of the Sunna in legislation and mentions people who say they follow the Quran alone. He holds that whoever rejects a sound hadith has committed disbelief, since lying against the Prophet is the same as denying his hadith, and mentions by sense that whoever lies deliberately against the Prophet should take his seat in the Fire. See [[concept/sunna|the Sunna]] and [[concept/hujjiyyat-al-sunna|the authority of the Sunna]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying on deliberate lying is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:30:30}
- He says al-Bukhari moved around to gather hadith so that narration from the Prophet through the Companions and their successors could be confirmed, and gives as an example a chain running from Nafi to Salim to Umar ibn al-Khattab for a hadith he introduces as saying that martyrs are of four kinds (the text of the hadith is garbled in the transcript). See [[concept/isnad|chain of transmission]] and [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]]. (needs_source: the hadith text is garbled and he names no collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:12:00}
- He says the Companions wrote hadith down, relates that Abdullah ibn Amr ibn al-As kept a notebook he called the truthful one, and says those after them went on to write hadith. See [[person/abdullah-ibn-amr-ibn-al-as|Abdullah ibn Amr ibn al-As]]. (needs_source: the report of the notebook has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:20:00}
- He relates that Umar advised collecting the Quran and that the one he advised asked God for guidance until God opened his chest to it (the transcript garbles that man's name), and he stresses that a person should seek guidance through istikhara in his choices. See [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]] and [[practice/istikhara|istikhara]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the report of the collection has no chain and the name is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:18:30}
- He relates a report in which a man asked an imam how God rose over the Throne, and the imam answered that the rising is known, the how is unknown, belief in it is required and asking about it is an innovation (the transcript garbles the imam's name). See [[concept/istiwa-ala-al-arsh|God's rising over the Throne]] and [[passage/quran-20-5|Ta-Ha, verse 5]]. (needs_source: the report has no chain and the imam's name is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:25:00}
- He lists the grades of hadith as sound, good, agreed upon, strange and fabricated, and compares the levels among hadith scholars to army and police ranks, with titles such as hakim and hafiz. He says every hadith in al-Bukhari is sound, because al-Bukhari presented the book to Ahmad ibn Hanbal and Yahya ibn Ma'in, who were authorities in hadith. See [[person/ahmad-ibn-hanbal|Ahmad ibn Hanbal]] and [[person/yahya-ibn-main|Yahya ibn Ma'in]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the report that the book was presented to the two scholars has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:29:00}
- He says storytellers appeared who mixed hadith with tales, and relates a story in which two hadith scholars listened to a storyteller narrating a hadith about the reward of saying the declaration of faith, in which God creates from it a being with seventy thousand tongues (the end of the story is garbled in the transcript). (needs_source: the story has no chain, its end is garbled and the number of seventy thousand has no reference) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:32:00}
- He mentions by sense that the Prophet warned that a man would come reclining on his couch and, when a hadith of his was told, would say that between us and you is the Book of God, holding lawful only what he finds lawful in it, and that the Prophet said what the Messenger of God forbade is like what God forbade. (needs_source: the hadith is quoted by sense without narrator or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:35:30}
- He says the Sunna explains the Quran, as an explanatory memorandum accompanies a law: the Quran commands prayer and the Sunna shows the five prayers, their times and how they are done, and the Quran commands zakat while the Sunna sets its threshold and its kinds, such as farmland, horses and livestock. See [[concept/bayan-al-sunna-lil-quran|the Sunna as explanation of the Quran]], [[practice/salah|prayer]] and [[practice/zakat|zakat]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:41:00}
- He says whoever denies the Sunna denies the Quran, and likens it to one who says wine is not forbidden or who rejects prayer, whom he calls a disbeliever. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:39:00}
- He turns to the first hadith of Sahih al-Bukhari, which he says everyone knows by heart, and reads it as saying that deeds are by intentions and each person gets what he intended, so that one whose emigration was for God and His Messenger has it for them, and one whose emigration was for something worldly or a woman to marry has it for that. See [[concept/niyya|intention]]. (needs_source: the narrator's name is garbled in the transcript and no page is given) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:42:30}
- He says some scholars held that the whole religion revolves around four hadiths, of which this is the first, and stresses that intention matters, mentioning fasting in Ramadan as a case where it counts. This is his view. (needs_source: the scholars who held the four-hadith view are not named) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:45:00}
- He turns to the hadith that the lawful is clear and the unlawful is clear, giving drinking water as an example of what is plainly lawful by nature, and mentions by sense that sin is what stirs in the heart and what one dislikes people to learn of. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying on what stirs in the heart is quoted by sense from a garbled stretch) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:46:30}
- He says that whoever brings into the religion something found neither in the Book nor in the Sunna has it turned back on him, in the sense of the hadith on innovation. See [[concept/bida|innovation]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the hadith on innovation is quoted by sense without narrator or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:52:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:53:00}
- He reads the hadith that each person's creation is gathered in the mother's womb, forty days as a drop and forty as a clot, after which an angel is sent and it is written whether he will be wretched or happy, and he adds that provision is written in advance. See [[concept/qadar|divine decree]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the hadith is given without narrator or reference, and the numbers of days in it have no reference) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:52:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:52:30}
- He gathers the four hadiths as deeds by intentions, the lawful is clear and the unlawful is clear, whoever brings something new into this matter of ours, and the creation of man in the womb, and says Abu Dawud, author of the Sunan, looked at it from another angle, counting among them that part of the excellence of a person's Islam is leaving what does not concern him. See [[person/abu-dawud-al-sijistani|Abu Dawud al-Sijistani]] and [[work/sunan-abi-dawud|Sunan Abi Dawud]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the claim that the religion revolves around these hadiths and Abu Dawud's counting are stated without a reference) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:53:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:54:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:55:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:55:30}
- He explains the hadith that part of the excellence of a person's Islam is leaving what does not concern him, giving examples of people busy with what is not theirs, and relates that after the battle of Uhud a mother said paradise was for the one who had fallen and the Prophet answered, how do you know, perhaps he spoke of what did not concern him, adding that the matter is serious. See [[event/battle-of-uhud|the Battle of Uhud]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the report of the woman after Uhud has no chain and is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:55:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:56:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:56:30}
- He mentions by sense the hadith that none of you truly believes until he loves for his brother what he loves for himself. See [[concept/iman|faith]]. (needs_source: the hadith is quoted by sense without narrator or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 00:59:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 01:01:30}
- Taking up a person who enters the mosque and finds another praying voluntarily, he says it is valid to pray an obligatory prayer behind someone praying a voluntary one, and mentions by sense the report of a man praying alone behind the row, about whom the Prophet asked who would give charity to this man by praying with him, and that another man stood at his right. This is his view. (needs_source: the report is quoted by sense without narrator or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 01:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 01:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 01:04:30}
- He says a worshipper must not rise, sit, bow or prostrate before the imam, and mentions by sense a warning that God may change the face of one who precedes the imam, naming a donkey (this stretch of the transcript is garbled). This is his view. (needs_source: the warning is quoted by sense from a garbled stretch) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 01:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 01:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 01:08:00}
- On reciting al-Fatiha behind the imam he mentions al-Shafi'i, and says that when the imam recites it the follower who listens is counted as one who recited. See [[person/al-shafii|al-Shafi'i]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 01:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 01:09:00}
- He says all worship begins with intention and mentions by sense the hadith that whoever intends a good deed and does not do it has a good deed written for him and ten if he does it, while whoever intends a bad deed and does not do it has nothing written against him, and one bad deed if he does it. (needs_source: the hadith is quoted by sense without narrator or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 01:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep01 01:10:00}

## Episode 02: the Companions, the hadith of intentions and the beginning of revelation
- He says that if the first miracle of the Prophet is the Quran, there is a second one beside it, the Companions, and that many wish they had lived in the Prophet's time though being there guaranteed nothing, since Abu Jahl and al-Walid ibn al-Mughira saw him and did not believe. He says the Companions who narrated the hadiths he begins with today include al-Harith ibn Hisham, the brother of Abu Jahl, and that Abu Jahl's own family had noble Companions, such as his son Ikrima. See [[person/abu-jahl|Abu Jahl]], [[person/al-walid-ibn-al-mughira|al-Walid ibn al-Mughira]], [[person/al-harith-ibn-hisham|al-Harith ibn Hisham]] and [[person/ikrima-ibn-abi-jahl|Ikrima ibn Abi Jahl]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:04:30}
- He says among the early Muslims was Abu Bakr, at whose hands five of the ten promised Paradise embraced Islam, naming Sa'd ibn Abi Waqqas, Abd al-Rahman ibn Awf and Abu Ubayda ibn al-Jarrah, and mentions by sense that whoever starts a good practice has its reward and the reward of those who act on it until the Day of Resurrection without lessening theirs, and the opposite for a bad one, so their reward lies in Abu Bakr's scale. See [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]], [[person/saad-ibn-abi-waqqas|Sa'd ibn Abi Waqqas]], [[person/abd-al-rahman-ibn-awf|Abd al-Rahman ibn Awf]] and [[person/abu-ubayda-ibn-al-jarrah|Abu Ubayda ibn al-Jarrah]]. (needs_source: the hadith is quoted by sense without a chain and the names have no reference) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:07:00}
- He applies the same idea to the conquest of Egypt, saying the reward of the Muslims who entered Islam there until the Day of Resurrection lies in the scale of the one who conquered it, the one who conquered it. See [[event/conquest-of-egypt|the conquest of Egypt]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:07:30}
- He relates that a man named Uqba set out with sixty thousand men, many of them Companions, that they settled in a hard land, and that Uqba called on the snakes and beasts to leave the valley and they went. He relates that the Muslim army came to a great river near al-Mada'in and one of its men said he stood on God and would cross, and that the army crossed as if on dry land without knowing of those behind them. See [[event/conquest-of-madain|the conquest of al-Mada'in]]. (needs_source: the reports have no chain and the men are not named) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:12:00}
- He relates that a man named Abu Hazim, offered a request by a ruler, asked whether it was for this world or the next, and said he would not ask the ruler for a thing of this world because the ruler did not own it. (needs_source: the report has no chain and the ruler's name is not clear) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:13:00}
- He says the Quran was not first written on paper but on the hearts of the Companions, so that each of them was like a Quran walking on the earth, and that this is the great miracle of the Prophet. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:14:30}
- He relates a woman Companion who went out on a campaign far from Medina, died as a martyr there, and was buried in a distant land where her grave is marked as the martyr woman. See [[place/medina|Medina]]. (needs_source: the report has no chain and the woman is not clearly named) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:16:30}
- He returns to the hadith of intentions, that deeds are by intentions and each person has what he intended, and that whoever's hijra was to God and His Messenger, his hijra is to God and His Messenger. See [[concept/niyya|intention]] and [[event/hijra|the hijra]]. (needs_source: the narrator and collection are not named in this stretch) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:18:00}
- He compares today's emigrants with the first ones, relating that Umm Salama set out and that the family of Abu Salama would not let her take their son, pulling the boy between them until his arm came out of joint. See [[person/umm-salama|Umm Salama]]. (needs_source: the report has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:18:30}
- He relates that the date-palm trunk the Prophet used to lean on moaned when he moved to the pulpit, and says that if an inanimate thing showed such loyalty, the Companions were more entitled to long for him. This is his view. (needs_source: the report has no narrator or reference) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:22:30}
- He relates that after the Prophet's death Bilal asked Abu Bakr to let him go out to Syria, that the Prophet then appeared to him in a dream asking about the estrangement, and that when Bilal returned to Medina and gave the call to prayer, the whole city was stirred as he spoke the declaration of faith. See [[person/bilal-ibn-rabah|Bilal ibn Rabah]], [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]], [[practice/adhan|the call to prayer]] and [[place/medina|Medina]]. (needs_source: the report has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:26:30}
- He says the hadiths of Sahih al-Bukhari are the most important after the Book of God, and that the Quran is not understood except through the Sunna. He says the intention belongs to the whole of the work, which has three parts, conviction of the heart, saying with the tongue and acting with the limbs. See [[concept/niyya|intention]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:27:30}
- He cites a saying that revelation has ended, so people are judged by what they show, and whoever shows good is trusted and whoever shows otherwise is not. See [[concept/wahy|revelation]]. (needs_source: the saying has no speaker and no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:28:30}
- He relates that a Companion killed a man who had spoken the declaration of faith, and that the Prophet asked what he would do with that declaration on the Day of Resurrection. (needs_source: the report has no chain and the Companion is not clearly named) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:29:30}
- He says grief is natural, mentions by sense that the eye weeps and the heart grieves yet one says only what pleases the Lord, and that God steadies hearts and remembrance of God comforts them. See [[concept/dhikr|remembrance of God]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:33:00}
- He says knowledge is learned to benefit and to act on, and that it should bear fruit in being God-fearing. See [[concept/ilm|knowledge]] and [[concept/taqwa|God-fearing]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:33:30}
- He mentions by sense that men who did not go on an expedition were written the same good deeds as those who went. (needs_source: the saying is quoted by sense without a chain and the expedition is not named) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:36:30}
- He gives the hadith by sense that there is no hijra after the conquest but jihad and intention, and that whoever's hijra is for worldly gain or a woman to marry has only what he emigrated for, mentions the story of the man who emigrated for Umm Qays, and applies it to those who travel to the oil countries, Canada or Europe for this world. He says, as the scholars of exegesis do, that the lesson lies in the generality of the wording and not in the particular cause, so the ruling applies to every like case. See [[event/hijra|the hijra]] and [[concept/niyya|intention]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the hadith is quoted by sense without a chain and the story of Umm Qays has no reference) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:41:30}
- He relates that Ibn Mas'ud took many surahs from the Prophet's own mouth and knew for each whether it came by night or day, on plain or mountain, in Mecca or outside it, and that the Prophet put his hand on his head and called him a learned young man. See [[person/abdullah-ibn-masud|Abd Allah ibn Mas'ud]] and [[place/mecca|Mecca]]. (needs_source: the report has no chain and the number of surahs is not clear in the recording) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:44:00}
- He relates from Aisha that al-Harith ibn Hisham asked the Prophet how revelation comes to him, and that the Prophet said it sometimes comes like the ringing of a bell, and he retains what was said when it leaves, and sometimes the angel takes the form of a man and speaks to him, and that Aisha said she saw it descend on him on a bitterly cold day and leave him. See [[person/aisha|Aisha]], [[person/al-harith-ibn-hisham|al-Harith ibn Hisham]], [[concept/wahy|revelation]] and [[person/jibril|Jibril]]. (needs_source: the narrator is named but no collection or number is given in this stretch) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:53:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:53:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:54:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 00:54:30}
- He mentions by sense that the Prophet, addressing Mecca, said it was the most beloved of God's land to him and that he would not have left it had its people not driven him out. See [[place/mecca|Mecca]]. (needs_source: the saying is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 01:02:00}
- He cites the verse in which God declared the religion perfected and His favour complete, and mentions by sense that the Prophet said a servant was given a choice between this world and the company of the highest friend and chose the latter, that those present answered they would ransom him with their souls and families, and that he left the community on a white path whose night is as its day. See [[passage/quran-5-3|al-Ma'ida, verse 3]]. (needs_source: the hadiths are quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 01:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 01:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 01:04:00}
- He relates that a Muslim envoy said that God sent them to bring people out of the worship of servants to the worship of the Lord of servants and out of the narrowness of this world to the breadth of the next. (needs_source: the report has no chain and the envoy is not named) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 01:05:00}
- He turns to the second hadith on revelation and says it opens with true dreams, a part of prophecy which he reckons by the length of the prophetic mission, and that every dream he saw came true like the break of dawn. See [[concept/wahy|revelation]] and [[event/first-revelation|the first revelation]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the hadith is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 01:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 01:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 01:11:30}
- He says solitude was then made dear to the Prophet, so he would withdraw to a cave to devote himself to worship and seek the truth, and cites the verse in which God reminds him that He found him an orphan and gave him shelter. He says the Prophet would take provisions and return to his retreat, notes with wonder that he chose the month of Ramadan, and that the truth came to him in the cave of Hira when the angel came. See [[passage/quran-93-6|al-Duha, verse 6]], [[place/ghar-hira|the cave of Hira]], [[practice/sawm-ramadan|Ramadan fasting]] and [[event/first-revelation|the first revelation]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 01:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 01:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 01:16:00}
- He stresses that the Prophet was unlettered, asks how Muhammad could have learned and answers that God taught him what he did not know, citing by meaning the first verses revealed on reading and teaching by the pen. He relates that the angel pressed the Prophet hard, he answered that he was not a reader, and this was repeated until he feared for himself. See [[passage/quran-96-1|al-Alaq, verse 1]], [[passage/quran-96-5|al-Alaq, verse 5]], [[person/jibril|Jibril]] and [[event/first-revelation|the first revelation]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 01:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 01:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 01:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 01:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 01:21:00}
- He says the scholars have given several readings of the Prophet's fear for himself, such as illness or death, and that there is fear and fear, for the righteous fear for their deeds and fear showing off. This is his view. (needs_source: what he says of the scholars is not attributed to anyone) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 01:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 01:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 01:19:00}
- He relates that the Prophet was reassured that God would never disgrace him, since he helps in the misfortunes of truth, and that Waraqa ibn Nawfal, a cousin, is then named. See [[person/waraqa-ibn-nawfal|Waraqa ibn Nawfal]]. (needs_source: the recording is partly garbled and the report has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep02 01:22:30}

## Episode 03: the testimony to the Prophet from reality, from enemies and from revelation, and the report of Abu Sufyan
- He opens by recalling the previous episode: the first miracle of the Prophet is the Book of God, and the second, as he explained earlier, is taken up in this episode. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep03 00:01:00}
- He says nobody stood before the Prophet without his greatness showing, and that the Companions, the people nearest to him, were also the ones who held him in the highest respect. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep03 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep03 00:06:00}
- He says he will present three testimonies to the Prophet's character: the testimony of reality, the testimony of his enemies and the testimony of revelation, and that the Prophet is the example to follow closely. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep03 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep03 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep03 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep03 00:08:00}
- He says the first mark of prophethood is trustworthiness, that the Quraysh described the Prophet by it before the message, and that a prophet cannot be dull-witted. See [[concept/amana|trustworthiness]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the description by Quraysh is stated without a report or reference) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep03 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep03 00:09:30}
- He tells of Rukana, whom he describes as the foremost wrestler among the Arabs, and of an exchange between him and the Prophet, and gives it as an example of an opponent who came to bear witness to him. See [[person/rukana|Rukana]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the story of Rukana is told without a chain; the transcript of the exchange is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep03 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep03 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep03 00:21:30}
- He relates that when the daughter of Hatim al-Tai was held captive, the Prophet released her, saying her father had loved noble character, and presents it as an example of the Prophet's conduct. See [[person/hatim-al-tai|Hatim al-Tai]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the story is told without a chain or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep03 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep03 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep03 00:24:30}
- He tells of a man who stood over the Prophet with a weapon while he rested under a tree and asked who would protect him, and offers it within his discussion of the testimony of reality. This is his view. (needs_source: the story is told without a chain and the transcript of it is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep03 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep03 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep03 00:27:30}
- He says that during the forty years before revelation nobody in history proved that the Prophet had lied, that a trustworthy man does not lie, that his words did not differ from his deeds, and that it was never shown that he wronged anyone. This is his view. (needs_source: a sweeping claim about history and the forty-year span, stated without a reference) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep03 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep03 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep03 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep03 00:30:30}
- He begins reading the report of Abu Sufyan ibn Harb, from the start of its chain with Abu al-Yaman, that word was sent to him while he was with a party of Quraysh on a trading journey. See [[person/abu-sufyan-ibn-harb|Abu Sufyan ibn Harb]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep03 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep03 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep03 00:35:30}
- He says the questioner asked which of them was nearest in lineage to the man who claimed to be a prophet, that Abu Sufyan answered that he was, and that the others were placed behind his back. See [[person/abu-sufyan-ibn-harb|Abu Sufyan ibn Harb]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep03 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep03 00:36:30}
- He relates from Abu Sufyan that the questioner asked whether anyone among them had said this before him, whether any of his fathers was a king, whether any who entered his religion left it, and whether they had accused him of lying before he said what he said, and that he answered no to each. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep03 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep03 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep03 00:44:00}
- He says the questioner asked what the man commands, and that Abu Sufyan answered that he commands people to worship God alone without partners, to leave what the forefathers said, and to pray and keep chastity. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep03 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep03 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep03 01:07:00}
- He relates that the Prophet said God chose Abraham from the descendants of Adam, then Ishmael from the descendants of Abraham, then Quraysh, so that he is the choicest of a line of chosen ancestors. This is his view. (needs_source: the hadith is given by sense without narrator or collection, and part of the line is unclear in the transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep03 00:52:30}
- He says the questioner reasoned that if anyone had said such a thing before, he would have taken the Prophet for a man imitating an earlier claim, and then draws a similar inference from the question about a king among his fathers. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep03 00:56:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep03 00:58:30}
- He says the questioner concluded that if what Abu Sufyan said was true, the man would come to rule the ground beneath his feet, and that he had known a prophet would appear but had not thought he would come from among them. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep03 01:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep03 01:07:30}
- He reads the Prophet's letter, which opens with peace on whoever follows guidance and calls the reader to accept Islam and be safe. See [[event/letters-to-rulers-and-kings|the letters to rulers and kings]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep03 01:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep03 01:10:00}
- He says the letter then calls the People of the Book to a common word, and he recites the verse that holds this call. See [[passage/quran-3-64|Al Imran, verse 64]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep03 01:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep03 01:12:30}

## Episode 04: faith has sixty-odd branches and modesty is one of them
- He recalls that the last lessons dealt with the Prophet's character through the answers Abu Sufyan gave to Heraclius, and says the Prophet had the testimony of his enemies, of events, and of his companions, quoting a wise Arab saying that the truth is what enemies bear witness to, and adding that the testimony of one who loves is coloured by passion. See [[person/abu-sufyan-ibn-harb|Abu Sufyan ibn Harb]] and [[person/heraclius|Heraclius]]. (needs_source: the saying of the Arab sage is quoted without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:03:30}
- He distinguishes an unintended mistake from a deliberate wrong, citing the words addressed in the story of Joseph to ask forgiveness for the sin, and mentions by sense that if people did not sin, God would bring others who sin and seek His forgiveness, so a person who slips once should not persist. See [[passage/quran-12-29|Yusuf, verse 29]]. (needs_source: the hadith is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:06:00}
- He says a Muslim must speak the truth even about someone he dislikes, citing that hatred of a people must not lead to injustice and that justice is nearer to piety. See [[passage/quran-5-8|al-Ma'ida, verse 8]] and [[concept/adl|justice]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:07:00}
- He tells of Ali, as commander of the faithful, standing in a case before the judge Shurayh against a Jewish adversary, sitting beside him as an equal, and of the judge ruling by the usual rules of evidence, after which the man declared the testimony of faith. See [[person/ali-ibn-abi-talib|Ali ibn Abi Talib]] and [[person/shurayh-al-qadi|Shurayh the judge]]. (needs_source: the story is told without a chain and parts of the details are unclear) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:11:30}
- He says the companions found no falsehood in the Prophet's word or deed, so their testimony to him was one of truthfulness. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:13:00}
- He reads the hadith with its chain up to Abu Hurayra, that the Prophet said faith has sixty-odd branches and modesty is a branch of faith, and says a branch means a degree or level, that another narration gives seventy-odd, and that modesty is tied to faith. See [[person/abu-hurayra|Abu Hurayra]], [[concept/iman|faith]] and [[concept/haya|modesty]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:23:00}
- He says he will go through the branches of faith as counted by Ibn Hajar in Fath al-Bari, and that the first is belief in God. See [[person/ibn-hajar-al-asqalani|Ibn Hajar al-Asqalani]] and [[work/fath-al-bari|Fath al-Bari]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:24:00}
- He cites a saying that if you want to know your place with God, look at what He has established you in, and tells listeners to thank God for placing them in congregational prayer, in speaking the truth, in following the Sunna, in caring for their family and in night prayer. See [[concept/shukr|gratitude]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying is quoted without naming who said it) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:16:30}
- He mentions by sense that the believer's affair is all good, since gratitude in ease and patience in hardship are both good for him. See [[concept/shukr|gratitude]] and [[concept/sabr|patience]]. (needs_source: the hadith is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:17:00}
- He says belief in God includes belief in His power and gives signs from nature: a leaf falling in a forest is known to God, a newborn knows to suckle, and fish return to the waters they came from, citing that God gave everything its form and then guided it, the answer given to Pharaoh. See [[passage/quran-20-50|Ta Ha, verse 50]], [[person/musa|Moses]] and [[person/firawn|Pharaoh]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the natural-history examples are given without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:33:30}
- He says belief in God includes belief in His names and attributes, and speaks of majesty and beauty, with fear drawn from His majesty and hope from His beauty, naming the Merciful, the Loving, the Forbearing and the Pardoner. See [[concept/asma-allah-al-husna|the Beautiful Names]], [[concept/asma-al-rahim|al-Rahim]], [[concept/asma-al-wadud|al-Wadud]], [[concept/asma-al-halim|al-Halim]] and [[concept/asma-al-afuww|al-Afuww]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:42:00}
- He explains by sense that al-Awwal means nothing is before Him and al-Akhir that nothing is after Him. See [[concept/asma-al-awwal|al-Awwal]] and [[concept/asma-al-akhir|al-Akhir]]. (needs_source: the explanation is by sense and the hadith it rests on is not named, and the transcript is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:42:30}
- He says belief in God goes with belief in the angels, whom he describes as luminous bodies who do not disobey what God commands them, and in His books and in the messengers sent before the Prophet; he adds that belief in God covers His essence, His attributes and that nothing is like Him. See [[concept/malaika|angels]] and [[concept/tawhid|tawhid]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:56:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:57:00}
- He turns to Solomon, to whom the wind, the beasts and the devils were made subject, and to the hoopoe that came from Sheba. See [[person/sulayman|Solomon]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:49:00}
- He relates the Prophet's likeness of himself and the prophets as a man who built a house and left the place of one brick, so that people circled it marvelling and asked why the brick was not placed. (needs_source: the hadith is related without naming the narrator or the collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:55:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:56:00}
- He relates that Ali used to answer those who doubted the hereafter that if it is real he has gained and if it is not he has lost nothing. See [[person/ali-ibn-abi-talib|Ali ibn Abi Talib]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the report is given without a chain and the transcript is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:57:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:57:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:58:00}
- He speaks of the kinds of God's blessings, some already present and some awaited, including water, sitting and sleep, and says what matters is to thank God for them. See [[concept/shukr|gratitude]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:58:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:59:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 00:59:30}
- He says God has decreed each person's moment and place, that the angel is sent to write the lifespan, and that a person may die in a land other than the one he lived in. See [[concept/qadar|divine decree]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the report of the angel writing the lifespan is given by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 01:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 01:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 01:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 01:02:00}
- He mentions by sense that if people relied on God as they should, He would provide for them as He does the birds, which go out hungry and return full. See [[concept/tawakkul|reliance on God]]. (needs_source: the hadith is quoted by sense without a chain and the transcript is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 01:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 01:06:00}
- He says belief in the last day includes belief in the grave, the resurrection, the scale, Paradise and Hell. See [[concept/akhira|the hereafter]], [[concept/mizan|the scale]] and [[concept/jannah|Paradise]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 01:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 01:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 01:14:00}
- He reads a hadith qudsi by sense in which God says that if the first and last of His servants were all of one heart it would add nothing to His kingdom, that if He gave every one of them what he asked it would lessen what He has only as a needle lessens the sea, and that He tallies their deeds, so whoever finds good should praise God. (needs_source: the narrator and collection of the hadith are not named) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 01:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 01:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 01:17:00}
- He mentions by sense that those who love one another for God's sake are in the shade of His throne, and lists love in God among the branches of faith. This is his view. (needs_source: the hadith is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 01:18:30}
- He mentions by sense that souls are conscripted hosts. (needs_source: the hadith is quoted by sense without a narrator, and the stretch around it is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 01:19:30}
- He closes by saying the branches of faith begin with love of God and then love of the Prophet, and cover both belief and deeds. See [[concept/mahabbat-al-nabi|love of the Prophet]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep04 01:22:30}

## Episode 05: the branches of faith that belong to the heart
- He recalls the hadith of the previous lesson, that faith has some sixty-odd branches and that modesty is a branch of faith, and says the branches that belong to the heart were set out last time, twenty-four of them by his count, following the division he attributes to Ibn Hajar. See [[concept/iman|faith]], [[concept/haya|modesty]] and [[person/ibn-hajar-al-asqalani|Ibn Hajar al-Asqalani]]. (needs_source: the count and the division are attributed to Ibn Hajar without a reference) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 00:05:00}
- He says advice is heavy on the soul, above all when it runs against desire, and that a person should train himself to say no to what desire pulls toward, since what goes with desire is not for God. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 00:07:30}
- He reviews the first heart branches, belief in God, in His essence, His attributes, His oneness and His mercy, and in the fact that everything other than Him is created. See [[concept/tawhid|oneness of God]] and [[concept/iman|faith]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 00:12:30}
- He objects to the rule taught to schoolchildren that matter is neither made nor created from nothing, saying matter is created from nothing because its creator is God, who needs only to say to a thing that it be. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 00:13:30}
- He turns to belief in the angels, saying by sense that angels attend gatherings of remembrance and that God mentions those present before them, and that their hosts are beyond counting and known only to God. See [[concept/malaika|angels]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the report of angels attending gatherings is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 00:14:30}
- He says belief in the books means the Torah and the Gospel as they were before alteration, adding that no original copy of the Gospel has been found and that the Gospels were written some three hundred years after Jesus, and that the Qur'an is the book sent down on the Prophet. See [[work/tawrat|the Torah]] and [[work/injil|the Gospel]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the claim about the date of the Gospels is given without a reference) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 00:16:00}
- He continues with belief in the decree, its good and its bad, and belief in the last day, which he describes as two pillars set in the heart. See [[concept/qadar|divine decree]] and [[concept/akhira|the hereafter]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 00:17:00}
- He relates that the Prophet said the grave is the first of the stations of the hereafter, so whoever is spared its trial finds what follows easier and whoever is not finds what follows harder. See [[concept/al-barzakh|the barzakh]]. (needs_source: the hadith is given without narrator or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 00:18:00}
- On belief in the Hour and the resurrection he tells of a man who took a decayed bone, crushed it in his hand and asked the Prophet whether his Lord could gather it again, and says the answer came down that the One who brought it into being the first time will bring it back, and that He made fire from the green tree. See [[passage/quran-36-78|Ya-Sin, verse 78]], [[passage/quran-36-79|Ya-Sin, verse 79]] and [[passage/quran-36-80|Ya-Sin, verse 80]]. (needs_source: the story is told without a chain and the man is not named) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 00:20:30}
- He describes how a plant grows from a seed, drawing water and nourishment up through fine channels and exchanging gases with the air by day and night, and calls it a sign of God's power, adding that plants and animals are in the service of the human being. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 00:23:30}
- He relates that the Prophet passed two graves whose occupants were being punished, one for not guarding himself from urine and the other for going about with tale-bearing between people. See [[concept/namimah|tale-bearing]]. (needs_source: the narrator and the collection are not named) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 00:24:30}
- On belief in the reckoning and the balance he quotes the saying to hold yourselves to account before you are held to account and to weigh your deeds before they are weighed. See [[concept/mizan|the balance]]. (needs_source: the saying is quoted without attribution or chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 00:27:00}
- He tells that the surah on the earthquake was recited and that someone, on reaching its end, where the weight of an atom of good and of evil is mentioned, said that this was enough teaching of Islam for him. See [[passage/quran-99-7|al-Zalzala, verse 7]] and [[passage/quran-99-8|al-Zalzala, verse 8]]. (needs_source: the report is told without a chain and the speaker is not clearly named) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 00:28:00}
- He moves on to belief in Paradise and the Fire, as one of the branches of the heart. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 00:33:30}
- He speaks of love and hatred for God's sake, saying the best servants are those who make people love God and make God beloved to people, and that one dislikes a person's disobedience rather than the person. See [[concept/hubb-allah|love of God]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying on the best servants is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 00:36:30}
- He defines showing off as wanting to be seen and to have good said of you and not ill, and warns against doing a good act so that people will speak well of you. See [[concept/riya|showing off]] and [[concept/ikhlas|sincerity]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 00:45:00}
- He takes up fear and hope as branches of the heart, and in this context mentions standing before God on the day of resurrection and the moment of death. See [[concept/khawf|fear]] and [[concept/raja|hope]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 00:56:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 00:57:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 00:57:30}
- He relates a saying of Ibn Mas'ud about thinking well of God, and says that thinking well of God is not enough for one who does no good. See [[person/abdullah-ibn-masud|Abdullah ibn Mas'ud]] and [[concept/husn-al-zann-billah|thinking well of God]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the report of Ibn Mas'ud has no chain and the transcript is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 00:58:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 00:58:30}
- He speaks of gratitude as a branch of the heart, saying thanks is of the kind of the blessing and that thanking God for a blessing brings an increase of it. See [[concept/shukr|gratitude]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 01:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 01:03:00}
- He cites by sense that it was by a mercy from God that the Prophet was gentle with people. See [[passage/quran-3-159|Al Imran, verse 159]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 01:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 01:09:00}
- He mentions by sense that whoever leaves something for God's sake, God gives him better in exchange. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep05 01:16:30}

## Episode 06: the branches of faith continued, from Ibn Hajar's enumeration
- He recalls that in the previous lesson Ibn Hajar was listing the branches of faith from the hadith in Sahih al-Bukhari, says each branch is either a positive virtue to practise or a negative trait to leave, and adds that the difficulty lies not in hearing exhortation but in accepting it. See [[work/fath-al-bari|Fath al-Bari]], [[person/ibn-hajar-al-asqalani|Ibn Hajar al-Asqalani]], [[work/sahih-al-bukhari|Sahih al-Bukhari]] and [[concept/iman|faith]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:05:30}
- He relates by sense that the Prophet said hearts rust as iron rusts, and when asked what polishes them answered the remembrance of God. See [[concept/dhikr|remembrance]]. (needs_source: the hadith is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:06:00}
- He says a person can know his faults by five measures: setting himself against the Book of God, against the Sunna of the Prophet, against a scholar he trusts, with the help of a Muslim friend in God who tells him what he sees in him, and through what people say of him, even his enemies, since some of what they say may be true. See [[concept/sunna|Sunna]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:20:30}
- He says nothing should be set above the mushaf, neither papers nor anything else, because nothing rises above the Book of God. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:14:00}
- He mentions by sense that angels do not enter a house with a statue, in another wording a picture, and a dog, as part of weighing oneself against the Sunna. (needs_source: the hadith is quoted by sense without a chain or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:16:00}
- He recalls the previous lesson on reverence for the Prophet, including prayers upon him, and on hypocrisy, describing the hypocrite by sense as one who lies when he speaks, betrays when he promises and fails when trusted, and separating this conduct from the hypocrisy of a group. See [[concept/nifaq|hypocrisy]]. (needs_source: the hadith on the hypocrite is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:23:00}
- He lists the conditions of repentance as giving up the sin, regret, the resolve not to return, and giving rights back to their owners. See [[concept/tawba|repentance]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:25:30}
- He warns against wronging others in a will, relating by sense that a man may act like the people of paradise for sixty years and then wrong in his will and be among the people of the fire, and says to stay within God's limits. See [[concept/wasiyya-fiqh|will]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the hadith is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:28:00}
- He cites a saying that if people feared hell as they fear poverty they would be saved from both, if they sought paradise as they seek wealth they would win both, and asks listeners to take it to heart. See [[concept/khawf|fear]] and [[concept/raja|hope]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying has no chain or attribution) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:32:00}
- He says real poverty is poverty of the heart and of faith rather than of money, mentions by sense that wealth is not abundance of possessions but richness of the soul, and speaks of the person with thousands who is still not content. See [[concept/qanaa|contentment]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the hadith on richness of the soul is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:31:30}
- He recalls that the heart travelling to God has two wings, fear and hope, and that the previous week he covered contentment with God's decree and trust in Him among the branches. See [[concept/khawf|fear]], [[concept/raja|hope]], [[concept/rida-bil-qada|contentment with the decree]] and [[concept/tawakkul|trust in God]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:33:00}
- He relates by sense that the Prophet said one is not of us who does not honour our elder, show mercy to our young and know the right of our scholar, and says that those who show mercy to the old will find God provides them in their own old age people who show them mercy. See [[concept/rahma|mercy]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the hadith is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:35:30}
- He mentions by sense a report that God should be feared regarding dumb animals, and says that as there are societies for kindness to animals there should be societies for kindness to people. This is his view. (needs_source: the report is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:34:30}
- He says humility is not to regard one's own opinion as correct and the opinion of those who differ as wrong, and begins to illustrate it with al-Shafi'i, naming also Malik and Ahmad, though the example is only partly recoverable. See [[concept/tawadu|humility]] and [[person/al-shafii|al-Shafi'i]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:36:30}
- He describes four kinds of people by how quickly they anger and relent: quick to anger and quick to calm, slow to anger and slow to calm, slow to anger and quick to calm, and quick to anger and slow to calm. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:39:00}
- He lists leaving pride, envy and anger among the branches, says envy is not accepting God's apportioning among people, one rich and another poor, one healthy and another tested, and says to be content with what God has apportioned. See [[concept/kibr|pride]], [[concept/hasad|envy]] and [[concept/qanaa|contentment]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:48:00}
- He lists as further branches saying that there is no god but God, reciting the Qur'an, learning and teaching knowledge, and supplication, and says by sense that supplication is either answered, or a harm is lifted, or its reward is kept for the Day of Resurrection, adding remembrance and seeking forgiveness. See [[practice/dua|supplication]], [[concept/dhikr|remembrance]] and [[concept/istighfar|seeking forgiveness]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the hadith on supplication is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:50:30}
- He explains by sense a rule on the urine of a baby boy and a baby girl, that the boy's is sprinkled with water and the girl's washed. See [[concept/tahara|purity]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the rule is quoted without a chain or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:51:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:52:00}
- He turns to the major ritual washing after sexual intercourse and says the water must reach the roots of the hair, the scalp. See [[practice/ghusl-al-janaba|washing after janaba]] and [[concept/janaba|janaba]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:54:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:55:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:56:00}
- He says slaughter in Muslim lands differs from animals in Europe, America or Australia, which he says are stunned by electricity, gas or a blow to the brain and not bled. See [[practice/dhabihah|slaughter]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the claim about methods abroad is a general statement without a reference) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 00:59:30}
- He mentions by sense that the Prophet warned against relieving oneself in still water and in holes in the earth, and relates a remark that the Prophet left nothing without teaching it. (needs_source: the hadith is quoted by sense and the remark has no chain or speaker) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 01:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 01:03:30}
- He says a man's awra is from the navel to the knee, and relates by sense that the Prophet sat with his knee uncovered while Abu Bakr and Umar came in, then covered himself when a third man came, saying he is shy of a man of whom the angels are shy. See [[concept/awra|awra]], [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]] and [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the hadith is quoted by sense without a chain, and the third man's name is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 01:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 01:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 01:05:00}
- He mentions by sense the hadith to command children to pray, to strike them over it at ten and to separate them in their beds, so that girls and boys sleep apart. This is his view. (needs_source: the hadith is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 01:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 01:09:00}
- He says prayer gathers the branches of faith, obligatory and voluntary, and relates by sense that whoever guards the prayer, it will be a light and a proof for him on the Day of Resurrection. See [[practice/salah|prayer]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the hadith is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 01:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 01:10:00}
- He lists generosity as a branch, including feeding people. See [[concept/karam|generosity]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 01:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 01:15:00}
- He goes on to list as further branches the pilgrimage, the lesser pilgrimage, seclusion in the mosque, and emigrating from a land of idolatry. See [[practice/hajj|Hajj]], [[practice/umrah|Umrah]], [[practice/itikaf|i'tikaf]] and [[concept/hijra-fiqh|emigration]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 01:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 01:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 01:17:30}
- He says the false oath is the one that plunges its taker into the fire when he knows he is lying, and, answering a questioner about an oath, advises expiation. See [[concept/yamin-fiqh|oaths]] and [[practice/kaffarat-al-yamin|expiation of an oath]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the phrase on the oath is quoted without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 01:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 01:25:00}
- Answering a young woman who loves a neighbour whose family is opposed, he advises her to obey her parents and hopes God will give her good. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 01:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 01:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep06 01:25:00}

## Episode 07: faith has branches and modesty is a branch of faith
- He says the difficulty is not giving advice but accepting it, and that a speaker should try to hold himself to what he says while a listener should try to apply what he hears. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 00:01:00}
- He says there should be no enmity among Muslims, that a difference of opinion between scholars does not mean hostility, and that the names of the Muslim groups are only means of calling to God. He holds that they should return to the Book of God and the Sunna of the Prophet, work together where they agree and excuse one another where they differ, and he likens the community to people sharing one ship. This is his view. See [[concept/ikhtilaf|disagreement]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 00:06:30}
- He says every Muslim calls to God through his conduct at home, at work and among people, so that someone who does not know Islam may wish to be like that Muslim, and he adds that Western scholars speak of the Prophet's character in this way. This is his view. See [[concept/akhlaq|character]]. (needs_source: what he says Western scholars say about the Prophet's character has no named source and the sentence is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 00:07:30}
- He tells a caller to God to put the more important before the important, and he cites from Ibn Taymiyya that the wise person is not the one who tells good from evil but the one who tells the better of two goods and the lesser of two evils. He gives disputes such as where to place the hands in prayer as matters that should not distract from the priorities. This is his view. See [[person/ibn-taymiyya|Ibn Taymiyya]]. (needs_source: the saying of Ibn Taymiyya is quoted without a reference) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 00:09:00}
- He asks what in religion is actually harming people and answers with conduct: truthfulness, trustworthiness, respect for elders, and respect for time. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 00:10:00}
- He tells how the Prophet raised children, giving the first fruit of the season to the youngest child present, and he says one reason is that a small child has not yet been held accountable. This is his view. (needs_source: the report of the first fruit given to the youngest child is told by sense without a chain and the stretch is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 00:11:00}
- He resumes the hadith of al-Bukhari that he began earlier, which says that faith has branches and that modesty is a branch of faith, and he recalls that the previous Monday's lesson stopped at the point about emigration. See [[concept/iman|faith]], [[concept/haya|modesty]] and [[concept/hijra-fiqh|emigration]]. (needs_source: the hadith is given by sense without a narrator or collection named, and the opening lines are garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 00:17:30}
- He says that what God has made forbidden stays forbidden and what He has made lawful stays lawful until the Day of Resurrection, whatever people's opinions or customs become. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 00:21:00}
