- He speaks about oaths and says that a person who swears all the time loses his awe of the name of God, and that expiations must be paid and not treated lightly. This is his view. See [[concept/yamin-fiqh|oaths]] and [[practice/kaffarat-al-yamin|expiation of oaths]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 00:25:00}
- He says that on the Day of Resurrection the rights of people come due, and he tells by sense that the first matter judged between people is blood. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying that blood is the first matter judged is given by sense without a chain and the line is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 00:25:30}
- He tells by sense of a dream of a palace in Paradise and recalls Umar's jealous protectiveness over what is forbidden, and he uses it to speak about protective jealousy for one's family. This is his view. See [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]]. (needs_source: the report is told without a narrator or chain, and who saw the dream is unclear in the garbled stretch) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 00:39:30}
- He says the greatest wealth a person owns is righteous sons and daughters, who build a society. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 00:40:00}
- He points to verses chanted in the presence of the Prophet and the Companions, whose meaning is that without God they would not have been guided, would not have given charity or prayed, and he says singing is lawful on conditions, among them that it is not accompanied by musical instruments and that its words call only to virtue and good character. This is his view and it is contested among scholars. (needs_source: the verses chanted in the Prophet's company are told without a chain and the stretch is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 00:45:30}
- He relates by sense that the Prophet said the best of people are those best to their families and that he is the best to his family, and he urges fathers to fulfil the rights of their children. This is his view. (needs_source: the hadith is told by sense without a narrator or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 00:59:30}
- He advises parents to sit with their children and help them solve their problems with friends, and he tells by sense of three stages of seven years each in raising a child, the first being play. This is his view. (needs_source: the stages of seven years are told by sense without a chain and the stretch is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 01:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 01:02:00}
- He says Islam has an answer to every question, so a parent should equip himself with knowledge in order to answer his children. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 01:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 01:03:00}
- He tells by sense that the Prophet said your Lord, your self and your family each have a right over you, so give every holder of a right his due, and he applies it to the rights of children. This is his view. (needs_source: the hadith is told by sense without a narrator or collection and the line is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 01:05:00}
- He advises fathers to teach their children dutifulness by telling them how they treated their own father, so that the children will treat them the same way. This is his view. See [[practice/birr-al-walidayn|dutifulness to parents]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 01:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 01:05:30}
- He lists gentleness with servants and household helpers among the branches of faith. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 01:07:30}
- He relates by sense words said gently to a boy: say the name of God, eat with your right hand and eat from what is in front of you, and he presents it as the way to teach children manners. This is his view. (needs_source: the words are told by sense with no clear speaker, narrator or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 01:08:30}
- He lists cooperation in righteousness and enjoining what is right and forbidding what is wrong among the further branches of faith. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 01:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 01:10:30}
- He tells by sense that whoever takes a debt without intending to repay it is a thief, and whoever marries on a dowry without intending to pay it is an adulterer, and he gives these as examples of the branch of fulfilling trusts. This is his view. See [[concept/amana|trust]] and [[concept/mahr|dowry]]. (needs_source: the two sayings about debt and dowry are told by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 01:12:30}
- He lists honouring the neighbour among the branches of faith and mentions by sense that Gabriel kept urging the Prophet about the neighbour. This is his view. (needs_source: the report about Gabriel urging the neighbour's right is told by sense without a narrator or collection and the line is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 01:13:30}
- He lists earning money by lawful means, spending it rightly and avoiding waste among the branches of faith, and he adds returning the greeting, giving other Muslims their due, keeping harm away from people and avoiding idle amusement. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 01:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep07 01:14:30}

## Episode 08: the sweetness of faith and what blocks the heart from it
- He says a Muslim takes rulings from the Book, then the Sunna, then the consensus of the Companions, then the scholars' reasoned efforts, then public interest and preference, and that everything the Prophet said on religion is conveyed from God. He recalls the Prophet warning of a man reclining on his couch who says to hold only to what is in the Book. See [[concept/al-sunna-al-nabawiyya|the Sunna]], [[concept/ijma|consensus]], [[concept/ijtihad|ijtihad]] and [[concept/istihsan|juristic preference]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the warning is given by sense, no narrator or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:03:30}
- He says hadith scholars set rules to tell a sound hadith from an unsound one, so a person who doubts a hadith is answered that there is a science for it. He holds that the Prophet speaks from revelation in matters of religion, while in worldly matters he may be right or may err. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:05:00}
- He gives the story of the people pollinating their palm trees, to whom the Prophet suggested another way; the Prophet then told them that they know their worldly affairs better, while he gives them what comes from God on their religion. (needs_source: the story is told without a chain or collection and the transcript is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:06:00}
- He says the believer eats with one intestine and the disbeliever with seven, and teaches table manners from the Prophet's instruction to a boy: to say God's name, to eat with the right hand, and to eat from what is nearest, and with three fingers. He adds that one who forgot God's name at the start says it for the first and the last part of the meal. (needs_source: the reports are given by sense, no narrator, collection or chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:13:00}
- He tells of a man who looked at the Prophet's face and then at the moon and said that the Prophet was more beautiful than the moon. (needs_source: the report is told without a chain and the name of the speaker is garbled in the transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:14:30}
- He says the hadith he is explaining is in [[work/sahih-al-bukhari|Sahih al-Bukhari]], in the chapter on the sweetness of faith. He says every act of obedience has a sweetness and every disobedience a bitterness, that whoever tastes it knows, and he gives the night prayer, reflection on the Qur'an and the gatherings of knowledge as examples. See [[concept/iman|faith]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:16:30}
- He says a man is a man by faith and strength is by faith, and that wealth is not abundance of goods but the richness of the self. He adds that faith makes a person smell the fragrance of Paradise in this world, citing a Companion in a battle who said he could smell it. This is his view. (needs_source: the sayings are quoted by sense and the Companion is not clearly named) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:19:30}
- He describes the believer in the grave: the two angels ask whether he wants to return to the world, and he answers that he wants to return to his Lord, and then he sleeps like a bride whom only the dearest of her family wakes. He cites a verse about those who are sent back to the world. See [[passage/quran-6-28|al-An'am, verse 28]]. (needs_source: the report of the two angels and the bride is given by sense without narrator or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:21:00}
- He says trials can be a sign of God's care: God keeps His servants from the world as a family keeps a sick relative from food, and a child kept from play before exams understands it later. He tells the listener that if forty days pass without any trial or test, he should look at his relation with God. This is his view. (needs_source: the forty-day figure is given with no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:26:00}
- He reads the hadith: the Prophet said that whoever has three things finds the sweetness of faith, that God and His Messenger are dearer to him than all else, that he loves a person only for God, and that he hates returning to disbelief as he hates being thrown into the fire. He says scholars count it among the greatest hadiths that lead to God, and that love of God brings fear of Him. He then says he will take up what blocks the heart from this sweetness and what feeds it. See [[concept/iman|faith]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the hadith is given without a clear narrator in the transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:28:00}
- He says Paradise is surrounded by what the self dislikes and the Fire by desires, and names the believer's enemies: the self, desire, the world, the devil of humans and of jinn, and money, family and children. See [[concept/hawa|desire]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying on Paradise and the Fire is given by sense, no narrator or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:29:30}
- He says the devil can prompt a person to worry about showing off, and that the answer is to say that one prays for God and not for people. He relates that Abu Bakr said he was tried by four enemies: Iblis, the world, himself and desire. See [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]], [[concept/riya|showing off]] and [[person/iblis|Iblis]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying of Abu Bakr is quoted without a chain or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:31:00}
- He compares the heart to a house: when it is dark the devils live in it, as bats and owls live in darkness, and when the light of faith enters, the angels and the oneness of God dwell there. See [[concept/iman|faith]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:33:00}
- He tells how Umar, whose sister Fatima bint al-Khattab and her husband Said ibn Zayd had become Muslims, set out against the Prophet and came to Islam. In his telling the Prophet took hold of his garment, Umar declared the testimony of faith and asked whether they were not on the truth, the Prophet said yes, and they went out in two rows with Hamza, calling out takbir. See [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]], [[person/fatima-bint-al-khattab|Fatima bint al-Khattab]] and [[person/said-ibn-zayd|Said ibn Zayd]]. (needs_source: the story is told without a chain or collection and parts of the transcript are garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:39:00}
- He relates that when a ruler sent for Muhammad ibn Ismail al-Bukhari to come to his house, he refused to go, saying that when he magnified God in his heart he saw such men as small, and that if they imprisoned him it would be seclusion with God and if they killed him it would be martyrdom. He gives this as an example of the sweetness of faith. See [[person/al-bukhari|al-Bukhari]]. (needs_source: the story is told without a chain or source and the ruler is not named) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:42:30}
- He relates the Prophet's saying that God is beautiful and loves beauty, clean and loves cleanliness, good and accepts only what is good, and links it to cleanliness. (needs_source: the saying is quoted by sense with no narrator or collection and the occasion is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:45:30}
- He tells that on a journey the Prophet did not set himself apart from his companions, and said that God dislikes His servant to stand out from his companions. He then says that when the verse about God protecting the Prophet from the people was revealed, the Prophet sent the guards away, and he tells how Umm Jamil came with a stone looking for the Prophet, who was sitting with Abu Bakr, and did not see him. See [[passage/quran-5-67|al-Ma'ida, verse 67]], [[person/umm-jamil|Umm Jamil]] and [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]]. (needs_source: the stories are told without chains or collections and parts of the transcript are garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:50:30}
- He names desire as an obstacle and cites the Prophet's saying that none of you believes until his desire follows what the Prophet brought. He says people in his time follow whim, so that they say to follow what suits them. See [[concept/hawa|desire]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying is quoted by sense with no narrator or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:51:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:52:00}
- He says one should accept the truth even from far away and reject falsehood even from a close relative, a husband, a wife or a brother. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying is given with no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:54:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:54:30}
- He turns to the devil as an obstacle, in two kinds, of humans and of jinn, and says the devil makes a person lazy about prayer. He recalls the knots the devil ties on one who sleeps, and says the call to prayer helps one rise from sleep. See [[event/shaytan-ties-knots-on-the-sleeper|the devil ties knots on the sleeper]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the report of the knots is given by sense without narrator or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:55:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:55:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 00:56:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 01:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 01:05:00}
- He speaks of the devil in the house, and mentions one who enters his home without greeting his family. See [[event/shaytan-household-protections|household protections against the devil]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the report on entering the house is given by sense with no narrator or collection and the transcript is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 01:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 01:09:30}
- He says the devil afflicts a person with several matters, and names among them arrogance, which makes one refuse the truth even when it is right, envy, which does not accept God's allotment, and grudge. See [[concept/kibr|arrogance]] and [[concept/hasad|envy]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 01:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 01:16:30}
- He says the devil makes the forbidden look sweet and the lawful dull, so that a person compares what he has at home with what appears on screens. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 01:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 01:18:00}
- In the closing questions he answers a group of pilgrims whose agent could not buy the sacrificial animals, and says their pilgrimage is valid, God willing, and offers options such as having an acquaintance in the Hijaz buy and slaughter on their behalf. He adds that scholars' opinions differ, that every opinion except the Prophet's may be taken or left, and that his own may be mistaken. This is his view. (needs_source: the details of the question are partly garbled and no source is given for the options) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 01:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 01:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 01:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 01:21:00}
- To a mother whose two sons drowned, he says that they will enter Paradise, that God has put her to a great test, and that she should be patient. This is his view. (needs_source: no hadith or source is given for the ruling on the two sons) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 01:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep08 01:22:00}

## Episode 09: the pledge of the night of al-Aqaba, whoever is faithful to it has his reward with God
- He says the day's hadith is in the chapter of faith, and relates from a Companion who was one of the leaders on the night of al-Aqaba that the Prophet, with a group of his Companions around him, asked them to pledge not to associate anything with God, not to steal, not to commit adultery, not to kill their children, not to bring a slander they invent, and not to disobey in what is right; he says whoever is faithful to this, his reward is with God, whoever falls into one of these and is punished for it in this world, it is an expiation for him, and whoever falls into one and God conceals it, his matter is with God, who may forgive or punish. See [[event/pledges-of-aqaba|the pledges of al-Aqaba]] and [[concept/kaffara|expiation]]. (needs_source: the name of the narrator is not clear in the recording and no collection is named) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep09 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep09 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep09 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep09 01:15:00}
- He speaks of the prescribed punishments, naming flogging or stoning for adultery and flogging for slander and for drinking, and mentions by sense that the Prophet said one prescribed punishment carried out is better for the community than rain. See [[concept/hudud-penalties|prescribed punishments]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the hadith is quoted by sense without a chain and the length of the rain is unclear) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep09 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep09 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep09 00:04:30}
- He says that whoever gets to know God in ease, God knows him in hardship, and that whoever remembers God in his youth, God will not forget him when he is old. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep09 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep09 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep09 00:11:30}
- He says the scholars give reasons why people do not see the punishment of the grave, among them that belief in the unseen is kept and that God hides the state of the dead so that people do not shame one another, and he mentions by sense that the grave is either a garden of Paradise or a pit of Hell. See [[concept/adhab-al-qabr|the punishment of the grave]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the scholars' reasons are not attributed to anyone and the hadith is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep09 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep09 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep09 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep09 00:11:00}
- He relates that Jabir's father Abdullah fell as a martyr and his body was mutilated, that the Prophet had his face covered so Jabir would not see it, and that the Prophet told Jabir God spoke to his father without a veil and said to him wish, and he wished to be returned to the world to be killed again. See [[person/jabir-ibn-abdullah|Jabir ibn Abdullah]]. (needs_source: the report of Jabir's father has no chain and the names are not fully clear in the recording) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep09 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep09 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep09 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep09 00:16:30}
- He mentions by sense the saying that whoever brings into our religion something of his own, it is rejected, and speaks of two covenants, one with God to uphold His rulings and one with the Prophet to obey him in all he brought. See [[concept/bida|innovation]] and [[concept/ahd|covenant]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep09 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep09 00:23:00}
- He explains the word used in the hadith for the group around the Prophet, saying that through ignorance of the language it is now used only for a gang, while in Arabic it means a body of people with one aim. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep09 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep09 00:27:30}
- He says the first term of the pledge, not associating anything with God, is the rule of life and the base of faith, and that whoever returns to Islam returns to a tawhid pure for God. See [[concept/tawhid|tawhid]] and [[concept/shirk|associating partners with God]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep09 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep09 00:35:30}
- He says God does not accept a deed whose doer's heart is elsewhere, and relates that God said to Moses of one who calls on Him with his tongue while his heart is with another, adding that the heart must be sincere for God alone. See [[concept/ikhlas|sincerity]] and [[person/musa|Moses]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the report of God's words to Moses has no chain and the wording is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep09 01:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep09 01:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep09 01:04:30}
- He links the pledge not to kill children to abortion, speaking of a pregnancy late in a mother's life. See [[concept/ijhad|abortion]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep09 00:57:30}

## Episode 10: the people brought out of the fire and the undivided heart
- He reads a hadith from a Companion (the name is garbled in the transcript) that the Prophet said the people of Paradise enter Paradise and the people of the Fire enter the Fire, then God says to bring out of the Fire whoever has in his heart faith of the weight of a mustard seed. They are placed in a river called the river of life and sprout as a seed sprouts in the silt of a flood. He calls it one of the glad tidings the Prophet gave. See [[concept/iman|faith]]. (needs_source: the narrator's name and chain are not clear in the transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 00:04:30}
- He announces what he will take up: what raises and lowers faith and certainty in the heart, the diseases of the heart compared with those of the limbs, repentance from each, whether the heart holds minor and major sins, how the heart becomes sincerely God's, and how the hearts of the Companions and the Followers differed from the heart of the disbeliever, the sinner and the hypocrite. See [[concept/iman|faith]], [[concept/tawba|repentance]] and [[concept/ikhlas|sincerity]]. This is his plan for the lesson. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 00:05:30}
- He says the heart should be wholly for God, since wife, children, wealth and ideas can occupy it, and repeats that as a shared deed is not accepted, a shared heart is not accepted either: a deed given ninety-nine parts to God and one to someone else is not accepted, because God is free of need of partners. See [[concept/shirk|associating partners with God]] and [[concept/tawhid|divine oneness]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying that God is free of need of partners is given without a source, and the ninety-nine and one parts are an illustration) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 00:17:30}
- He tells that when Ali was about to strike an enemy and the man spat in his face, Ali held back, saying he had first meant to kill for God's sake and feared that striking now would be revenge for himself. He gives it as an example of a deed kept for God. See [[person/ali-ibn-abi-talib|Ali ibn Abi Talib]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the story of Ali is told without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 00:08:30}
- He says Ibrahim, the friend of God, held a high rank with God and that after Ismail's birth something crossed the angels' minds about Ibrahim (the stretch is unclear in the transcript); he then says God seemed to show that nothing was in the heart of His friend but God. See [[person/ibrahim|Ibrahim]] and [[person/ismail|Ismail]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the thought of the angels is told without a source and the transcript is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 00:16:00}
- He says an attachment to what creatures give is a loss: a heart that looks to people to give and to withhold is a divided heart, and a creature's giving can cut one off from divine oneness. He compares God's withholding to a father who keeps food from a sick child he loves. See [[concept/tawhid|divine oneness]]. This is his view. (needs_source: he quotes an unnamed scholar and the stretch is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 00:21:30}
- He says a disturbed heart is like a shaken tree that any wind moves, and contrasts the Companions who, when told that people had gathered against them, found their faith increased and said God is enough for them and the best disposer of affairs. See [[passage/quran-3-173|Al Imran, verse 173]] and [[concept/tawakkul|reliance on God]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 00:24:30}
- He quotes words in which a speaker says that whoever worshipped Muhammad should know Muhammad has died and whoever worshipped God should know God is living, then the verse asking whether, if the Messenger dies or is killed, they would turn back. The speaker's name is garbled in the transcript. See [[passage/quran-3-144|Al Imran, verse 144]]. (needs_source: the words are quoted by sense and its speaker is not clear) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 00:33:00}
- He tells of a man named Fadala who came meaning to strike the Prophet. The Prophet asked what would stop him now, he answered God, the sword fell from his hand, and the Prophet laid a hand on his chest after which, the man said, the Prophet became the most beloved of people to him. This is his view. (needs_source: the story is told without a chain and the name is not clear in the transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 00:37:30}
- He says strength of faith and certainty is stronger than a virus or a microbe He had mentioned handshaking and microbes in a garbled stretch just before. See [[concept/tawakkul|reliance on God]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 00:39:00}
- He says istikhara has no connection with dreams, that the Prophet taught it for every matter, and that one then relies on God. See [[practice/istikhara|istikhara]] and [[concept/tawakkul|reliance on God]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 00:44:00}
- He turns to the diseases of the heart and names three: pride, greed and a third he calls love, which he says every body has though the base show it and the noble hide it. The third term is not clear in the transcript. See [[concept/kibr|pride]], [[concept/hirs|greed]] and [[concept/qaswat-al-qalb-wa-amradih|hardness and diseases of the heart]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying is given without attribution and the third term is not clear) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 00:46:00}
- He says greed shows in craving for status, position and wealth, and relates by sense a report in which the Prophet asked whether his Companions knew of anyone stingier than another and said the stingiest is the one who is stingy with the greeting of peace. See [[concept/hirs|greed]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the report on stinginess with greeting is given by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 00:49:30}
- He says pride is another sign of a disturbed heart, and relates by sense a saying in which God says pride is His wrapper and greatness His cloak, and whoever contends with Him over them He casts into the Fire. See [[concept/kibr|pride]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying is given by sense, without chain or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 00:52:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 00:52:30}
- He says the believer should always rely on God and then says these are some of the diseases and the strongholds of the heart. See [[concept/tawakkul|reliance on God]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 00:58:00}
- He relates by sense that on the Day of Resurrection a caller summons groups in turn, such as those who prayed, remembered God and gave in charity, and then those who were patient when tried, thankful when given and turned to the One who neither forgets nor sleeps when denied. He says Paradise is for them. This is his view. (needs_source: the report is given by sense without a chain and the stretch is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 00:31:00}
- He says deeds are judged by their endings and gives the example of Pharaoh's sorcerers, who in a few moments believed and, he says, entered Paradise. This is his view. (needs_source: the example is told briefly and the stretch is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 01:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 01:01:30}
- He says the Prophet, when putting on a garment, would praise God who clothed him with what covers his nakedness and adorns him, then mentions a supplication asking God to make his character good as He made his form good (the transcript does not make clear where it was said or to whom it is attributed), and adds that God loves to see the trace of His favour on a servant. See [[practice/dua|supplication]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the supplications are given by sense without chain or collection and the stretch is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 01:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 01:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 01:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 01:10:00}
- He closes by returning to the opening hadith: by His mercy God says on the Day of Resurrection to bring out of the Fire whoever has in his heart a measure of faith. See [[concept/iman|faith]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep10 01:10:30}

## Episode 11: the hadith of kindness forgotten and the Fire, with the hereafter and the rights of husband and wife
- He says he will take three hadiths and begins with the first, which he says concerns everyone, and relates it from Ibn Abbas, though the chain as read out is garbled. See [[person/abdullah-ibn-abbas|Abdullah ibn Abbas]] and [[work/sahih-al-bukhari|Sahih al-Bukhari]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep11 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep11 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep11 00:03:00}
- He says the hadith, read by its sense, speaks of kindness shown to a wife all one's life, after which she sees something in him, though the wording as transcribed is partly garbled, and he ties it to his earlier talks on the rights of husband and wife. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep11 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep11 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep11 01:14:00}
- He prays that God grant open hearts and says knowledge leaves a visible mark: whoever tastes it, its effect shows in the home and in society. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep11 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep11 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep11 00:05:00}
- He speaks of the knowledge of tawhid and creed, the oneness of God, as the life of the religion. See [[concept/tawhid|tawhid]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep11 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep11 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep11 00:19:00}
- He says four things keep people from the worship of God and names kingship, ministry and wealth among them, while the fourth is garbled. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep11 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep11 00:08:00}
- He mentions a believing woman married to Pharaoh who asked for a house in paradise, and relates by sense that among those most severely punished in the Fire on the Day of Resurrection are the people of the table. (needs_source: the report has no chain or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep11 00:15:00}
- He relates by sense that every innovation is misguidance and every misguidance is in the Fire. See [[concept/bida|innovation]]. (needs_source: the hadith is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep11 00:21:30}
- He urges listeners to be children of the hereafter and not children of this world, saying each has its own children. See [[concept/akhira|the hereafter]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying has no attribution) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep11 00:25:30}
- He describes paradise by its peace and the absence of harm, pain, illness, sleep and menstruation, and says the texts describe its light, palaces and bliss as they describe the chains, shackles and punishment of the Fire. See [[concept/jannah|paradise]] and [[concept/jahannam|hell]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the description is given by sense without a reference) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep11 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep11 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep11 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep11 00:35:30}
- He relates by sense that paradise is entered through God's mercy while its ranks are apportioned by deeds. See [[concept/rahma|mercy]]. (needs_source: the hadith is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep11 00:34:30}
- He says that when the forbidden enters the lawful it ruins it, giving this as a saying of a wise man of the Arabs. See [[concept/halal-haram|the lawful and the forbidden]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying of the wise Arab is unnamed and has no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep11 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep11 00:39:30}
- He relates by sense that on the Day of Resurrection each prophet says myself, myself, with Abraham the Friend named among them, while the Messenger of God turns to his Lord. See [[concept/shafaa|intercession]]. (needs_source: the hadith is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep11 00:41:30}
- He says all he has said so far is an introduction, that talk of the Fire stirs hearts, and then sets out three questions on the hadith, whose wording is garbled. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep11 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep11 00:43:00}
- He says the gain from a gathering of knowledge lies in acting on it, and relates by sense that whoever acts on what he knows, God gives him knowledge of what he did not know. See [[concept/ilm|knowledge]]. (needs_source: the hadith is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep11 00:43:30}
- He says gratitude to God goes with gratitude to parents, for their upbringing, kindness and patience. See [[passage/quran-31-14|Luqman, verse 14]] and [[concept/huquq-al-walidayn|the rights of parents]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep11 00:44:30}
- He relates by sense the episode at al-Hudaybiyya when Quraysh stopped them: the Companions were reluctant to shave and end their pilgrimage state, then the Prophet went out, shaved and sacrificed, and they hurried to shave after him. See [[event/treaty-of-hudaybiyya|the Treaty of al-Hudaybiyya]]. (needs_source: the report has no chain or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep11 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep11 00:51:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep11 00:52:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep11 00:53:00}
- He relates that the Prophet's wives formed two groups, an elder and a younger, the younger under Aisha, while the leader of the other is garbled. See [[person/aisha|Aisha]]. (needs_source: the report has no chain or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep11 00:53:30}
- He relates that the Prophet kept apart from his wives for twenty-nine days and that Umar came to him, and that when the verse giving them the choice between worldly life and God, His Messenger and the hereafter was revealed, he addressed Aisha and told her to consult her parents. See [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]], [[person/aisha|Aisha]], [[passage/quran-33-28|al-Ahzab, verse 28]] and [[passage/quran-33-29|al-Ahzab, verse 29]]. (needs_source: the report has no chain or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep11 00:55:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep11 00:55:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep11 00:56:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep11 00:57:00}
- He mentions by sense that the Prophet said that were he to command anyone to prostrate to other than God he would command a wife to prostrate to her husband, saying one hadith explains another. (needs_source: the hadith is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep11 00:58:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep11 00:59:00}
- He says that after one's father and mother, the one who does the most good to a person is the one who taught him knowledge. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep11 00:59:30}
- He cites by sense a description of the good wife, who obeys when he commands and guards his property in his absence, and that a woman is a guardian in her husband's house and answerable for her charge. (needs_source: both sayings are quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep11 01:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep11 01:14:00}

## Episode 12: they are your brothers, and the calamity of the tongue
- He reads a narration in which a man met Abu Dharr, whose given name he says was Jundub ibn Junada, wearing a fine garment with his servant in the same one, and asked him about it; Abu Dharr said he had insulted a man by his mother, and the Prophet told him there was ignorance of the pre-Islamic kind in him and that servants are his brothers whom God placed under his hands, so he should feed them from what he eats, clothe them from what he wears, not burden them beyond what they can bear, and help them if he does. See [[person/abu-dharr-al-ghifari|Abu Dharr al-Ghifari]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 00:04:30}
- He says the hadith of the day concerns men and women alike because it is about the tongue, and that the Companions counted the tongue a blessing as a means of remembering God, while in our time he calls it a calamity because it can lead to the fire. See [[practice/dhikr|remembrance]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 00:06:00}
- He likens the heart to a pot and the tongue to the ladle that draws from it: what is in the pot, clean or spoiled, is what the ladle brings out, so the tongue shows the state of the heart, and he distinguishes a sound heart from one that sickness has dimmed. See [[concept/qalb-salim|the sound heart]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 00:08:00}
- He gives as the first sign of a sound heart pure tawhid, saying that such a person sees a gift from a creature as a deprivation and God's withholding as a gift, because God withholds only for good, and that rejoicing in a blessing such as a child can make one forget the One who gave it. See [[concept/tawhid|tawhid]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 00:11:30}
- He relates that Abu Hanifa dreamed he was digging up the Prophet's grave, woke frightened and told his teacher, who gave him good news that he would uncover the Prophet's Sunna and spread it among people. See [[person/abu-hanifa|Abu Hanifa]]. (needs_source: the report of the dream has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 00:15:00}
- He relates that the Prophet asked a group of Companions, among them Umar, Uthman and Ali, what they love: one answered sitting before him, looking at him and spending his wealth on him; another the love of truth, speaking it and forbidding wrong; Uthman answered feeding food, spreading peace and praying at night while people sleep; and one answered that he loves hunger, sickness and death. See [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]], [[person/uthman-ibn-affan|Uthman ibn Affan]] and [[person/ali-ibn-abi-talib|Ali ibn Abi Talib]]. (needs_source: the report has no chain and the speakers are not clearly named) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 00:22:00}
- He explains that answer by saying that when a person is ill his sins grow lighter, that hunger softens the heart, and that death is the meeting with God, mentioning by sense that whoever loves to meet God, God loves to meet him, and that a believer's groan in illness is written as remembrance of God. See [[concept/mawt|death]]. This is his view. (needs_source: both sayings are quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 00:30:30}
- He gives as another sign of a sound heart that a person leaves prayer and lessons of knowledge in a better state than before, as prayer forbids indecency and wrong, and that when he reads the Qur'an he feels it was written for him. See [[practice/salah|prayer]] and [[concept/ilm|knowledge]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 00:31:30}
- He cites a first speech on taking office, for which the recording gives conflicting names for the speaker, in which the weak is strong in the speaker's eyes until he takes the right for him and the strong is weak until he takes the right from him, and in which he asks the people to help him if he is right and to set him straight if he is wrong. (needs_source: the speech is quoted without a chain and the speaker is unclear in the recording) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 00:32:30}
- He says the sound heart feels only the remembrance of God, so that when a person has a court case or a problem he first recalls that nothing happens except by God's decree. See [[concept/qadar|divine decree]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 00:40:30}
- He says the sound heart grows in longing when it hears verses of paradise and in fear and awe when it hears verses of the fire. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 00:46:30}
- He compares the heart to a city with gates, each with its guard, naming among them a daily portion of the Qur'an and the assembly of knowledge, and says that when the guards are strong the gates stay shut. See [[concept/ilm|knowledge]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 00:49:00}
- He says lowering the gaze brings a sweetness of faith into the heart when it is done out of awe of God, and cites the verses telling believing men and women to lower their gaze and guard their modesty, as purer for them. See [[passage/quran-24-30|al-Nur, verse 30]], [[passage/quran-24-31|al-Nur, verse 31]] and [[concept/ghadd-al-basar|lowering the gaze]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 00:52:30}
- He says the weak heart shows itself at the first news of a calamity, in the face, in the tearing of clothes and in wrong words. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 00:58:30}
- He says assemblies of knowledge and remembrance are like batteries that recharge the heart each day, strengthening certainty and letting the light of faith in. See [[concept/ilm|knowledge]] and [[practice/dhikr|remembrance]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 01:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 01:01:30}
- He mentions that Ammar ibn Yasir fought when he was ninety-two. See [[person/ammar-ibn-yasir|Ammar ibn Yasir]]. (needs_source: the age is given without a chain and the battle is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 01:05:00}
- He says that if people set their afterlife right the world follows, that the world cannot be set right without religion, and that one should begin with what God first commanded, worship and tawhid. See [[concept/tawhid|tawhid]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 01:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 01:09:30}
- He tells a listener to look for his heart in three places, prayer, the assembly of knowledge, and reciting or hearing the Qur'an, and says that if he does not find it there the door of hope is still not closed, since God's door is open night and day. See [[practice/salah|prayer]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 01:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 01:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 01:12:00}
- He returns to the hadith, saying the Prophet told Abu Dharr that these were traits of pre-Islamic ignorance, and cites the verse forbidding one people to mock another, and women to mock women, and forbidding defaming one another and calling by nicknames. See [[person/abu-dharr-al-ghifari|Abu Dharr al-Ghifari]] and [[passage/quran-49-11|al-Hujurat, verse 11]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 01:12:30}
- He mentions by sense that all the words of a son of Adam count against him except in three cases, naming a teacher, a learner and a listener, and that whoever speaks should speak good or stay silent. (needs_source: both sayings are quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 01:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 01:15:00}
- He says every person has his own trial, that one cannot know what hearts and closed homes hold, and so one should not busy himself with the affairs of others. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 01:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 01:16:00}
- Answering a question about saying that God has spoken the truth at the end of reciting the Qur'an, he says there are views that it is not established by an authentic report from the Prophet, and that one may take either view. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 01:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 01:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep12 01:21:30}

## Episode 13: faith has some sixty branches and modesty is one of them
- He says this is the thirteenth session of the series, taken from Ibn Hajar's explanation of al-Bukhari, and that al-Bukhari relates hadiths on the degrees of faith, so the session asks what raises a person's faith and what lowers it. He adds that a person is not alone in the world and that the people around him, such as spouse, neighbour, child and employer, press on the branches of his faith. See [[work/sahih-al-bukhari|Sahih al-Bukhari]], [[work/fath-al-bari|Fath al-Bari]], [[person/ibn-hajar-al-asqalani|Ibn Hajar al-Asqalani]] and [[concept/iman|faith]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:02:00}
- He relates that the Prophet said the strong believer is better and more beloved to God than the weak believer, and recalls the verse in which the woman tells her father to hire Musa as the strong and trustworthy; he prays to be among the strong and trustworthy. He says the Muslim must be strong yet gentle and must never aggress against God's servants, and compares domestic fowl that, once fed on meat, turn into birds of prey. See [[person/musa|Musa]] and [[passage/quran-28-26|al-Qasas, verse 26]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the hadith is given by sense without a chain, and the remark on fowl has no reference) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:07:00}
- He tells a story in which two Companions, Ali among them, see Umar ibn al-Khattab busy with animals in the noon heat, and one of them says to Ali that whoever wants to look at the strong and trustworthy should look at Umar. See [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]] and [[person/ali-ibn-abi-talib|Ali ibn Abi Talib]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the story is given without a chain and the transcript garbles the names and details) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:05:30}
- He asks which is better in God's sight, a man living in a society where disobedience is almost impossible, as among the Companions, or a man living among temptations who resists them, and he holds that the second is better. He recalls the hadith of the seven whom God shades on the Day of Rising, among them the man who remembers God alone so that his eyes overflow. This is his view. (needs_source: the hadith of the seven is given by sense without a chain, and the comparison has no reference) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:11:00}
- He reads the hadith of al-Bukhari on the branches of faith, which says faith has some sixty-odd branches and modesty is a branch of faith, and relates that the Prophet said the highest is the declaration that there is no god but God and the lowest is removing harm from the road. He says the session will look at these degrees. See [[work/sahih-al-bukhari|Sahih al-Bukhari]], [[concept/iman|faith]] and [[concept/haya|modesty]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:22:30}
- He recalls the verse in which God explains the reality of righteousness and faith, that it is not only a matter of turning one's face east or west in prayer, and says the larger part of faith lies inside a person. He says the first people one faces are the nearest, spouse, children and neighbours, then the street, the quarter, the country and the whole world. See [[passage/quran-2-177|al-Baqara, verse 177]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:23:00}
- He says sincerity is the main key of faith and worship, like the key that starts a car, and that it is a secret among God's secrets. See [[concept/ikhlas|sincerity]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying that sincerity is a secret of God is given without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:29:00}
- He says honouring the Book of God includes not putting the mushaf on the ground and not placing anything above it. See [[concept/mushaf|mushaf]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:35:30}
- He speaks of signs showing a lack of sincerity and says a sincere deed is free of showing off, so that a person does not display even a small act to people. See [[concept/ikhlas|sincerity]] and [[concept/riya|showing off]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:37:00}
- He tells that Ali Zayn al-Abidin used to do good in secret and that people found out only when he died. See [[person/ali-zayn-al-abidin|Ali Zayn al-Abidin]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the report is given without a chain and its details are garbled in the transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:37:30}
- He says the miser lives like a poor man out of fear of poverty yet is reckoned with the rich on the Day of Reckoning, and quotes a saying of scholars that between a person and the winners stands the mountain of desire, meaning love of the world. This is his view. (needs_source: both sayings are given without a source or chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:42:30}
- He says the believer should not invest his heart in this world, since one who invests in it must face the call to leave it, and tells of a king who asks his vizier how good kingship would be if it lasted, to which the vizier answers that had it lasted it would not have reached the king; he adds that days pass in turn among people and compares the world to a ball passed from hand to hand. This is his view. (needs_source: the story of the king and the vizier is given without a chain or names) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:47:00}
- He says the branches of faith begin with faith in God, in His essence and His attributes, and names among them the Merciful, the Forgiving, the Loving, the Generous, the All-Seeing, the All-Knowing and the Kind; he adds that everything other than God is originated. See [[concept/asma-allah-al-husna|the names of God]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:52:00}
- He relates a report in which God, after showing Ibrahim the dominion of the heavens and the earth, tells him that if sinners return to Him He is their beloved and if they do not He is their healer, and that He is more merciful to His servants than a mother to her children; he adds a report in which God reminds Ibrahim that He has provided for a disbeliever in His dominion for fifty years. See [[person/ibrahim|Ibrahim]]. (needs_source: both reports about Ibrahim are given without a chain and are partly garbled in the transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:50:00}
- He says faith includes belief in the angels, that God sends guardians who protect a person while he sleeps, and he relates that angels succeed one another among people by night and by day, gathering at the dawn and afternoon prayers. See [[concept/malaika|angels]], [[passage/quran-6-61|al-An'am, verse 61]] and [[practice/salah|prayer]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the hadith of the succession of angels is given by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:52:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:53:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:53:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:54:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:54:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:55:00}
- He says the bearers of the Throne are eight, four with one kind of remembrance and four with another. This is his view. (needs_source: the report on the eight bearers is given without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:55:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:55:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:56:00}
- He mentions the Frequented House, which he describes as a place where angels enter every day and do not leave until the Day of Rising, some standing, some bowing and some prostrating, all glorifying God. See [[concept/bayt-al-mamur|the Frequented House]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the description of the Frequented House is given without a chain and the numbers are garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:57:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:58:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:58:30}
- He says there is another kind of angels, called the exalted, who know only God's oneness and remembrance and do not know that anything exists on earth, and recalls the verse in which God asks Iblis whether he was arrogant or among the exalted. See [[passage/quran-38-75|Sad, verse 75]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:58:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:59:00}
- He says the Companions used to see Jibril standing with the Prophet, and relates that the Prophet said Jibril kept urging him to treat the neighbour well. See [[person/jibril|Jibril]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the hadith on the neighbour is given by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:59:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 00:59:30}
- He moves to faith in the revealed books, and cites the verse of the Throne and the chapter of sincerity. See [[passage/quran-2-255|al-Baqara, verse 255]] and [[passage/quran-112-1|al-Ikhlas, verse 1]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 01:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 01:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 01:02:30}
- He gives arguments from creation: the saying of a bedouin that dung points to the camel and footprints to the traveller, so a sky with constellations and an earth with paths point to the Subtle and All-Aware; and a single leaf which, eaten by different creatures, yields silk in one case and dung in another. He recalls the verse on pure milk from between dung and blood. See [[passage/quran-16-66|al-Nahl, verse 66]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the bedouin saying and the leaf example are given without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 01:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 01:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 01:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 01:04:30}
- He says God gave believers repeated occasions to seek forgiveness across the day, the week, the month, the year and the lifetime, naming the daily prayers, the blessed days and months, and Ramadan to Ramadan, Hajj to Hajj and Umrah to Umrah. See [[practice/salah|prayer]], [[practice/sawm-ramadan|Ramadan fasting]], [[practice/hajj|Hajj]] and [[practice/umrah|Umrah]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the sequence of occasions is given by sense without the hadith text) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 01:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 01:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 01:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 01:12:00}
- He says regret is repentance and that weeping brings a person closer to God. See [[concept/tawba|repentance]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 01:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 01:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 01:16:30}
- He relates the report of three people who came to the Prophet's gathering: one took a place near and God took him in, and one turned away and God turned away from him; he adds that angels surround gatherings like this and tranquillity descends on them. This is his view. (needs_source: the report is given by sense without a chain or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 01:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 01:17:30}
- He reads a hadith in which Abu Bakr says from the Prophet's pulpit that the Prophet stood in that place the year before and was overcome by weeping, and relates that truthfulness goes with righteousness and both lead to Paradise, while lying goes with wickedness and both lead to the Fire, and that after certainty nothing better is given than well-being. See [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]] and [[concept/al-sidq-truthfulness|truthfulness]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the hadith is read without naming its collection and its chain is garbled in the transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 01:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 01:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 01:25:30}
- He says that even had Muhammad not been sent as a messenger, his character would be enough as a model, and cites fair-minded European scholars who he says judged that no reformer reached what Muhammad reached. See [[person/muhammad|Muhammad]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the European scholars are unnamed and no work is cited) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 01:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep13 01:26:30}

## Episode 14: the man of Najd who asked about Islam
- He says the series explains the hadiths of Sahih al-Bukhari through Ibn Hajar's Fath al-Bari, and that the hadith of the day is one everyone knows but whose explanation needs a long discussion. See [[work/sahih-al-bukhari|Sahih al-Bukhari]], [[work/fath-al-bari|Fath al-Bari]] and [[person/ibn-hajar-al-asqalani|Ibn Hajar al-Asqalani]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:03:00}
- He says al-Bukhari placed the Book of Faith first, then the Book of Knowledge, that faith is the foundation, and that it is the belief of ahl al-sunna that faith increases and decreases. See [[concept/iman|faith]] and [[concept/ilm|knowledge]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:04:00}
- He says faith rises with obedience, for example after a lesson of knowledge or a good prayer, and falls when a person lessens his daily portion of the Quran or grows to dislike gatherings of knowledge. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:05:30}
- He says the decline of faith among the Companions was measured differently from ours, using the picture of a pupil who whose marks slip from the top while another who failed every year now passes, and says a Companion's lapse was missing the first takbir behind the Prophet. This is his view. (needs_source: the report about a Companion missing the first takbir has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:06:30}
- He recalls the saying of the Prophet about guarding oneself from the fire even with half a date, and compares the giving of one who has little with that of one who has much. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying about guarding against the fire even with half a date is quoted without narrator or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:08:00}
- He says obedience first means keeping the obligations, then the voluntary acts, citing the saying that God says His servant keeps drawing near through voluntary acts until He loves him, and compares the obligations to a trader's capital and the voluntary acts to profit. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying that the servant keeps drawing near through voluntary acts until God loves him is given without narrator or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:13:30}
- He relates from Talha ibn Ubaydullah that a man of Najd with dishevelled hair came to the Prophet, his voice heard as a hum without its words being understood, until he came near and asked about Islam. The Prophet told him five prayers in the day and night; asked whether he owed anything else, he was told no, unless he volunteered; then the fast of Ramadan and the zakat, each with the same answer. The man turned away saying he would neither add to this nor subtract from it. See [[person/talha-ibn-ubaydullah|Talha ibn Ubaydullah]], [[place/najd|Najd]], [[practice/salah|prayer]], [[practice/sawm-ramadan|the fast of Ramadan]] and [[practice/zakat|zakat]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:18:30}
- He says four people are rewarded for a question: the asker, the one who answers, the listener and the one who loves them. He adds that the Prophet gave the man headings rather than details, as one who already knew them, and gives the prayer's rak'as as an example. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying about four who share the reward of a question has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:20:30}
- He says the narrator Talha is one of the ten given the good news of Paradise and that he and al-Zubayr are counted as disciples of the Prophet, as Jesus had disciples. See [[person/talha-ibn-ubaydullah|Talha ibn Ubaydullah]] and [[concept/ashara-mubashshara|the ten given the good news]]. (needs_source: the description of Talha and al-Zubayr as the Prophet's disciples is given without narrator or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:21:30}
- He says Islam stands on pillars, that every act of worship has a secret scholars taught us to look for, and that the prayer has conditions of validity that differ from its pillars, as the engineers and builders of a mosque are a condition of its existence while its walls are its structure. See [[practice/salah|prayer]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:24:30}
- He lists the first condition of the prayer as a body free of impurity and the next as purity from the greater and lesser impurity, and says the Prophet forbade praying while pressed by the need to relieve oneself or by hunger, so that nothing pulls the worshipper away. He mentions that Malik counts touching women among the things that break wudu. See [[concept/tahara|purity]], [[concept/najasa|impurity]] and [[person/malik-ibn-anas|Malik ibn Anas]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the report that the Prophet forbade praying while pressed by the call of nature or hungry is quoted by sense and the terms are garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:36:00}
- He lists the pillars of wudu as the face, forearms, head and feet, with rinsing the mouth and nose as acts the Prophet never left, and relates how the Prophet washed three times, ran water between the fingers and toes and reached the ears and the folds of the face. He relates that the Prophet said this was his wudu and that of the prophets before him and that whoever adds or subtracts has done wrong, and says a ring must be moved so water reaches under it. See [[practice/wudu|wudu]]. This is his view.
