- He relates by sense that al-Hasan and al-Husayn climbed on the Prophet's back while he was prostrating in prayer and the Companions raised their heads thinking something had happened to him, and that Umar sat with the two boys and remarked what a fine mount they had, to which the Prophet replied and what fine riders. See [[person/hasan-ibn-ali|al-Hasan ibn Ali]], [[person/husayn-ibn-ali|al-Husayn ibn Ali]] and [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]]. (needs_source: the report is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 01:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 01:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 01:02:00}
- He tells of the day the Prophet died: Abu Bakr was leading the Muslims in prayer, and the Prophet, feeling some relief, came to the front row, signalled to Abu Bakr and sat at his right. See [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]]. (needs_source: the report is partly garbled and has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 01:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 01:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 01:11:30}
- He tells that the Prophet asked the Companions why they wept so much over him and reminded them, by the verse that Muhammad is only a messenger before whom messengers passed, that messengers pass away. See [[passage/quran-3-144|Al Imran, verse 144]]. (needs_source: the report is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 01:13:00}
- He says truthfulness belongs to speech and to action, so a person's words should not differ from his deeds, and that a liar's face and manner give him away. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 01:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 01:15:00}

## Episode 24: love for the sake of God, the signs of God's love and the rights of the Muslim
- He cites a saying, whose speaker he does not name, that people are of four kinds: one who knows and knows that he knows, one who knows and does not know that he knows, one who does not know and knows that he does not know, and one who does not know and does not know that he does not know. He says one should learn from the first, wake the second, advise the third and keep away from the fourth. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying is given without naming its speaker or a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:09:00}
- He says the Prophet treated people with gentleness, above all a person who wrongs himself, and cites by meaning a saying that people are either afflicted or in well-being, so one should have mercy on the afflicted and ask God for well-being. He adds that of a person who seems mad one should say afflicted, and that the truly mad person is the one far from God. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying is given by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:07:00}
- He relates that Qatada reported from Anas that the Prophet said none of you believes until he loves for his brother what he loves for himself, and says al-Bukhari reports it. He says nearly every Muslim, of about a thousand million, has memorised it, and that people like talking about love more than acting on it. See [[person/anas-ibn-malik|Anas ibn Malik]], [[person/qatada-ibn-diama|Qatada]], [[person/al-bukhari|al-Bukhari]] and [[concept/ukhuwwa|brotherhood]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the number is his own estimate, and the attribution to al-Bukhari is garbled in the transcript, where Shuab al-Iman is also named) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:10:00}
- He tells a story set with al-Shafii in which visitors who say they are brothers in God are asked whether any of them would put a hand into his brother's pocket and take what he needs, as an illustration of brotherhood that goes beyond words. See [[person/al-shafii|al-Shafii]] and [[concept/ukhuwwa|brotherhood]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the story is told in a partly garbled stretch and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:10:30}
- He tells that Umar found Abu Bakr busy with his tongue (the wording is garbled) and asked him why, and that Abu Bakr answered that it had brought him into dangerous places, and he draws attention to how people treat their tongues. See [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]] and [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the report is given without a chain and the wording is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:12:30}
- He warns against treating people so that one wrong done to oneself wipes out all the good they have done, and cites a report that the angel of good deeds is commander over the angel of bad deeds, who waits before recording. This is his view. (needs_source: the report about the angels is given without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:16:30}
- He recites by meaning a saying reported from God: that if all people, first and last, were as devout as the most devout heart among them it would add nothing to God's dominion, and if as wicked it would take nothing from it; that deeds are counted and returned to people; and that whoever finds good should praise God and whoever finds otherwise should blame only himself. (needs_source: the divine saying is given by sense and no narrator is named) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:17:30}
- He says the lack of mutual love among people comes from lack of love of God, and that he will give signs by which one can tell whether God loves a person and whether a person loves God. He cites by meaning the Prophet's saying that none of you should wish for death because of a harm that befalls him, and that if he must, he should ask God to keep him alive while life is better for him and to take him when death is better. See [[concept/hubb-allah|love of God]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying about death is given by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:21:30}
- He gives the first sign as trial: he says God tests those He loves, and that when a person's illness or hardship lasts people say God has forgotten him, which he calls wrong because God does not forget. See [[concept/ibtila|trial]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:23:30}
- He compares God's tests to a father who takes his child to the doctor and the barber: the child dislikes the injection, the ointment and the haircut, but the father knows the child's good. He cites by meaning a divine saying that among God's servants is one whom only poverty sets right and one whom only wealth sets right, and likewise health and illness. He says he does not think God delights in anyone's illness. This is his view. (needs_source: the divine saying is given by sense and no narrator is named) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:29:30}
- He tells of a man who prayed for three years without an answer and heard a voice say that God was delaying because He loves to hear his voice. He says the supplication of the sick is not turned away, reports that the Prophet raised his hands in supplication, once until his cloak slipped from his shoulder, and cites by meaning a saying that God cleanses a servant through a bodily illness, a worry or a loss in his family, expiating sins that his works could not. See [[practice/dua|supplication]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the story of the man and the sayings are given without chains) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:33:00}
- He says the second sign is loving those whom God loves, first of all the Prophet, and cites by meaning the verse that those who love God should follow the Prophet and God will love them. He relates that Umar told the Prophet he loved him more than anything, that the Prophet answered that none of you believes until he is dearer to him than his child and himself, and that after Umar's reply the Prophet said, Now, O Umar. He says such love must be practical, not only theoretical. See [[passage/quran-3-31|Al Imran, verse 31]], [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]] and [[concept/mahabbat-al-nabi|love of the Prophet]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the account of Umar is given by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:35:00}
- He tells of a Companion captured by the Quraysh who is asked whether he would like to be safe among his family while Muhammad is in his place, and who refuses to let even a thorn prick the Prophet. He also quotes a statement ascribed to Abu Sufyan, speaking of the emperor of the day, that he had seen no one loved by others as the Companions of Muhammad loved him. See [[person/abu-sufyan-ibn-harb|Abu Sufyan ibn Harb]]. This is his view. (needs_source: both reports are given without chains and the names and setting are garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:37:00}
- He relates that al-Hasan al-Basri gave four reasons for his detachment from the world: that he knew his provision would not be taken by another, so his heart was at rest; that his work would not be done by anyone else, so he busied himself with it; that God watches him at every moment, so he is ashamed to be seen in disobedience; and that death awaits him, so he prepares. See [[person/al-hasan-al-basri|al-Hasan al-Basri]] and [[concept/zuhd|detachment]]. (needs_source: the saying is given without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:45:30}
- He says another sign that God loves a person is that He guides him to good and makes him glad at good that reaches Muslims and troubled at harm that reaches them. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:47:00}
- He tells that while the Prophet slept under a tree a man raised a sword over him and asked who would protect him now, and the Prophet answered God; the man then said, O Muhammad, be the best of captors, declared the faith, and said that he had come when no one on earth was more hated by him than the Prophet and that now no one was dearer. He uses it to show the mercy God placed in the Prophet. This is his view. (needs_source: the story is given without a chain, the man is not named and the passages are garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:49:00}
- He says love in the heart is a gift from God, and that loving the religion and those who uphold it is evidence of love of God. He cites by meaning the saying to love God for the favours with which He nourishes people, and to love the Prophet out of love of God, and says people should love God at least for the favours of health, knowledge and faith. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying is given by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:51:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:52:00}
- He relates by meaning a divine saying to David that describes the pious servant as one who intends harm to no one and does not go about with slander, and that God told him to love God and those who love Him and to make God beloved to His servants; asked how, God said by reminding them of His favours so that they love Him. See [[person/dawud|David]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the narration is given without a chain and the opening is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:51:00}
- He says rulings stay fixed though the world changes: what God said in His book and what the Prophet said in authentic hadiths remains until the Day of Resurrection, and no one can make lawful what God forbade or forbid what He allowed. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 00:57:00}
- He turns again to the hadith that none of you believes until he loves for his brother what he loves for himself, and lists by meaning the Muslim's rights over a Muslim: to greet him when you meet him, to answer him when he sneezes and praises God, to visit him when he is sick, and to follow his funeral. He distinguishes ziyara, visiting the healthy, from iyada, visiting the sick. He then relates by meaning a divine saying that God will say to a servant on the Day of Resurrection that He was hungry and was not fed and naked and was not clothed, and that had the servant fed or clothed a needy person he would have found it with God. See [[practice/greeting-salam|the greeting of peace]] and [[concept/ukhuwwa|brotherhood]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the rights and the divine saying are given by sense without chains and parts are garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 01:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 01:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 01:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 01:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 01:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 01:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 01:05:30}
- He says the hardest thing is to love a brother for God's sake. He relates by meaning that when a man visits a brother for God's sake an angel tells him he is good and that God forgives his sins, and he advises telling a brother that one loves him for God's sake. He recalls the saying that a believer to a believer is like a building whose parts strengthen each other. This is his view. (needs_source: the report about the angel and the saying about the building are given by sense without chains) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 01:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 01:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 01:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 01:07:00}
- He reads a report that he gives from Ibn Umar, from the Prophet, as a divine address: that the son of Adam is not content with little nor satisfied with much, and that a person who is safe in his home, healthy in body and holding his day's food should need nothing more of the world. He says contentment is a fruit of faith. See [[person/abdullah-ibn-umar|Abdullah ibn Umar]] and [[concept/qanaa|contentment]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the chain is garbled in the transcription and the wording is by sense) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 01:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 01:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 01:08:30}
- He reads a saying that names self-admiration, and, in a stretch the transcription garbles, apparently stinginess that is obeyed and desire that is followed. This is his view. (needs_source: no narrator is named and the wording is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 01:23:00}
- He says each person should reckon with the favours he has, such as health, rank, safety, a home and the movement of the body, and that people are stingy with them. He warns against using the tongue, itself a favour, for backbiting, slander and stirring up trouble between people, and says it should be used for the Qur'an and for good. See [[concept/shukr|gratitude]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 01:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 01:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 01:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 01:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 01:26:00}
- He tells that when Abu Bakr recited he kept his voice low and Umar recited aloud, and that someone told Abu Bakr to raise his voice and Umar to lower his; the transcript does not make clear who spoke. See [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]] and [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]]. (needs_source: the report is given without a chain, the speaker is unclear and the stretch ends garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep24 01:31:00}

## Episode 25: Mecca was made a sanctuary by God, and the conquest of Mecca
- He reads a hadith of the Sahih which he relates from Abu Shurayh: that he said to Amr ibn Sa'id, who was sending expeditions to Mecca, permit me, O governor, to tell you what the Prophet said on the day after the conquest, which my ears heard, my heart retained and my eyes saw as he spoke. See [[person/amr-ibn-said-ibn-al-as|Amr ibn Sa'id]] and [[event/conquest-of-mecca|the conquest of Mecca]]. (needs_source: the hadith has no number or chapter given in this stretch) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:08:30}
- He relates that after praising God the Prophet made clear that the sanctity of Mecca is from God and not from people, that one who believes in God and the last day may not shed blood in it or cut down a tree, that anyone who points to the Messenger's fighting there is to be told the permission was for him alone for an hour of a day, after which its sanctity returned as it had been the day before, and that the present should convey this to the absent. See [[event/fath-mecca-sermon|the sermon at the conquest of Mecca]], [[concept/hurmat-al-haram-makka|the sanctity of the sanctuary of Mecca]] and [[place/mecca|Mecca]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 01:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 01:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 01:18:00}
- He says this hadith takes his listeners to the conquest of Mecca and lets them live with the Messenger, that before the story he must first speak about the question of fighting (jihad) in Islam, which he says its opponents raise against it, and that he will then look at how the Prophet trained the Companions and taught them to conduct their affairs in supporting the word of God. See [[concept/jihad|jihad]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:12:30}
- He relates that when the Crusaders took Jerusalem they killed a great number of Muslims in a single day, until their horses waded in blood, and he sets this against what follows. See [[place/jerusalem|Jerusalem]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the report of the killing is given without a source and the figure differs between the two machine transcriptions, so none is stated) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:13:30}
- He relates that Umar sent armies, that when they reached Jerusalem its people would hand the city over only in the presence of the caliph himself, and that the army's commander sent word of this to Umar. See [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]] and [[place/jerusalem|Jerusalem]]. (needs_source: the report of Umar's journey is given without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:14:30}
- He relates that the people of Jerusalem found in their own books that the one who would open the city was a just ruler who worships one God, with seventeen patches on his garment, walking on the ground, and that a covenant with clauses was written for them in Umar's name. This is his view. (needs_source: the report and the number are given without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:22:30}
- He relates that seven centuries after that the Crusaders occupied Jerusalem, and mentions Salah al-Din al-Ayyubi in this connection. See [[place/jerusalem|Jerusalem]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the dates are given without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:25:00}
- He places the events he turns to in the eighth year of the Hijra and says the treaty of Hudaybiyya was very important to them. See [[event/treaty-of-hudaybiyya|the treaty of Hudaybiyya]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the year is given without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:28:30}
- He relates that when the Prophet left Mecca in the emigration he turned toward it with tears in his eyes and told it that it was the most beloved of God's lands to him and that had his people not driven him out he would not have left. See [[place/mecca|Mecca]]. (needs_source: the report is given without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:32:00}
- He relates that when the treaty was written the Prophet dictated the name of God, the Merciful, the Compassionate, that the other side objected that it knew neither the Merciful nor the Compassionate, and that when the Messenger's title was dictated it objected to that too, so that Muhammad son of Abd Allah was written. See [[event/treaty-of-hudaybiyya|the treaty of Hudaybiyya]]. (needs_source: the report is given without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:35:00}
- He relates the terms: no war between the two sides, whoever goes over from the Muslims to Quraysh is not returned by Quraysh, and a clause on those who come from the polytheists as Muslims to the Messenger. See [[event/treaty-of-hudaybiyya|the treaty of Hudaybiyya]]. (needs_source: the terms are given without a source and the text is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:38:30}
- He relates that Umar objected to the terms, that the Prophet answered that he is the Messenger of God and that God would not forsake him, and that Umar then went to Abu Bakr, who gave the same answer. See [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]] and [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]]. (needs_source: the report is given without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:40:30}
- He relates that the Prophet told the Companions to shave or trim their hair and leave the state of pilgrim sanctity, and that he then went into the tent of Umm Salama. See [[person/umm-salama|Umm Salama]]. (needs_source: the report is given without a chain and what followed in the tent is too garbled to state) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:40:30}
- He relates that a group, with masked young men of Quraysh among them, entered the sanctuary and killed twenty-three men of Khuza'a, allies of the Messenger. See [[group/khuzaa|Khuza'a]]. (needs_source: the report and the number are given without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:42:30}
- He relates that Quraysh met in the Dar al-Nadwa to avert war with the Muslims and decided to send someone of standing, choosing Abu Sufyan. See [[place/dar-al-nadwa|Dar al-Nadwa]] and [[person/abu-sufyan-ibn-harb|Abu Sufyan ibn Harb]]. (needs_source: the report is given without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:43:30}
- He relates that Abu Sufyan went to his daughter Umm Habiba, a wife of the Prophet whose earlier husband had died and whose marriage the Prophet had arranged through Ja'far ibn Abi Talib, that when he went to sit on the Prophet's bedding she did not let him, and that he says she would not let him sit even though he was her father. See [[person/abu-sufyan-ibn-harb|Abu Sufyan ibn Harb]], [[person/umm-habiba-bint-abi-sufyan|Umm Habiba]] and [[person/jafar-ibn-abi-talib|Ja'far ibn Abi Talib]]. (needs_source: the report is given without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:46:30}
- He relates that Abu Bakr's son, after becoming a Muslim, told his father something he had seen of him, and that Abu Bakr answered that had he seen him he would have killed him, and he says faith outweighs the tenderness between father and son. See [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the report is given without a chain and its wording is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:47:00}
- He relates that Abu Sufyan asked Abu Bakr to speak for him with the Prophet and was told he was not the one for it, that Umar refused firmly, that Ali, reminded of kinship, said it was not in his power, and that Fatima answered that her young sons had no part in such a matter. See [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]], [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]], [[person/ali-ibn-abi-talib|Ali ibn Abi Talib]] and [[person/fatima|Fatima]]. (needs_source: the report is given without a chain and the dialogue is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:50:00}
- He relates that a Companion of Khuza'a reached Medina the day after the attack, entered the Prophet's mosque, told him that Quraysh had broken the pact and recited verses, and that the Prophet rose and said he would help them. See [[group/khuzaa|Khuza'a]]. (needs_source: the report is given without a chain and the man's name is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:51:00}
- He relates that the Prophet told Aisha to prepare provisions and, asked where to, said to wherever God willed, that Abu Bakr found her preparing and learned from the Prophet that God had commanded him to open Mecca and harm no one, and that the army set out with ten thousand while the Companions did not know the destination until near Mecca, except Abu Bakr. See [[person/aisha|Aisha]] and [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]]. (needs_source: the report is given without a chain and the number is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:51:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:52:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:53:00}
- He relates that a Companion who had fought at Badr sent a letter with a woman to Quraysh warning that the Prophet meant to take Mecca, and that the Prophet ordered Umar to stop anyone leaving Medina and to close the roads to Mecca. See [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]]. (needs_source: the report is given without a chain and the Companion's name is garbled so it is not given) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:53:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:53:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:54:00}
- He relates that the woman took a road between the mountains, that she reached the area of Dhu al-Hulayfa, where the Prophet sent Ali after her telling him that Gabriel had told him of the letter, that she denied having one until Ali said he would search her, and that she brought it out of her braided hair. See [[place/dhu-al-hulayfa|Dhu al-Hulayfa]] and [[person/ali-ibn-abi-talib|Ali ibn Abi Talib]]. (needs_source: the report is given without a chain and the account is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:55:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:55:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:56:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:57:00}
- He relates that the Prophet sent word to the tribes around Medina that whoever believes in God and the last day should be in Medina by Ramadan, that the army left Medina on the tenth of Ramadan with tribes joining on the way, that he divided it under several commanders including Khalid ibn al-Walid, Sa'd ibn Ubada and al-Zubayr, and that a man was left to lead the prayers in Medina. See [[person/khalid-ibn-al-walid|Khalid ibn al-Walid]] and [[person/sad-ibn-ubada|Sa'd ibn Ubada]]. (needs_source: the report is given without a chain and the names of some commanders and of the man left in charge are garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:57:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:58:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:59:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 00:59:30}
- He relates that al-Abbas, leaving Mecca for Medina to declare his Islam, met the Prophet and, asked who was older, said the Messenger of God is greater though he himself was born before him, and that al-Abbas then rode the Prophet's mule to find Abu Sufyan so that no Muslim would kill him, and led him past the ranks of the Muslim army. (needs_source: the report is given without a chain and the wording is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 01:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 01:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 01:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 01:02:30}
- He relates that Umar, whose strides were longer, reached the Prophet's tent before al-Abbas, that al-Abbas then asked protection for Abu Sufyan and the Prophet said they had granted safety to whomever he had protected, and that al-Abbas asked Abu Sufyan whether it was not time he bore witness that there is no god but God, after which Abu Sufyan declared his Islam. See [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]] and [[person/abu-sufyan-ibn-harb|Abu Sufyan ibn Harb]]. (needs_source: the report is given without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 01:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 01:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 01:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 01:08:30}
- He says the Prophet was tender, and cites by meaning the verse that tells him that by mercy from God he was gentle with them and that had he been harsh of heart they would have dispersed from around him. See [[passage/quran-3-159|Al Imran, verse 159]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 01:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 01:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 01:08:00}
- He relates that the Prophet had Abu Sufyan kept in the tent until morning because his faith might not yet be complete and, as a war leader, he might try to learn the army's strength, and that the Prophet had every Companion light a fire so that Abu Sufyan would see the size of the army in the morning. See [[person/abu-sufyan-ibn-harb|Abu Sufyan ibn Harb]]. (needs_source: the report is given without a chain and the reasons are his own) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 01:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 01:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 01:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 01:10:00}
- He relates that in the morning the Prophet divided the entry into Mecca among several columns, naming Khalid ibn al-Walid, al-Zubayr, Abu Ubayda ibn al-Jarrah and Sa'd ibn Ubada, each to enter from a different side. See [[person/khalid-ibn-al-walid|Khalid ibn al-Walid]], [[person/abu-ubayda-ibn-al-jarrah|Abu Ubayda ibn al-Jarrah]] and [[person/sad-ibn-ubada|Sa'd ibn Ubada]]. (needs_source: the report is given without a chain and the directions differ between the two transcriptions) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 01:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 01:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 01:11:00}
- He relates that a cry was heard from the commander entering from the east, today is the day of slaughter, today the sanctuary is made lawful, and that the Prophet answered that today is a day of mercy. See [[person/sad-ibn-ubada|Sa'd ibn Ubada]]. (needs_source: the report is given without a chain and the speaker is not named clearly) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 01:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 01:12:00}
- He relates that the Prophet told Khalid that God had made lawful for the Prophet what he made lawful for no one before or after him. See [[person/khalid-ibn-al-walid|Khalid ibn al-Walid]]. (needs_source: the report is given without a chain and the dialogue before it is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 01:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 01:13:30}
- He relates that the Prophet entered Mecca bowing in such humility to the One who created him that his beard nearly touched the saddle. See [[place/mecca|Mecca]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the report is given without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 01:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 01:15:00}
- He relates that the Prophet asked Quraysh what they thought he would do with them after the long years of driving him out and fighting him, that they said good, a noble brother and the son of a noble brother, and that he answered with the words Yusuf said to his brothers, that there is no reproach on them today, and told them to go. See [[passage/quran-12-92|Yusuf, verse 92]] and [[place/mecca|Mecca]]. (needs_source: the report is given without a chain and the wording is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 01:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 01:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep25 01:16:30}

## Episode 26: do not lie about me, for whoever lies about me enters the Fire
- He says the five hadiths of the day are from Sahih al-Bukhari and all concern one matter, and reads one of them, which he relates from Ali: the Prophet told people not to lie about him and said that whoever does so enters the Fire. See [[person/ali-ibn-abi-talib|Ali ibn Abi Talib]], [[concept/kadhib|lying]] and [[work/sahih-al-bukhari|Sahih al-Bukhari]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 00:05:00}
- He relates that Abdullah asked his father why he did not narrate from the Prophet as others do, and that the father answered that what restrained him from narrating much was having heard the Prophet say that whoever intentionally lies about him has his seat in the Fire. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 00:08:00}
- He describes the father as a son of an aunt of the Prophet whose wife was Asma bint Abi Bakr, and mentions by sense that every prophet has a disciple whose place is in Paradise, naming Abu Bakr, Uthman and Abd al-Rahman ibn Awf, a merchant, among the ten given good news of Paradise. See [[person/asma-bint-abi-bakr|Asma bint Abi Bakr]], [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]], [[person/uthman-ibn-affan|Uthman ibn Affan]] and [[person/abd-al-rahman-ibn-awf|Abd al-Rahman ibn Awf]]. (needs_source: the saying on the disciple and the ten given good news are quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 00:06:30}
- He reads a further hadith from Salama, whose full name is not clear in the recording, in which the Prophet warns against attributing to him what he did not say, with the Fire as the end of whoever does it. (needs_source: the narrator's name is not clear in the recording) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 00:09:00}
- He reads further hadiths of the group, which he gives by sense: one on taking the Prophet's name and one on seeing him in a dream, and says the group ends with the same warning for whoever lies about him. (needs_source: the hadiths are quoted by sense, their wording is partly unclear in the recording, and no narrator or chain is given) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 00:10:00}
- He says he will speak on three subjects: first lying in general, second the station of the Prophet, and third the standing of the one who lies about him. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 01:02:00}
- He opens the first subject with a saying of the Prophet, given by sense, in which he asks people to guarantee certain things so that he guarantees them Paradise, among them keeping the hands back, and uses it as a way into speaking about the limbs. (needs_source: the saying is quoted by sense without a narrator or chain and its wording is unclear in the recording) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 00:12:00}
- He takes up where a follower stands beside the imam, saying on the imam's right, and says what changes when a third person arrives; some of the details of the steps are unclear in the recording. See [[concept/imamat-al-salat|leading the prayer]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 00:33:00}
- He takes up the follower's recitation of al-Fatiha behind the imam and says that in his view the imam's pause is not long enough to recite it, and mentions by sense that there is no prayer without the opening chapter of the Book. See [[concept/al-fatiha-umm-al-kitab|al-Fatiha, the mother of the Book]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying that there is no prayer without al-Fatiha is quoted by sense without a narrator or chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 00:42:30}
- He relates that Muhammad, the student of Abu Hanifa, asked his teacher about reciting al-Fatiha behind him, then goes on to compare the positions of Abu Hanifa and al-Shafi'i; the teacher's answer is unclear in the recording. See [[person/muhammad-ibn-al-hasan-al-shaybani|Muhammad ibn al-Hasan al-Shaybani]], [[person/abu-hanifa|Abu Hanifa]] and [[person/al-shafii|al-Shafi'i]]. (needs_source: the story has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 00:46:30}
- He gives, as a case where scholars weigh the strength of evidence, the saying known as water is from water and a report from Aisha on what makes the full bath obligatory, and says one trained in the field can tell the stronger evidence from the weaker; he adds that many hadiths turn out, when the hadith scholars examine them, to be fabricated or weak, which is why care is needed in saying the Prophet said something, given the warning about lying about him. See [[person/aisha|Aisha]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying and the report from Aisha are quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 00:48:30}
- He relates that the Prophet came in and found Aisha with her hand closed, calling a child to come and take something, and asked whether she had something in her hand; when she said yes, he said that had she had nothing in it a lie would have been recorded against her with God, and then asks what this means for all of our speech. See [[person/aisha|Aisha]] and [[concept/kadhib|lying]]. (needs_source: the report has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 00:50:00}
- He says people speak of a white lie and a black lie, and that for him all of it is lying. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 00:51:00}
- He says there are particular situations in which words are not counted as lying, and gives as one of them speaking well between people to reconcile them. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying behind this is not named) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 00:51:30}
- He relates that on the journey of the Hijra a man who did not know the Prophet asked Abu Bakr who was with him, and Abu Bakr answered that he was a man who guides him on the way, and he mentions this within his discussion of lying. See [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]] and [[event/hijra|the Hijra]]. (needs_source: the report has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 00:54:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 00:55:00}
- He recalls the Prophet's truthfulness, asking whether he was ever found lying or betraying a trust, and says a well-known Irish writer said every civilised person in the world should follow Muhammad for his call to trustworthiness, care for the neighbour and good character. (needs_source: the writer's saying is given without a name or reference) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 00:56:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 00:56:30}
- He relates by sense that the Prophet was asked whether a believer can be a liar and said no, and that a believer may have other failings such as miserliness but never lies, and says this closes the first subject. (needs_source: the hadith is quoted by sense without a narrator or chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 01:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 01:01:30}
- On the second subject, the station of the Prophet, he says God purified him in his limbs and in his heart, and that God took a covenant from the earlier prophets that they would believe in a final messenger if he were sent; he adds that though the last of the prophets he is mentioned first, citing verses that set revelation to him beside revelation to Noah and the prophets after him and that mention what God enjoined on Noah, Abraham and Moses. See [[passage/quran-3-81|Al Imran, verse 81]], [[passage/quran-4-163|al-Nisa, verse 163]] and [[passage/quran-42-13|al-Shura, verse 13]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 01:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 01:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 01:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 01:03:30}
- He mentions the station called the praised station, which he says suits only one human being on the Day of Resurrection, and relates by sense that the Prophet told people to ask God for him the wasila and the high rank, and said he hoped to be that one servant. See [[concept/al-maqam-al-mahmud|the praised station]]. (needs_source: the hadith is quoted by sense without a narrator or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 01:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 01:04:00}
- He says the Companions could not look long at the Prophet's face, and relates that a bedouin who came before him was in awe of him until the Prophet told him to take it easy, saying he was only the son of a woman of Mecca. (needs_source: the report has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 01:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 01:04:30}
- He relates that the Prophet stopped at a grave at al-Abwa' and wept, and when asked said it was his mother's grave: he had asked his Lord for permission to ask forgiveness for her and was not given it, and had asked to visit her and was given it; he recalls that she died when he was about six, on the way back from his maternal relatives of Banu al-Najjar in Medina, that Umm Ayman who cared for him was with him, and that he never forgot her tenderness. See [[person/amina-bint-wahb|Amina bint Wahb]], [[place/al-abwa|al-Abwa']] and [[person/umm-ayman|Umm Ayman]]. (needs_source: the report has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 01:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 01:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 01:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 01:06:30}
- He says the Prophet had several women who mothered him and names Halima al-Sa'diyya, and relates that the Arabs sent their infants to be raised in the desert where they learned sound Arabic, archery and riding, that Halima took the Prophet when he was an orphan, and that she saw her mount outrun the whole caravan and said this boy must be blessed. See [[person/halima-al-sadiyya|Halima al-Sa'diyya]]. (needs_source: the report has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 01:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 01:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 01:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 01:08:00}
- He relates that his foster sister al-Shayma came to him at a time when her people were fighting him and said she was al-Shayma, and that he spread his cloak for her and honoured her. See [[person/al-shayma-bint-al-harith|al-Shayma bint al-Harith]]. (needs_source: the report has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 01:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 01:09:00}
- He relates that Zaynab sent a necklace that had been her mother Khadija's in the matter of her husband, and that on seeing it the Prophet was moved and said it reminded him of Khadija, and told the Companions that if they wished they could release her captive. See [[person/zaynab-bint-muhammad|Zaynab bint Muhammad]] and [[person/khadija|Khadija]]. (needs_source: the report has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 01:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 01:10:00}
- He relates that when the wife of his uncle died the Prophet prayed over her and left his cloak in her grave, saying she had been a mother to him after his mother, and when asked why he did it he said so that God would ease the squeeze of the grave for her and keep her firm at the questioning, and he describes this as loyalty on his part. (needs_source: the report has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 01:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 01:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 01:11:30}
- He says what he has given is only a glimpse of the Prophet's standing, and that lying against the Prophet includes denying a hadith itself: if someone says the Prophet commanded something as God commanded it and another replies that he never commanded or said it, he has denied God and His Messenger; he says what God and His Messenger said must be believed, and asks those who call people to God to verify the hadiths people repeat, since nobody wants God and His Messenger to be denied. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 01:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 01:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 01:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 01:15:30}
- In answer to a question about a door during prayer, he says that if the door lies in the direction of the qibla the worshipper may take a few steps and open it without turning away, and the prayer is sound, and he suggests writing a note on the door telling visitors; the rest of his reasoning is unclear in the recording. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 01:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep26 01:17:00}

## Episode 27: the Prophet's request for a writing in his last illness and the will of Abu Bakr
- He says it pains him that the falsified history taught in school leads people to speak wrongly of Companions and followers and hides the truth of matters. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:02:30}
- He relates that the Prophet warned a man against cursing his own parents, that people asked how a man could do that, and that the answer by sense is that he reviles another man's father, who then reviles his father and mother. He adds that telling another person that he and his father are unbelievers is not the way of Islam. This is his view. (needs_source: the hadith is quoted by sense without a chain or narrator and the sound is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:04:00}
- He recounts that when some people greeted the Prophet with a distorted salutation he replied only and upon you, that Aisha answered them harshly, and that the Prophet smiled and called her by name. He draws from it a lesson in manners of reply even to those who wrong us. See [[person/aisha|Aisha]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the report has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:05:30}
- He relates a hadith that when the Prophet's illness grew heavy he said bring me a writing that I may write for you a document after which you will not go astray, that Umar said the Prophet was overcome by pain and the Book of God was enough for them, that those present disputed, and that the Prophet said rise from me, dispute is not fitting in my presence. The narrator ends that the real calamity was whatever stood between the Messenger and his writing. See [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]] and [[event/prophets-deathbed-writing-request|the Prophet's request for a writing]]. (needs_source: the narrator and collection are not clearly audible in either recording) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:08:00}
- He says the subject of the lesson is the dispute among the Companions at the Prophet's death. He says he will stop in turn with each of the four great Companions around him, [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]], [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar]], [[person/uthman-ibn-affan|Uthman]] and [[person/ali-ibn-abi-talib|Ali]], then take the last words the Prophet spoke before his soul left and the will he gave the community as a father gives his sons when he feels death near. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:10:30}
- He relates that Abu Bakr was the person most beloved to the Prophet, who said by sense that had he taken a close friend he would have taken Abu Bakr, and adds that Abu Bakr was given glad tidings of Paradise. He says clear and truthful words came from Abu Bakr. He mentions that the Prophet made Abu Bakr amir of the pilgrimage in the year before the Farewell Pilgrimage, and that an exchange took place between him and someone arriving on the Prophet's camel, asking whether he came as amir or as a messenger from the Messenger of God, and points to the courtesy of the question. See [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]] and [[event/farewell-pilgrimage|the Farewell Pilgrimage]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the hadith on the close friend and the exchange on the camel are quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:13:00}
- He relates that a letter reached Khalid ibn al-Walid telling him to lay down his arms, and that when Khalid, Abu Sulayman, died, Umar wept and said women had become barren of the like of him. He adds that, unlike states that glorified persons, in Islam there is no glorification except of God and persons only do what God commanded. See [[person/khalid-ibn-al-walid|Khalid ibn al-Walid]] and [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the report on the letter and Umar's weeping has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:15:30}
- He relates that before a battle an envoy from the Persian army asked to speak to the Muslim commander and asked whether it was true that God had sent their Prophet a sword from heaven that gave them victory. The commander answered no, that they had a Lord and a book sent down to their Messenger which they read night and day, following its commands and avoiding its prohibitions, and that this is how they are helped. (needs_source: the report has no chain and the commander is not clearly named) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:16:30}
- He says the stage, at the start of peoples entering the faith, did not call for Khalid but for leaders like [[person/abu-ubayda-ibn-al-jarrah|Abu Ubayda ibn al-Jarrah]] and [[person/al-muthanna-ibn-haritha|al-Muthanna ibn Haritha]], Companions of the Prophet. He relates that Khalid used to say battles and conquests had kept him from the Book of God, and that before a fight he had a reciter recite al-Anfal and al-Tawba before the army, then said God is greatest. This is his view. (needs_source: Khalid's saying and practice have no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:18:30}
- He relates that when Abu Bakr wrote to Khalid in Iraq to go and help his brothers in Syria, the army crossed the desert after the camels had been given abundant water so that they stored as much as they could, kept in the heat of the sun, and the soldiers then took the water stored inside the camels. See [[person/khalid-ibn-al-walid|Khalid]], [[place/iraq|Iraq]] and [[place/syria|Syria]]. (needs_source: the report has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:20:30}
- He says Muslims should learn manners of dialogue and of disagreement, and that when advising a brother one should not answer with accusations of heresy, immorality, sin or injustice, but be truthful in what one says. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:22:30}
- He relates that when the Muslim army had come under four senior Companions, a plan was proposed that the commanders hold command in turn, a day each, with the first day left to the one proposing it, and that the army was rearranged so that the vanguard went to the rear, the centre and the flanks changed places, and the banners each tribe carried were changed. (needs_source: the account of the battle has no chain and the audio is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:24:00}
- He relates that Abu Bakr wrote to Khalid in Iraq words to the effect that an army with al-Qa'qa' in it would not be defeated. He cites a saying that men are of kinds in consultation, the last being the man who consults nobody. See [[person/al-qaqa-ibn-amr|al-Qa'qa']] and [[concept/consultation-shura|consultation]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the letter and the saying on men are given without a source and the audio is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:26:30}
- He relates that a Roman commander sent forty horsemen from the enemy ranks toward the Muslims, that as soon as al-Qa'qa' arrived he began to manoeuvre against the forty, that the Muslims then gathered and entered the enemy army, and that by sunset they had won the first day. (needs_source: the account has no chain and the audio is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:28:00}
- He says that victory that day came with supplication to God, brotherhood for God's sake, the commander's mercy to those he leads, the soldiers' devotion to their commander, and the commander feeling hardship before the soldier rather than sitting in an air-conditioned office at the rear. He adds that so long as Muslims hold to the Book and the Sunna victory is with them, and when they turn from them defeat follows. See [[practice/dua|supplication]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:29:00}
- He relates that Abu Bakr dictated his will to Uthman, beginning that this was the testament of Abu Bakr ibn Abi Quhafa at his last time in the world, leaving it, and first time in the Hereafter, entering it, and that he appointed over them. Abu Bakr lost consciousness, so Uthman wrote the name of Umar ibn al-Khattab, and when Abu Bakr recovered he asked Uthman to read it back and confirmed that he appointed Umar. He relates that people objected that Umar had been harsh with them while the Messenger was among them and while Abu Bakr was with them, so how would he be after, and he remarks on this exchange as real democracy. See [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]], [[person/uthman-ibn-affan|Uthman ibn Affan]] and [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the report of the will has no chain and the audio is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:32:30}
- He relates that the Prophet said his Lord promised that seventy thousand of his community would enter Paradise without reckoning, that a Bedouin asked whether he would not ask his Lord for more, and that he answered that he had and was given seventy thousand with each one. He adds that Umar interjected and the Bedouin answered away from me, son of al-Khattab. (needs_source: the hadith is quoted by sense, its narrator is not named and the end of the story is unclear) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:33:00}
- He relates that a Bedouin pulled the Prophet by the edge of his garment and asked to be loaded on his two camels from God's money, that the Prophet said he would but retaliation was due first, and that the Bedouin said he would not do it because the Prophet does not repay evil with evil, at which the Prophet laughed. He describes the Prophet as speaking with his whole mouth, clearly, and says that had a Bedouin come and looked at his face he would have said by God this is not the face of a liar. He adds that a Companion sat with the Prophet in front of the mosque after the evening prayer on a night of the full moon and looked from the moon to the Prophet's face, and that a Companion described his face as like a page of the Qur'an. This is his view. (needs_source: the reports have no chain and the names of the Companions are not clear in the audio) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:36:00}
- He relates that Abu Bakr, told that people feared Umar, asked whether they were frightening him with his Lord, and said that if his Lord asked him he would answer that he had left over His people the best of them. He then relates his counsel to Umar that God has a right in the night which He does not accept in the day and a right in the day which He does not accept at night, that the scales are heavy for those whose scales were heavy by following the truth in the world, and light for those who followed falsehood, and that only the truth is placed in the scale. See [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]] and [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar]]. (needs_source: the will is reported without a chain and the audio is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:53:30}
- He mentions by sense the report that the Prophet said wake the women of the rooms, and said that many a woman clothed in this world is naked in the Hereafter. (needs_source: quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:39:30}
- He relates that the Prophet came to the house of [[person/fatima|Fatima]] looking for Ali, that she said they had quarrelled, and that he found Ali and said rise Abu Turab, a name that stayed with Ali. He relates the Prophet's words on hearing that Ali ibn Abi Talib wished to marry in addition to Fatima: that she is of him, that what troubles her troubles him, that he does not permit it, said three times, unless Ali divorces his daughter and marries whom he wishes, and says many people are surprised at this stand. He also relates that the Prophet praised God who made Fatima like Mary daughter of Imran, to whom Zakariyya came in the sanctuary. See [[person/ali-ibn-abi-talib|Ali ibn Abi Talib]], [[person/maryam|Mary]], [[person/zakariyya|Zakariyya]] and [[passage/quran-3-37|Al Imran, verse 37]]. (needs_source: the reports have no chain and the audio is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:52:00}
- He relates that the Prophet told Ali that if he believed in God and the last day he should not leave a raised grave without levelling it. (needs_source: the hadith is quoted without a chain and the second half is not clear in the audio) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:53:00}
- He relates, from Abu Bakr's counsel to Umar, that when God mentions the people of Paradise He mentions their best deeds and passes over their worst, so that one says he will not reach them, and that He mentions the people of the Fire with their worst deeds and sets aside their best, so that a person says he is better than they. He adds that God sent verses of mercy together with verses of punishment so that the believer is hopeful and fearful, wishes nothing on God but the truth and does not throw himself into ruin. He recalls God's description of the servants of the Merciful in the verse of al-Furqan. See [[concept/khawf|fear of God]] and [[passage/quran-25-63|al-Furqan, verse 63]]. (needs_source: the counsel is reported without a chain and the audio is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:55:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:55:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:57:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 00:59:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 01:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 01:05:00}
- He relates that the Prophet told one of the Companions the names of the hypocrites of Medina and that no one else knew them, and that the caliph asked him to swear by God whether the Prophet had named him among them. (needs_source: the report has no chain and the audio is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 01:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 01:00:30}
- He relates the close of Abu Bakr's will to Umar: that if he kept the will nothing absent would be dearer to him than death, which was coming to him, and if he wasted it nothing absent would be more hateful, and he could not escape death. Abu Bakr then turned to those present, and he relates a supplication asking God, who created creatures without need of them and made a party for bliss and a party for the Fire, to place the speaker among those of bliss. (needs_source: the will and the supplication are reported without a chain and the speaker of the supplication is not clear in the audio) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 01:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 01:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 01:08:00}
- He relates that the Prophet said that God employs a servant, and that asked how, he said He guides him to a good deed so that he dies in a good state. See [[concept/husn-al-khatima|a good ending]]. (needs_source: the hadith is quoted without a chain or narrator) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 01:10:00}
- In the question session he answers a question about ending an engagement and what is given back. He says the matter is settled either by strict justice or by grace, and recommends grace: either the suitor leaves the whole shabka to the fiancee as compensation for the harm she suffered, or her father waives it for the suitor given the circumstances, each treating the other by grace so that God treats them with grace. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 01:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 01:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 01:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 01:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 01:16:30}
- In the question session he answers a question about a friend who says she is driven to steal, has taken money several times from people close to her and cannot stop, though she has tried to return it. He advises her to read the explanation of the verse on theft and its punishment, to remind herself of God and to increase her prayer and Qur'an reading. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 01:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 01:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 01:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep27 01:19:30}

## Episode 28: the Prophet's last illness, seeking forgiveness and his passing
- He recalls the hadith that ended the previous lesson: the Prophet asked for something to be written so that they would not go astray, the Companions disagreed, and Umar said the Book of God was enough for them. He says disputing in front of the Prophet is never right, and that attributing to him what he did not say is lying about him, for which he recalls the warning given in an earlier lesson. This is his view. See [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]] and [[event/death-of-the-prophet|the death of the Prophet]]. (needs_source: the warning hadith on lying about the Prophet is recalled without narrator or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:07:30}
- He applies the principle that whatever leads to the forbidden is itself forbidden, giving the example of someone who sells grapes to a buyer known to make wine from them. He relates a saying that everyone involved in pressing, carrying and selling wine is alike in burden, and says this principle is taught in the fundamentals of jurisprudence. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying on those involved in wine is given by sense with no narrator or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:11:00}
- He relates that the Prophet divided matters into the clearly lawful, the clearly unlawful and the doubtful between them, and that whoever avoids the doubtful has cleared his religion, like a shepherd who grazes near a boundary and risks crossing it. He says he mentioned earlier that this is among the few hadiths around which the religion revolves. See [[concept/sunna|the Sunna]]. (needs_source: narrator and collection are not named in this passage) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:12:00}
- He holds that a non-Muslim in a Muslim land should respect Islam, as Muslims who live in Europe or America live by the laws of those lands. He adds that Muslims must first present Islam clearly and correctly, and that God took a pledge from scholars to make knowledge plain and not hide it. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:17:30}
- He says the link between heaven and earth ran through the Prophet: when a Companion asked a question, the answer came down from heaven. He says that with the Prophet's death this link ended, and that earlier prophets were also followed by the coming down of Gabriel only until their deaths. This is his view. See [[person/muhammad|Muhammad]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:19:00}
- He says the community was safe while the Prophet was among them, and relates that Abu Bakr wept at his death and said a security had gone from them. He points to seeking forgiveness as the security that remains, and relates that three men came to Ali about their hardships and he told them to seek forgiveness, reminding them that the Qur'an links it to rain, wealth, children and gardens. He adds that whoever seeks forgiveness much, God gives him relief from every worry. See [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]], [[person/ali-ibn-abi-talib|Ali ibn Abi Talib]], [[concept/istighfar|seeking forgiveness]] and [[passage/quran-71-10|Nuh, verse 10]]. (needs_source: the reports of Abu Bakr and of Ali, and the saying on relief from worry, have no chain or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:21:00}
- He says seeking forgiveness must be sincere: it needs leaving the sin, regret and a resolve not to return, and he holds that asking forgiveness while persisting in the sin is not true repentance. This is his view. See [[concept/tawba|repentance]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:21:30}
- He urges haste in repentance and relates that Ibn Umar said that if he knew God had accepted a single prostration from him, nothing would be dearer to him than death, since no one can be sure it is accepted.
