He also relates that a servant who sins and repents is told by God that he knows he has a Lord who forgives, and that when this repeats God says He has forgiven him and made him firm, so that he does not return to that sin. See [[concept/tawba|repentance]]. (needs_source: the saying of Ibn Umar has no chain and the narrator of the second report is unclear in the recording) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:25:00}
- He explains the word servant, saying God calls even the Prophet his servant, and points to the verses on sending down the Book and on the night journey. See [[passage/quran-18-1|al-Kahf, verse 1]] and [[passage/quran-17-1|al-Isra, verse 1]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:26:00}
- He describes the world into which the Prophet was born: some peoples worshipped fire, the Arabs worshipped idols such as al-Uzza and Manat, and tribes fought wars lasting forty years over a she-camel. He places the Prophet in the family of Banu Hashim of Quraysh, as the son of Abdullah and Amina, and says the world needed a message. See [[person/muhammad|Muhammad]]. (needs_source: the forty-year figure is given with no reference) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:31:00}
- He relates that the Prophet spoke of people who would come after the Companions, believing in him without having seen him, and said the work of one of them would count like that of fifty of the Companions. He relates that the Prophet asked his greetings be conveyed to those who did not see him and to those who come after him. (needs_source: narrator and collection are not named, and the figure of fifty has no reference) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:37:00}
- He relates the Prophet's saying that people should pray as they saw him pray and take their rites from him. He relates a saying on ablution that whoever adds to it or subtracts from it has done wrong. See [[concept/sunna|the Sunna]]. (needs_source: the ablution saying is given without narrator or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:38:30}
- He tells that Jabir ibn Abdullah owed a debt to a Jewish creditor who would not wait until the next year, and that the Prophet asked the creditor to defer the debt. He presents this among the signs of the Prophet, and says the Qur'an is the greatest and enduring sign, whose preservation God promised, unlike the sensory signs of earlier prophets. See [[person/jabir-ibn-abdullah|Jabir ibn Abdullah]]. (needs_source: the story is told without a chain and its ending is not clear in the recording) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:42:00}
- He says the Prophet's last illness began in the final month of his life, in his sixty-third year. He relates that he told Aisha that he suffered pain as two men of his community do, so that his reward would be doubled. See [[person/aisha|Aisha]] and [[event/death-of-the-prophet|the death of the Prophet]]. (needs_source: the narrator of the saying is not named and the age is given with no reference) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:42:30}
- He relates further signs of the Prophet: that Safina, the Prophet's freed servant, met a lion, told it he was the Prophet's servant, and the lion went with him and he told the Prophet; that a man asked who would testify for the Prophet, and a tree did, after which the man declared his faith; and that a camel came to the Prophet in an orchard with tears in its eyes, which he took as a complaint against its owner. See [[person/safina-mawla-al-nabi|Safina]]. (needs_source: the three stories are told without chains and parts of them are unclear in the recording) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:46:00}
- He relates that in his last days the Prophet went to al-Baqi, wept long, greeted its dead and prayed for them, and that he then prayed for the martyrs of Uhud. He relates a saying of the Prophet, with some Companions on Mount Uhud, that it bore a prophet, a truthful one and a martyr, though the wording is unclear in the recording. He relates that he sat on the minbar and asked that the Ansar be treated well, their good accepted and their faults overlooked. See [[place/al-baqi|al-Baqi]] and [[event/farewell-visit-to-uhud-martyrs|the last visit to the martyrs of Uhud]]. (needs_source: the narrators are not named and parts of the wording are unclear in the recording) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:48:30}
- He relates that the Prophet told the people from the minbar that a servant had been given a choice between lasting life in this world and meeting God, and had chosen to meet God. He relates that Abu Bakr wept and said they would give their lives, children, families and wealth for the Prophet, and that the Prophet later said he had repaid every favour done to him except that of Abu Bakr, and ordered the doors opening on the mosque closed apart from his. He says all people, believers and others, agree that death will come, yet the reality is hard to face when it arrives. See [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]]. (needs_source: the reports are told without narrator or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:51:30}
- He says that even the one most beloved to God was not spared the pains of illness. He relates that Abd al-Rahman, Aisha's brother, came with a siwak, and that Aisha chewed it to soften it and gave it to the Prophet. See [[person/aisha|Aisha]]. (needs_source: the narrator is not named and the recording is partly unclear) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:52:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:52:30}
- He relates that on a Monday the Prophet came out to the mosque supported by Ali and al-Abbas, saw the people praying behind Abu Bakr and was glad, and that Abu Bakr began to step back but the Prophet sat on his left. He notes the narrations differ on how many days the Prophet had been confined to the house. See [[person/ali-ibn-abi-talib|Ali ibn Abi Talib]], [[person/abbas-ibn-abd-al-muttalib|al-Abbas ibn Abd al-Muttalib]] and [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]]. (needs_source: the narrator is not named and the recording is partly unclear) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:53:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:54:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:54:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:55:00}
- He relates that in his farewell address the Prophet told the people to fear God and not to exalt themselves over God's servants, and cited the verse that the home of the hereafter is for those who seek neither exaltation nor corruption on earth. He draws the lesson that people should be gentle with one another. This is his view. See [[passage/quran-28-83|al-Qasas, verse 83]]. (needs_source: the wording of the address is partly unclear in the recording and no narrator is named) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:56:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:56:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:57:00}
- He relates questions put to the Prophet about his death: who would wash him, and in what shroud. The Prophet answered that his family would wash him and that he should be shrouded in his clothes or in white, and asked that he be placed on his bier at the edge of his grave. He relates that the Prophet said the angels would pray for him first, then the men of his household, then the women, then the others, and asked not to be pained by wailing or praise. See [[passage/quran-33-56|al-Ahzab, verse 56]]. (needs_source: the narrator is not named and the wording is partly unclear in the recording) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:57:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:58:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:58:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:59:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 00:59:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 01:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 01:00:30}
- He relates that the Prophet asked that his greeting be conveyed to those who follow his religion after him, and stressed prayer, saying whoever abandons it has no share in Islam. He relates that he again urged good treatment of the Ansar and told the people that if they held fast to the Book of God and his Sunna they would not go astray, and that he would meet them at the Pool. See [[concept/sunna|the Sunna]]. (needs_source: the wording is given by sense with no narrator or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 01:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 01:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 01:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 01:02:00}
- He relates that on the last day Fatima sat at the Prophet's head weeping, and that he told her she would be the first of his household to follow him. He says the message was complete, citing the verse on the perfecting of the religion, and that Gabriel used to review the Qur'an with the Prophet in Ramadan. See [[person/fatima|Fatima]] and [[passage/quran-5-3|al-Ma'ida, verse 3]]. (needs_source: the report on Fatima and the Ramadan review are given without narrator or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 01:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 01:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 01:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 01:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 01:04:30}
- He relates that the Prophet's last words included repeated urging on prayer, and that Aisha heard him say that he chose the highest companionship, and said that then he would not choose us. He relates that he raised his hands and his soul departed. He relates a Companion's words that the day he entered Medina everything brightened for them, and the day he died everything darkened. See [[person/aisha|Aisha]] and [[event/death-of-the-prophet|the death of the Prophet]]. (needs_source: the Companion who spoke is not named and none of the reports has a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 01:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 01:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 01:05:30}
- He relates that Umar denied the Prophet had died, and that Abu Bakr then spoke from the minbar, saying that whoever worshipped Muhammad should know Muhammad had died, and whoever worshipped God should know God lives and does not die, and recited the verse that Muhammad was only a messenger. He relates that Umar said it was as if he were hearing it for the first time. See [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]], [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]] and [[passage/quran-3-144|Al Imran, verse 144]]. (needs_source: the report is told without a chain and its wording is partly unclear in the recording) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 01:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 01:06:30}
- He relates that Ali and al-Abbas washed the Prophet, that Abu Bakr kissed his face and said he was good in life and in death, and that his body was then buried. He relates that Fatima asked those who buried him how they could bear to throw earth on the Prophet, and that she was told the earth was thrown as they had been commanded. See [[person/fatima|Fatima]], [[person/ali-ibn-abi-talib|Ali ibn Abi Talib]] and [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]]. (needs_source: the reports are told without chains and the recording is partly unclear) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 01:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 01:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 01:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 01:08:30}
- Answering a listener's question about a loan taken with interest for a car, he says interest is forbidden for both parties to the contract. This is his view. See [[concept/riba|interest]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 01:10:30}
- Answering a listener worried about a scholarship abroad, he tells him that whoever leaves something for God's sake, God replaces it with something better. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying is given by sense with no narrator or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep28 01:11:30}

## Episode 29: the Prophet's description and his miracles
- He says the lesson takes three hadiths and begins with hadith number 105 of Sahih al-Bukhari, which he relates from Umm Salama: the Prophet woke one night and spoke of the trials sent down that night and of the good that was opened. See [[person/umm-salama|Umm Salama]] and [[concept/al-fitan|trials]]. (needs_source: the wording of the hadith is garbled in the recording and its chapter is not given) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:02:00}
- He relates from Abu Hurayra that people say he narrates too much from the Prophet, and Abu Hurayra answers by contrasting the emigrants, busy with the markets, and the Ansar, busy with their property, with himself, who stayed with the Prophet content with his food. He calls this the hadith on keeping knowledge. See [[person/abu-hurayra|Abu Hurayra]]. (needs_source: the collection is not named and the wording is garbled in the recording) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:03:30}
- He relates from Abu Hurayra that he told the Prophet he heard many hadiths from him and forgot them; the Prophet scooped with his hands and told him to hold it to himself, and Abu Hurayra says he forgot nothing afterwards. He calls this one of the Prophet's miracles and sums up the three hadiths as one on trials, one on why Abu Hurayra narrated so much and one on forgetting knowledge. See [[person/abu-hurayra|Abu Hurayra]] and [[concept/mujiza|miracle]]. (needs_source: the details of what the Prophet did are garbled in the recording and no collection is named) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:05:00}
- He says the Prophet feared trials for his community and that we now live in a time of trials, and that trials have sources they flow from and outlets they pour into. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:06:00}
- He says the lesson of the trials is to pray much, and advises saying after each prayer a prayer to be kept safe and not tried in one's religion. This is his view. See [[practice/dua|supplication]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:07:30}
- He says each lesson turns to the Prophet's character, his generosity, courage, eloquence, forgiveness and mercy for believers, so that we feel as if he were among us through his law, his words and God's book, and that the lesson has three parts: the Prophet's person, some of his miracles, and how knowledge is kept and what keeping it means, since some people keep knowledge and do not act on it. This is his view. See [[person/muhammad|the Prophet]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:10:00}
- He says the Companions left out nothing of the Prophet's life: his ablution, movement, sitting, speech, eating, sleeping, entering and leaving, his gatherings and expeditions, all carried in the books of Sunna, biography and history so that one can almost see him, and that they reported it truthfully. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:11:00}
- He says five Companions described the Prophet in detail and that he prefers the description of Hind ibn Abi Hala, whom he calls a son of Khadija from her earlier marriage and so the Prophet's stepson, the maternal uncle of al-Hasan ibn Ali, who asked him; he adds that Hind was already a grown man and eloquent. See [[person/hind-ibn-abi-hala|Hind ibn Abi Hala]], [[person/khadija|Khadija]] and [[person/hasan-ibn-ali|al-Hasan ibn Ali]]. This is his view. (needs_source: Hind's kinship and age are given in passing without reference) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:18:30}
- He relates from Hind's description that the Prophet was magnificent and held in awe, and says whoever saw him felt awe in the heart; he tells of a man who trembled before him and the Prophet put a hand on his shoulder and told him to ease down, and says no Companion could look long at his face except Abu Bakr. See [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the report of the trembling man and of Abu Bakr alone is given without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:25:00}
- He relates a story that a stranger complained to the Quraysh leaders that Abu Jahl had taken his camel and not paid; they mockingly sent him to Muhammad, who went with him, knocked at Abu Jahl's door, and Abu Jahl came out and paid. He adds that guards from the Prophet's household watched over him until the verse promising God's protection of him from people came down, when he sent them away. See [[person/abu-jahl|Abu Jahl]] and [[passage/quran-5-67|al-Ma'ida, verse 67]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the story of the camel and the report of the guards are given without a chain and the recording is garbled in places) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:24:00}
- He relates from Hind's description that the Prophet's face shone like the moon, that he was taller than the middle-sized and shorter than the very tall, neither tall nor short to the eye, and that he was well proportioned. This is his view. (needs_source: the words of the description are given without naming the collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:27:30}
- He relates from the description that the Prophet had a large head and wavy hair that was neither curled tight nor straight, parted, and reaching near his earlobes. He adds that the Prophet forbade shaving part of the head and leaving part, which he reports in answer to a Companion's question. (needs_source: the report of the prohibition is quoted without a chain or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:30:00}
- He relates from the description that the Prophet's complexion was white with a tinge of red, his forehead wide, his eyebrows fine and arched but not joined, with a vein between them that swells in anger, and his eyes very dark with long lashes. This is his view. (needs_source: the description is given by sense without naming the collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:38:00}
- He relates that a man asked the Prophet to carry him on a camel, the Prophet said he would carry him on the offspring of a camel, the man asked how that would do, and the Prophet answered that every camel is the offspring of a camel; he says the Prophet joked but said only what was true. (needs_source: the report is quoted without a chain or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:37:00}
- He relates from the description that the Prophet's nose rose in the middle and had a light over it, so that one who did not look closely thought it high. This is his view. (needs_source: the description is given by sense without naming the collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:45:00}
- He relates that the Prophet asked his Companions about the thought that comes when the devil asks who created the heavens, the earth and the sea, and then who created God, and that whoever has this thought should seek refuge in God and affirm that God is one. See [[concept/tawhid|oneness of God]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the hadith is quoted by sense without a chain and its introduction is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:41:00}
- He says that everyone who said there is no god but God will be brought out of the Fire, calling this good news for the community of those who affirm God's oneness. This is his view. (needs_source: the hadith is quoted without a chain or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:44:30}
- He relates from the description that the Prophet's beard was thick, and that his speech was clear, his words like pearls. This is his view. (needs_source: the description is given by sense without naming the collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:49:30}
- He relates from the description that the Prophet's neck was like a finely shaped figure's, his body well formed, belly level, broad between the shoulders, with large joints. This is his view. (needs_source: the description is given by sense and the recording is garbled in places) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:51:30}
- He relates that those who shook the Prophet's hand said it felt as if they had taken their hand out of a perfumer's bag, and describes his feet as smooth and flowing with hollow soles. This is his view. (needs_source: the report of the handshake is quoted without a chain and the wording is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:55:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:55:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:56:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:57:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:58:00}
- He relates from the description that the Prophet walked leaning forward, and that his glances were sidelong; he says he looked toward the ground longer than toward the sky. This is his view. (needs_source: the description is given by sense without naming the collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 00:58:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 01:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 01:01:30}
- He says the Prophet let his Companions walk ahead of him and was first to greet whoever he met. This is his view. (needs_source: the report is given without a chain or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 01:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 01:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 01:06:00}
- He turns to the second part, the Prophet's miracles, and says they came on inanimate things, on animals and on people. See [[concept/mujiza|miracle]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the recording is garbled on the word for the first kind) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 01:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 01:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 01:07:30}
- He relates the miracle of water flowing from between the Prophet's fingers until everyone had drunk, and mentions the multiplying of food at the home of Umm Sulaym and her husband, who invited the Prophet. See [[person/umm-sulaym|Umm Sulaym]]. (needs_source: both reports are told without a chain and the recording is garbled in the second) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 01:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 01:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 01:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 01:10:00}
- He says the Companions saw and heard the pebbles glorify God in the Prophet's hand, and relates that Abd al-Rahman ibn Abi Sumra, going aside on an expedition to relieve himself, was told to go to the trees and tell them to gather so he would be hidden behind them, and afterwards to return them to their places. (needs_source: both reports are told without a chain and the wording is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 01:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 01:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 01:12:30}
- He relates that a gazelle caught in a net asked the Prophet to release her so she could nurse her two young ones and return, and that she went and came back. (needs_source: the report is told without a chain or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 01:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 01:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 01:14:00}
- He mentions the story of Umm Haram and the sea, saying the Companions applied a prophecy of the Prophet to her, and says he has much more material and will return to the miracles at another meeting. See [[person/umm-haram-bint-milhan|Umm Haram bint Milhan]]. (needs_source: the story is only alluded to and the recording is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 01:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 01:16:00}
- Answering a listener's question, he cites the principle that what leads to the forbidden is forbidden, and says God will give something better. This is his view. (needs_source: the rule is cited without a reference and the question is unclear in the recording) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep29 01:17:00}

## Episode 30: the trials, and how poverty, wealth, repentance and fear test a person
- He recalls that the previous Monday they agreed to read four hadiths on the trials, and says the Prophet did not turn away from speaking of them but was shown them, so that the subject is heavy and frightening and not a soothing one; he asks God to protect the listeners from the trials, those apparent and those hidden. See [[concept/al-fitan|the trials]]. This is his view. (needs_source: that the Prophet was shown the trials is stated without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep30 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep30 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep30 00:04:00}
- He says there are about twenty headings of trials, some apparent and some hidden, and lists poverty, wealth, children, the wife, obedience, disobedience, knowledge, ignorance and desire, together with trials of doubts and trials of appetites. See [[concept/al-fitan|the trials]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the approximate number of trials is given without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep30 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep30 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep30 00:12:00}
- He relates by sense that the Prophet spoke of a time of many trials in which a man sells his religion for a share of worldly goods. (needs_source: the hadith is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep30 00:05:30}
- He says God decreed that there be rich and poor among people, each a trial for the other: wealth becomes a trial when the rich man gives the poor nothing, and poverty may become a trial for the poor man. See [[concept/faqr|poverty]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep30 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep30 00:13:00}
- He divides people in their relation to God into three kinds: one nurtured by divine favour; one who repents, regrets the time spent away from remembrance and stands at God's door awaiting mercy; and one struck down by heedlessness and desire who still waits at that door. See [[concept/tawba|repentance]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep30 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep30 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep30 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep30 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep30 00:24:00}
- He relates by sense that Gabriel was sent to greet Abu Bakr on behalf of his Lord and to ask whether he is pleased with God as God is pleased with him, as an example of the first kind. See [[person/jibril|Gabriel]] and [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]]. (needs_source: the report of Gabriel and Abu Bakr is given without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep30 00:15:00}
- He says a servant who is impatient in poverty may take what is not his, and that had he held on in patience God would have brought him the lawful. See [[concept/faqr|poverty]] and [[concept/sabr|patience]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep30 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep30 00:26:00}
- He relates a saying in the voice of God that whoever repents to Him He loves, whoever does not repent He treats like a physician, and that He is more merciful to them than a mother to her children. See [[concept/tawba|repentance]]. (needs_source: the saying is quoted by sense without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep30 00:29:00}
- He says the closer a person draws to God the greater his fear, and relates by sense that if a caller announced that everyone enters Paradise but one, a person would fear being that one. See [[concept/khawf|fear]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the report about the caller is given with no named speaker or chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep30 00:30:00}
- He relates by sense that charity does not reduce a person's wealth, and calls giving a dealing with God. See [[concept/sadaqa|charity]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the hadith is quoted by sense without narrator or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep30 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep30 00:34:00}
- He says no one should despair of God's mercy, that every door may open and close except the door of repentance, and that God is generous, forbearing and patient. See [[concept/tawba|repentance]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying about the door of repentance is given without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep30 00:37:00}
- He recalls by sense a supplication seeking refuge from a wife, children and a neighbour, describing the neighbour as one who spreads any evil he sees and does not repeat any good. (needs_source: the supplication is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep30 00:54:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep30 00:55:00}
- He says conditions change between the blink of an eye and its opening, the sick recovering and the sad rejoicing, because hearts are in the hand of the Merciful who turns them, and he asks God to turn our hearts. This is his view. (needs_source: that hearts are in the hand of the Merciful is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep30 00:59:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep30 01:00:00}
- He cites the verse that God does not love the exultant, and explains that the joy meant is the kind that passes the limit. See [[passage/quran-28-76|al-Qasas, verse 76]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep30 01:00:30}
- He says leaving a deed for the sake of people is showing off, and that doing it for their sake is likewise faulted. See [[concept/riya|showing off]] and [[concept/ikhlas|sincerity]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying is quoted without naming its speaker) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep30 01:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep30 01:03:30}
- He closes by saying that a person's inclination must follow what the Messenger brought. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep30 01:09:30}

## Episode 31: the trials of rulers and scholars, and letters of advice to rulers
- He says, as he did in the first lesson, that Ibn Hajar spent twenty-five years on his commentary and ten more before he died, so that the work took him thirty-five years. See [[person/ibn-hajar-al-asqalani|Ibn Hajar al-Asqalani]] and [[work/fath-al-bari|Fath al-Bari]]. (needs_source: the years are given without a reference) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:01:30}
- He says that when a scholar slips, a whole world slips with him, because scholars are the heirs of the prophets, and that the greatest loss is not of wealth or camels but of a man whose death takes many with it. He adds that a scholar can die in fact or in effect, when he is kept from teaching people. This is his view. See [[concept/ilm|knowledge]]. (needs_source: the verse on the loss is quoted without naming its author) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:03:30}
- He recalls that the previous lesson covered the trials of children, this world, the wife, and knowledge and ignorance, and says trials such as that of desires are still to come. He says that when the people of standing are sound the whole society is sound and when they are corrupt it is corrupt, naming rulers and scholars, and relates that al-Ghazali addressed scholars as the salt of the land, asking what can mend salt once it has spoiled. See [[person/al-ghazali-abu-hamid|al-Ghazali]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying of al-Ghazali is quoted without naming a work) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:06:30}
- He says responsibility falls on everyone and not on rulers alone, and that he wants to look at the role of scholars beside rulers in the Umayyad and Abbasid eras, since people left by both ruler and scholar have nowhere to turn. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:07:30}
- He says advice must be sincere even when short, and offers the saying that a hired mourner is not like a mother who has lost her child, whose grief is real. He then gives five points on advising, the first being sincerity, meaning the aim must not be to lower the other person, to expose him or to show off one's own knowledge. This is his view. See [[concept/nasiha|sincere advice]]. (needs_source: the saying on the mourner has no named speaker) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:12:00}
- He urges giving the religion and the call to it all one's time, saying God would bless lives, wives, children and wealth, and says the lawful stays lawful by God's Book until the day of resurrection and the unlawful stays unlawful, so one should not make the lawful unlawful or the reverse. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:09:30}
- He tells that a ruler of Egypt, angered by a scholar's advice, had him put before a lion starved for days; the man stood still, the lion lay at his feet, and when the ruler asked what he had been thinking, he said he was wondering whether the lion's saliva is pure. (needs_source: story told without names or chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:11:00}
- He announces the advice he will read from the early generations: Umar to Abu Musa, Ali to one of his governors, al-Hasan al-Basri to Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz, and Sufyan al-Thawri to Harun. See [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]], [[person/abu-musa-al-ashari|Abu Musa al-Ash'ari]], [[person/ali-ibn-abi-talib|Ali ibn Abi Talib]], [[person/al-hasan-al-basri|al-Hasan al-Basri]], [[person/umar-ibn-abd-al-aziz|Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz]] and [[person/sufyan-al-thawri|Sufyan al-Thawri]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:15:00}
- He relates by sense a report in which a companion volunteers to be the Prophet's doorkeeper for a day, and Abu Bakr and then Umar each ask leave to enter, and the Prophet tells him to admit each and give him the glad tidings of Paradise. See [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]] and [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]]. (needs_source: told by sense, no narrator or collection named) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:18:30}
- He relates by sense that the Prophet invoked God's mercy on the Ash'aris, who, when need pressed them or their families ran short, each brought what he had, pooled it and divided it equally, and says Umar appointed Abu Musa over Yemen. See [[person/abu-musa-al-ashari|Abu Musa al-Ash'ari]]. (needs_source: quoted by sense without narrator or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:20:00}
- He reads a letter he gives to Umar for one of his governors, saying it had reached him that the governor was at ease in his food and drink and sat reclining in the governor's seat, and that if the governor strays his people stray, and the most wretched of people is one through whom his people became wretched. See [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]]. (needs_source: letter read without chain or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:24:30}
- He reads a letter he gives to Ali for one of his governors who hurried to a banquet, saying he had not expected him to accept it, and reminding him that someone may be without bread or a memory of being full while he lies with a full belly among hungry livers. See [[person/ali-ibn-abi-talib|Ali ibn Abi Talib]]. (needs_source: letter read without chain or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:26:30}
- He continues the same letter with an address to the world, telling it to keep away since he has let go of its rein, asking where those it beguiled are now, and saying they are in the graves, and ends with a blessing on the soul that fulfilled its duty to its Lord, suffered hardship, gave up sleep at night and kept its lips moving in remembrance and in asking forgiveness. See [[concept/dunya|this world]]. This is his view of the letter. (needs_source: letter read without chain or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:29:30}
- He introduces al-Hasan al-Basri's letter to Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz, saying the two were close friends, and reads that God made the just ruler a support for every crooked thing, a check on every tyrant, a strength for the weak and justice for the wronged, as a compassionate father and a gentle mother are to a child. See [[person/al-hasan-al-basri|al-Hasan al-Basri]], [[person/umar-ibn-abd-al-aziz|Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz]] and [[concept/adl|justice]]. (needs_source: letter read without chain or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:35:30}
- He reads on that the just ruler stands between God and His servants, hearing God's word and making them hear it, and that God sent down the prescribed penalties to deter foul deeds, so how can it be when those who apply them commit them. The letter bids him remember death and what follows, not to let the arrogant rule over the weak, not to be deceived by those enjoying comforts, and to look not at his power today but at his standing tomorrow before God. (needs_source: letter read without chain or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:38:00}
- He tells that a man of knowledge entered on the ruler, took off his shoes at the door and did not greet him as commander of the believers, and when asked said he removes his shoes five times a day for prayer and that not all believers agree that the ruler is their commander, so he would be a hypocrite before God. (needs_source: story told without chain, the speaker is not named in the transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:41:00}
- He reads Sufyan al-Thawri's letter to Harun, which faults him for taking from the Muslims' treasury without right, asks whether the bearers of the Qur'an, the scholars, the orphans and the widows are content with it, bids him prepare an answer for the questioning and says he will stand before the just Judge. See [[person/sufyan-al-thawri|Sufyan al-Thawri]]. (needs_source: letter read without chain or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:42:30}
- He reads on that the letter rebukes the ruler for sitting on silk and wearing it, and for posting soldiers who wrong people and drink wine, and warns that he will stand before God with his hands bound to his neck, freed only by his justice; he says Harun wept. (needs_source: letter read without chain or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:44:30}
- He says one should not be taken with people's love since it may count against him, and that after the trial of knowledge comes a third, the trial of ignorance. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:45:30}
- He cites a saying that a person lives between livelihood and the return, so that one whom livelihood distracts from the return is among the lost, while one who occupies himself with both is among the risk-takers. See [[concept/akhira|the hereafter]]. (needs_source: the saying has no named source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:52:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:52:30}
- He cites a saying in which God tells the son of Adam that He created him for Himself and created things for his sake, so he should not tire himself over them, and says that busying oneself with what is guaranteed while falling short in what is required is a sign of clouded insight, and that the world, work and wealth are means and not ends. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying has no named source or chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:50:00}
- He relates an exchange of harsh admonition that is answered by saying God sent Moses and Aaron to Pharaoh and told them to speak to him gently. See [[person/musa|Moses]]. (needs_source: exchange told without names or chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:51:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:52:00}
- He says that the first time a person sins his conscience troubles him and he is uneasy, the second time less, the third less still, until it becomes a habit, and warns against sliding that way. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:53:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:53:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:54:00}
- He relates by sense that the Prophet sent a man to collect charity who returned saying this part is for you and this was given to me as a gift, and the Prophet asked why he did not sit in his parents' house and see whether any gift would come to him. He applies it to officials who receive gifts because of their post and urges reliance on God rather than a bribe. See [[practice/zakat|zakat]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the hadith is told by sense without narrator or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:58:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:59:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 00:59:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 01:01:00}
- He mentions by sense a saying in which the Prophet, asked about a kind of person, answers that it is the one who says that if people do good he does good and if they do wrong he does wrong. (needs_source: the saying is told by sense without narrator or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 01:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 01:03:00}
- He says that at the hour of death a person may be approached in the form of a relative who died earlier, claiming to have learned the truth and urging him to die as a Jew or in another religion, and tells of a dying man who, prompted to say there is no god but God, kept saying words from the chess he had played all his life. He closes by saying that livelihood must not distract us from the return. This is his view. (needs_source: both stories are told without names or chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 01:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 01:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 01:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep31 01:10:30}

## Episode 32: whoever is asked who is most knowledgeable should refer knowledge to God
- He says the Prophet left the community on a clear white path so that it would not argue and quarrel, and he relates that the Prophet warned against striking one another's necks after him and said raised voices in mosques are among the signs of the Hour. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying about not returning to disbelief and the sign of the Hour are given without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep32 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep32 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep32 00:02:30}
- He says the rows in prayer should be filled in order, the first row, then the one behind it, and that whoever comes after takes the row behind. This is his view. See [[practice/salah|prayer]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep32 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep32 00:03:30}
- He takes up hadith 122 in the chapter on what is due from the scholar when he is asked who among the people is most knowledgeable: he should refer the knowledge to God. He says the lesson is that a scholar does not claim to be the one who knows most. See [[concept/ilm|knowledge]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep32 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep32 00:29:30}
- He says that whoever thinks he has become learned has begun to fall into ignorance, and that going astray follows. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep32 00:08:00}
- He says people cannot know who is a hypocrite and no one may accuse another of hypocrisy, though hypocrisy has signs of which only some are known. This is his view. See [[concept/nifaq|hypocrisy]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep32 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep32 00:10:00}
- He relates that three groups will be in the lowest depth of the fire on the Day of Resurrection: the hypocrites, the people of Pharaoh and those who asked for the table and then disbelieved. See [[person/firawn|Pharaoh]] and [[concept/nifaq|hypocrisy]]. (needs_source: three groups in the lowest depth of the fire is stated without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep32 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep32 00:21:30}
- He says that when Pharaoh grew tyrannical and claimed to be the highest lord, God intervened and drowned him and those with him. This is his view. See [[person/firawn|Pharaoh]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep32 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep32 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep32 00:16:00}
- He relates that the Prophet said the grave is either a garden of Paradise or a pit of the fire, and that he spoke to the dead at the well and said he had found true what his Lord promised, and when the companions asked whether he spoke to the dead, he answered that they hear his words better than they do. He cites this as proof of the punishment of the grave. (needs_source: the saying about the grave and the address to the dead are given without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep32 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep32 00:15:00}
- He tells of the people of the table: they asked for a table from heaven and were told to eat and not to store up, and he says it came down with every kind of food, and that God warned whoever disbelieved after it of a punishment no one else in the worlds would receive. See [[passage/quran-5-115|al-Ma'ida, verse 115]]. (needs_source: the account of the table and its daily food is given without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep32 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep32 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep32 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep32 00:21:30}
- He says that laziness about the afternoon prayer is among the traits of the hypocrites. This is his view. See [[practice/salah|prayer]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep32 00:24:30}
- He relates that on the Path each believer's light will match his faith, one like a flickering lamp, another like the full moon, and that the speed of crossing follows the light. See [[concept/sirat|the Path]]. (needs_source: the report on the believers' light on the Path is given without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep32 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep32 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep32 00:27:30}
- He says Umar ibn al-Khattab will be the first to take his record in his right hand. See [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]]. (needs_source: the report that Umar is first to take his book in the right hand is given without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep32 00:28:30}
- He warns against innovation and says the religion is lost through it, and that there is no such thing as a good innovation. He gives the night prayer of Ramadan: the Prophet prayed it in congregation for some nights then stopped, and Umar later gathered people behind one imam, which he refuses to call an innovation, and he adds that whoever begins a good practice has its reward. This is his view. See [[concept/bida|innovation]], [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]] and [[practice/qiyam-al-layl|the night prayer]]. (needs_source: the saying about whoever begins a good practice is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep32 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep32 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep32 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep32 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep32 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep32 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep32 00:37:00}
- He relates the hadith that Musa stood to speak among the Children of Israel and was asked who was most knowledgeable, and said he was, so God rebuked him for not referring the knowledge to Him and told him of a servant at the meeting of the two seas who knew more, and the Prophet names this servant as al-Khidr. See [[person/musa|Musa]], [[person/al-khidr|al-Khidr]]. (needs_source: the narrator and collection of the hadith of Musa and al-Khidr are not clearly stated) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep32 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep32 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep32 00:40:00}
- He tells how Musa greeted al-Khidr, who answered that he had knowledge from God that Musa did not have, as Musa had knowledge al-Khidr did not have, and a bird dipped its beak in the sea and al-Khidr said their knowledge compared with God's was only what that beak took from the sea. See [[person/musa|Musa]] and [[person/al-khidr|al-Khidr]]. (needs_source: the exchange of greetings and the bird are given without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep32 01:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep32 01:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep32 01:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep32 01:05:00}
- He goes through the journey: al-Khidr bored a hole in the boat, then a boy was killed, then a wall was set upright, and he reports that the Prophet said God have mercy on Musa, had he been patient more would have been told. He closes by saying the stories of the Qur'an are for reminder and lesson, not for entertainment. This is his view. See [[passage/quran-18-71|al-Kahf, verse 71]], [[passage/quran-18-74|al-Kahf, verse 74]] and [[passage/quran-18-77|al-Kahf, verse 77]]. (needs_source: the Prophet's saying about Musa is reported without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep32 01:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep32 01:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep32 01:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep32 01:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep32 01:09:00}

## Episode 33: whoever fights so that the word of God is highest is in the way of God
- He relates from Abu Musa al-Ashari that a man came to the Prophet and asked what fighting in the way of God is, since one of us fights in anger and another fights from zeal, and that the Prophet raised his head to him and answered that whoever fights so that the word of God is highest is in the way of God. He gives it as a hadith of Sahih al-Bukhari, read with Ibn Hajar's Fath al-Bari, under al-Bukhari's chapter on one who asked a seated scholar while standing, and says the Prophet raised his head only because the man was standing, telling the scene as a video camera would. He adds that neither scholar nor learner stands for the other, since people stand only for the Lord of the worlds on the day of resurrection. See [[person/abu-musa-al-ashari|Abu Musa al-Ashari]], [[person/al-bukhari|al-Bukhari]], [[work/sahih-al-bukhari|Sahih al-Bukhari]], [[person/ibn-hajar-al-asqalani|Ibn Hajar]] and [[work/fath-al-bari|Fath al-Bari]]. This is his view on standing. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:04:30}
- Turning to fasting, he mentions by sense that the fast of David, a day on and a day off, is the most beloved to God, and that in Sha'ban the Prophet fasted so much that people said he would not break, and then broke until they said he would not fast. He says there are fixed rules: one already in the habit of a regular fast, such as three days at the start, middle and end of the month or alternate days, may continue, while one who is not in the habit should not suddenly take one on, and no text singles out a particular day of Rajab for fasting. He adds that he does not wish to stop anyone from a worship or an extra prayer. See [[person/dawud|David]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the reports on the fast of David and of Sha'ban are quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:06:00}
- He says that deeds have a daily, a weekly and an annual presentation to God, comparing them to a company's balance sheets that record profit and loss each day, and the daily total to a shopkeeper who sums up the till while each item is recorded in detail. He mentions that deeds are presented on Monday and Thursday, and says it is from the Prophet's sunna to keep to this confirmed practice of fasting on those days so that the book of deeds stands well when presented. See [[concept/sunna|the Sunna]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the report that deeds are presented on Monday and Thursday is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:15:00}
- He says the angels of the day close the record of the day's deeds somewhere between dawn and the afternoon, giving the afternoon prayer as the view of some scholars, and then hand over to the angels of the night, and that the angels carry the deeds up: the good word rises to God and the righteous deed lifts it. See [[passage/quran-35-10|Fatir, verse 10]] and [[concept/malaika|angels]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:09:30}
- He says the majority of exegetes hold that the angels of the night and the angels of the day both attend the dawn prayer, alluding to the verse on the witnessed dawn recitation, so that the day's angels take over from the night's. He mentions by sense that whoever stays in his place of prayer after the dawn prayer until sunrise and prays two rak'ahs, keeping to this every day, has the like of a hajj and an umra with the Messenger of God. See [[passage/quran-17-78|al-Isra, verse 78]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the report of the hajj and umra is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:10:30}
- He relates by sense that a caller calls at the start of the night for the worshippers to rise, at midnight for the devout, in the last third for those who seek forgiveness before dawn, and at daybreak for the heedless, who rise like the dead from their graves. He then says that one who opens with the evening prayer and closes with the dawn prayer is opened with light and closed with light, and that God, being generous, will accept what lies between. See [[practice/qiyam-al-layl|the night prayer]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the report of the callers is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:13:00}
- He turns to the annual presentation and to Ramadan, which he says God made the month of the community of Muhammad, and mentions by sense that on the first day of Ramadan God looks upon the Muslims, and whoever God looks upon He will not punish on the day of resurrection. He says the hope is to be counted among those who gain God's good pleasure. See [[concept/ramadan-month|Ramadan]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the report of God's look on the first day of Ramadan is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:16:00}
- Returning to the hadith, he explains that the man's question names two motives, fighting from anger, as for a cousin or a neighbour, and fighting from zeal, and that the Prophet's answer sets one measure, that the word of God be highest. He says not everyone who dies in the street is in the way of God, and lists other motives, among them love. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:17:30}
- He says jihad has kinds and degrees, by the self, by wealth, by knowledge and the word, and by conduct, and that the Prophet is the one from whom the law is taken, through his sunna and the consensus of his Companions. See [[concept/jihad|jihad]] and [[concept/sunna|the Sunna]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:18:30}
- He says the Companions went out with the Prophet on twenty-eight campaigns in seven years, an average of about four a year, and that every few months the Prophet would set out, meet the enemy, lose martyrs and cause losses, and return to Medina, all while teaching the community and passing on revelation. He takes this as showing that jihad is a cornerstone of Islam. See [[place/medina|Medina]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the figure of twenty-eight campaigns in seven years is given without a reference) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:20:30}
- He calls jihad the cornerstone of Islam, naming two situations, one of them when the call of tawhid is prevented from reaching people, and offers as evidence that some lands entered Islam without war. See [[concept/jihad|jihad]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the lands that entered Islam without war are not named) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:21:00}
- He recalls that when the Prophet was sent among Quraysh he had almost no one with him, and says that whoever is with the truth, even if the whole world stood against him, could not be made to retreat a single step. See [[place/mecca|Mecca]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:24:00}
- He says all the prophets worked, and mentions by sense that the best food a person eats is what he eats from his own labour and that the prophet of God David ate from the work of his hands. See [[person/dawud|David]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying on the best food is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:26:00}
- He mentions by sense that the believer is one who befriends and is befriended, with no good in one who does neither, and that the believers are like a building whose parts strengthen one another. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:30:00}
- He says scholars bear a responsibility before God to make clear truth from falsehood, truth from lie and the lawful from the unlawful, and mentions by sense a saying of the Prophet that when some of his Companions are reviled the scholar must show his knowledge or bear the curse of God, the angels and all people. He gives as an example a television speaker who speaks about Abdullah ibn al-Zubayr. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:39:30}
- He says Muslims believe in the descent of Jesus, but not as the Christians do: he will come as a follower of the Prophet Muhammad, and if the Muslim imam asks him to lead the prayer he will decline, saying he comes as a follower. See [[person/isa-ibn-maryam|Jesus]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the report of the imam and Jesus is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:44:00}
- He announces that he will treat jihad in two stages, first in the time of the Prophet and then after the age of the Companions, and says the first war between the Muslims and the opponents of Islam was the day of Badr. The basic lesson he draws is that the Muslims did not count numbers or equipment but relied on God, Lord of the worlds. See [[event/battle-of-badr|the Battle of Badr]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:46:00}
- He says it is not right to say that we did our part and the rest is on God; rather the whole matter is in God's hand from first to last and a person is only a cause. See [[concept/tawakkul|reliance on God]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:46:30}
- He tells that a sick man asked a preacher whether Joseph inclined toward the woman, and the preacher answered that God's care was present at that moment, which he offers as a picture of God's guarding. See [[person/yusuf|Joseph]] and [[passage/quran-12-24|Yusuf, verse 24]]. (needs_source: the story of the sick man and the preacher has no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:47:30}
- He says God sends guardians over a person, and mentions by sense that if the son of Adam were left to himself for a moment the devils would snatch him away, and that one who sleeps without remembrance of God is open to Satan. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:49:00}
- He begins the story of Badr with a caravan of some twenty or thirty men and says the party that went out from Medina had no war and no equipment in mind, and that when Quraysh learned that the caravan had escaped, Abu Jahl urged them to go on and settle the matter with Muhammad and those with him. See [[event/battle-of-badr|the Battle of Badr]], [[person/abu-jahl|Abu Jahl]] and [[place/medina|Medina]]. (needs_source: the account is told without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:52:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:53:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:53:30}
- He relates that a son of Abu Jahl fled at the conquest of Mecca, that when the Prophet was asked whether he would give him safety if he returned he said yes, and that the man came and entered Islam. He says the Prophet told his Companions he would come as a Muslim, so they should not revile his father, since that hurts the living and does not reach the dead, and draws the lesson of guarding the tongue. See [[person/abu-jahl|Abu Jahl]]. (needs_source: the report is told without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:55:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:56:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:56:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:57:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:57:30}
- He relates that Ayyash ibn Abi Rabia emigrated with Umar ibn al-Khattab, and that Abu Jahl, whom he calls Ayyash's half-brother on his mother's side, went with a companion to Medina and told him that his mother had refused to comb her hair or take shelter from the sun until he returned to Mecca. Umar warned him it was a trial, Ayyash said he had money in Mecca to fetch, and Umar offered to share his own money, but he went; on the road they bound him on a camel and entered Mecca with him in bonds, and Abu Jahl called out to the people to treat their fools as he had. See [[person/ayyash-ibn-abi-rabia|Ayyash ibn Abi Rabia]], [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]], [[person/abu-jahl|Abu Jahl]] and [[place/mecca|Mecca]]. (needs_source: the account is told without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:58:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:59:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:59:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 01:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 01:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 01:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 01:01:30}
- He relates that after months the Prophet longed for Ayyash and for Hisham ibn al-As, who had been due to emigrate with them, and asked who could bring them; al-Walid ibn al-Walid ibn al-Mughira said he would.
