He went to Mecca by night, found them held in a narrow room and brought them to Medina, where Ayyash came before the Prophet. He links to them the verse telling those who wronged themselves not to despair of God's mercy, and pauses on the heat of Mecca and on Abraham's prayer that God provide its people with fruits. See [[person/al-walid-ibn-al-walid|al-Walid ibn al-Walid]], [[passage/quran-39-53|al-Zumar, verse 53]], [[passage/quran-14-37|Ibrahim, verse 37]] and [[person/ibrahim|Abraham]]. (needs_source: the account is told without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 00:58:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 01:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 01:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 01:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 01:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 01:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 01:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 01:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 01:09:30}
- He relates that near the wells two men were questioned about how many camels the Quraysh army slaughtered each day, nine or ten, from which the Muslims reckoned it at between nine hundred and a thousand. He says the Prophet then consulted his Companions: Abu Bakr spoke well, Umar spoke well, and al-Miqdad said they would not say as the Children of Israel said to Moses, who were told to go with their Lord and fight while they sat, but would fight with him; one of the Ansar said that had he led them to Bark al-Ghimad, at the far end of Arabia, they would follow, and that if he plunged into the sea they would follow. He says a commander's heart is reassured when those around him speak so. See [[event/battle-of-badr|the Battle of Badr]], [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]], [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar]], [[person/al-miqdad-ibn-amr|al-Miqdad]], [[person/musa|Moses]] and [[passage/quran-5-24|al-Ma'ida, verse 24]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the account is told without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 01:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 01:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 01:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 01:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 01:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 01:13:00}
- He relates that the Prophet raised his hands in supplication, and explains that the word used for the band means a group. He tells that the Prophet declared that anyone he had struck could take retaliation, that a Companion named Sawad claimed it and ended by kissing the seal of prophethood, saying he had heard that whoever sees it will not see the Fire and hoping for martyrdom, and wishing it to be the last thing he saw. See [[practice/dua-at-battle|supplication in battle]]. (needs_source: the account of Sawad is told without a chain, and the saying on the seal is quoted by sense) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 01:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 01:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 01:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 01:16:00}
- He tells that Jibril came with thousands of angels, that God makes firm those who believe, that victory went to the small believing band and defeat to the large aggressive host, and calls this the first victory of the Prophet and the first episode of his series on jihad. See [[person/jibril|Jibril]], [[passage/quran-8-12|al-Anfal, verse 12]] and [[event/battle-of-badr|the Battle of Badr]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the report of the angels is told without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 01:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep33 01:17:00}

## Episode 34: whoever fights so that the word of God prevails
- He reads a hadith reported by al-Bukhari in which a man tells the Prophet that one of them fights out of zeal and another fights out of anger and asks which of them is in the way of God; the Prophet answers that whoever fights so that the word of God prevails is in the way of God. He says this lesson is the second part of the talk on jihad that he began the previous Monday. See [[concept/jihad|jihad]] and [[work/sahih-al-bukhari|Sahih al-Bukhari]]. (needs_source: the narrator and chapter of the hadith are not named in the recording) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:04:00}
- He says the later part of this community will be set right only by what set right its first part, namely the Book of God and the Sunna of the Prophet, and that the Prophet left no good unexplained and no harm unwarned against, leaving the community on a clear white path from which only one who is lost strays. See [[concept/sunna|the Sunna]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying about the first and last of the community and the report of the white path are given without attribution or chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:06:30}
- He says God's promise to those who believe and do righteous deeds is that He will make them successors on earth as He did with those before them and will turn their fear into security; he says the way is to believe in one God, in Muhammad as His Prophet and in the Qur'an, and then to do good deeds. See [[passage/quran-24-55|al-Nur, verse 55]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:09:00}
- He tells of Hind, the wife of Abu Sufyan before he entered Islam, saying that she held Hamza responsible for the deaths of her father, brother and uncle, that she promised a slave his freedom if he killed Hamza, and that after Hamza fell she mutilated his body. See [[person/hind-bint-utba|Hind bint Utba]] and [[person/hamza|Hamza]]. (needs_source: the exchange with the slave and the details of the mutilation are narrated without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:12:30}
- He says that in one battle fourteen Companions were killed while seventy of the idolaters were killed and seventy taken captive, and that in another battle seventy Muslims were killed; he says God consoled the Muslims by reminding them that they had inflicted a loss like this on their enemies earlier, and adds that for an Arab, having a man of the tribe taken captive was harder than having him killed. See [[passage/quran-3-165|Al Imran, verse 165]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the battles are not named in the recording and the casualty figures are given without a reference) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:17:30}
- He mentions by meaning a report of two people, one who had the most comfortable life in this world and one who had the hardest; after a single moment in the Fire for the first and in Paradise for the second, each, asked whether he ever saw anything of his former state, swears by God's might that he did not: the first says he has known only misery and the second only bliss. He adds the example of a man who, after thirty years of comfort, loses everything in one year and forgets the comfort. See [[concept/jannah|Paradise]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the narrator and collection of the report are not named) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:34:00}
- He explains a verse saying that God admits the believers to Paradise and makes it known to them: he says a person on his way to Paradise knows his place there better than he knows his home in the world, and that the root of the word used also carries the sense of a sweet scent, so that he takes the verse to include the fragrance of Paradise. See [[passage/quran-47-6|Muhammad, verse 6]] and [[concept/jannah|Paradise]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the report that a person knows his place in Paradise better than his home is given without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:21:30}
- He says the people of Paradise meet no grief or fear there and hear only words of peace, and that this peace comes from God. See [[passage/quran-56-26|al-Waqi'a, verse 26]] and [[passage/quran-36-58|Ya Sin, verse 58]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:22:00}
- He mentions by meaning a report that when a believer loses a young child and answers with the words of return to God, God has a house built for him in Paradise and names it the House of Praise. See [[concept/jannah|Paradise]]. (needs_source: the report is given by sense, the recording is unclear at this point and no chain is given) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:24:30}
- He says the bliss of Paradise is that its people do not grieve over what has passed or fear what is to come, and live in security, and that angels come to them from every gate. See [[passage/quran-13-23|al-Ra'd, verse 23]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:28:30}
- He says that whoever gives up the songs of this world is compensated by God, and he gives by meaning a song of the women of Paradise, that they are the contented who will never be displeased, the settled who will never depart and the immortal who will never die. This is his view. (needs_source: the song is quoted without a chain and its wording in the recording is unclear) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:31:00}
- He says that God Himself greets the people of Paradise with peace, tells them that He is pleased with them and will not be angry with them afterwards. This is his view. (needs_source: the report is narrated by sense without a chain and the recording is unclear) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:33:00}
- He gives examples of the Companions' protectiveness toward the Prophet in battle: he says one of the Companions bent over the Prophet's body to shield him, and that a woman among the Companions, whose name is unclear in the recording, stood in the way with her hand when an idolater came to strike him. (needs_source: the accounts are given without a chain and the names of the man and the woman are unclear in the recording) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:36:30}
- He reads a hadith in which the Prophet says that one of you may do the deeds of the people of Paradise until only a short distance remains between him and it, then what was written overtakes him, he does the deeds of the people of the Fire and enters it, and that another may do the deeds of the people of the Fire until only a short distance remains, then what was written overtakes him, he does the deeds of the people of Paradise and enters it. See [[concept/qadar|divine decree]] and [[concept/jannah|Paradise]]. (needs_source: the narrator and collection are not named in the recording) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:40:00}
- Right after the hadith he tells of a man who fought beside the Prophet and killed some nine of the idolaters, fighting out of zeal; when the pain of his wound became unbearable he took his sword, and he first claimed to be fighting in the way of God but then said he had fought to protect his people and their honour. The recording is unclear on the order of events. (needs_source: the story is told without a chain, the man is not named and the recording is unclear in places) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:41:30}
- He says intention makes the same act count differently, and gives the example of two people eating the same food in the same place at the same time, where one eats to gain strength for obeying God and to feel His favour and is rewarded, and the other is not. See [[concept/niyya|intention]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:43:30}
- He tells of a Muslim woman who went to the market of the Jewish tribe of Banu Qaynuqa; he notes that the reports differ on the details, but says that a man there tied her garment so that she was exposed when she stood, that she cried out for Muhammad, and that a Muslim who heard her intervened. He says the tribe was then made to leave Medina, and remarks that the Prophet did not wait for any international assembly or council, contrasting this with the present age. See [[event/ghazwat-banu-qaynuqa|the expedition against Banu Qaynuqa]] and [[place/medina|Medina]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the incident is narrated by sense and he notes that the narrations differ) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:47:00}
- He says the same kind of incident was repeated in the days of the caliph al-Mu'tasim, when a Muslim woman was wronged in a Roman market, and tells that the caliph prepared an army he puts at 120,000, took Amorium and had the woman brought back. See [[person/al-mutasim|al-Mu'tasim]], [[event/conquest-of-amorium|the conquest of Amorium]] and [[place/amorium|Amorium]]. (needs_source: the story and the size of the army are given without a chain and the recording is unclear in places) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:53:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:54:00}
- He describes the reaction in both accounts as protective honour (ghayra), which he calls Arab, and says it stirs a whole community. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:49:00}
- He urges care in speaking of the Companions, and says that some accounts in school books, such as that Khalid ibn al-Walid killed Malik ibn Nuwayra and married his wife on the battlefield, come from writers of Muslim history whom he describes as non-Muslims or Muslims in name only, naming Jurji Zaydan and Salama Musa, and that he finds the story unreasonable. See [[person/khalid-ibn-al-walid|Khalid ibn al-Walid]], [[person/malik-ibn-nuwayra|Malik ibn Nuwayra]] and [[person/jurji-zaydan|Jurji Zaydan]]. This is his view. (needs_source: no source is given for the claim about who wrote school-book history or for the saying about the Companions) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:56:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:56:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:57:00}
- He tells of a man who had been among the fiercest enemies of Islam and went to Medina after Badr intending to kill the Prophet, after another man promised to look after his children; Umar met him there, the Prophet told him what the two had agreed at the Kaaba, and he declared the testimony of faith. See [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]]. (needs_source: the story is told without a chain and the man's name is unclear in the recording) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:57:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:58:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 00:58:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 01:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 01:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 01:01:30}
- He retells the contest between Moses and Pharaoh's magicians: the meeting was set for a festival day when the people would gather, Moses stood with Aaron, the magicians offered him to cast first or to let them cast first, and he explains that their magic works through suggestion and imagination on the minds of onlookers, while God told Moses not to fear since he would prevail, and that the magician does not succeed wherever he comes. See [[person/musa|Musa]], [[person/firawn|Pharaoh]], [[concept/sihr|sorcery]], [[passage/quran-20-59|Ta Ha, verse 59]], [[passage/quran-20-65|verse 65]], [[passage/quran-20-68|verse 68]] and [[passage/quran-20-69|verse 69]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 01:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 01:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 01:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 01:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 01:07:30}
- He recounts that a man from Quraysh came to the Prophet offering wealth or kingship over them if he would give up his call, and that the Prophet answered by reciting the opening of the chapter that begins with the letters Ha and Mim; he also relates what al-Walid ibn al-Mughira, whom he presents as a leading man of Quraysh, said of the Qur'an: that it is neither the speech of a soothsayer nor of a poet, that it has sweetness and grace, that its upper part bears fruit and its lower part is abundant, that it rises and is not surpassed, and that it is not human speech. See [[person/al-walid-ibn-al-mughira|al-Walid ibn al-Mughira]]. (needs_source: both reports are told without a chain and the visitor is not named in the recording) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 01:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 01:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 01:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 01:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 01:12:00}
- He illustrates the eloquence of the Arabs with a story of a poem that one man could memorise on first hearing, another on hearing it a second time and a servant on the third. (needs_source: the story is given without a chain and the recording is garbled around its details) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 01:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 01:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 01:13:00}
- He says Pharaoh reproached the magicians for believing without his permission, mentions a figure that seems to be sixty thousand without it being clear that it refers to the magicians, and ties their turning to a single prostration; he cites a saying he attributes to Umar that if he knew God had accepted even one prostration from him, nothing would be dearer to him than death. See [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]] and [[passage/quran-20-71|Ta Ha, verse 71]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the figure mentioned and the saying of Umar are given without a reference and the recording is unclear) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 01:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 01:20:30}
- He closes by pointing to the jihad of the Prophet and of the Companions around him, saying that nothing concerned them but the pleasure of God, and that God helps those who help His cause. See [[passage/quran-22-40|al-Hajj, verse 40]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep34 01:21:00}

## Episode 35: the question about the spirit and the stations of the soul
- He reads the hadith he numbers 120 from al-Bukhari, under a chapter on God's saying that only a little knowledge has been given, and relates from a narrator he names Abdullah that he was with the Prophet among the ruins of Medina when some Jews passed and said to one another to ask him about the spirit, that the Prophet stood until revelation came to him, and that he then answered that the spirit is of the matter of his Lord and that only a little knowledge is given. He adds that the Companions could tell the moment revelation came upon the Prophet. See [[passage/quran-17-85|al-Isra, verse 85]], [[concept/wahy|revelation]] and [[concept/al-nafs-wal-ruh|the soul and the spirit]]. (needs_source: the narrator's full name is unclear in the recording and no reference to the place of narration is given) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:39:00}
- He says a little work according to the Sunna is better than much work in innovation. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying is quoted without a narrator or reference) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:08:30}
- He recalls the Battle of Uhud, saying the order was that the archers should not leave their place and that some left it for the spoils, after which Khalid ibn al-Walid surrounded the Muslims and the Prophet was wounded in the face so that drops of his blood fell to the ground, and he mentions by sense that the Prophet asked what a people will say to their Lord when their Prophet's face has been wounded. See [[event/battle-of-uhud|the Battle of Uhud]] and [[person/khalid-ibn-al-walid|Khalid ibn al-Walid]]. (needs_source: the saying about the wounded face is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:11:30}
- He relates that when Khalid ibn al-Walid asked for reinforcements, Abu Bakr wrote to him that he was sending al-Qa'qa', and says that by God's favour al-Qa'qa' opened a way for the Muslim army in enemy land. See [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]], [[person/khalid-ibn-al-walid|Khalid ibn al-Walid]]. (needs_source: the report has no chain and part of the recording is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:15:30}
- He relates that after many memorisers of the Quran died in the wars of apostasy Umar urged Abu Bakr to have the Quran collected, and takes it as an answer to those who speak of an obligatory innovation, stating the principle that what an obligation cannot be fulfilled without is itself obligatory. He says the text was written on leather, bones and camel shoulder blades and that the copy was kept in the house of Hafsa, the mother of the believers. See [[event/compilation-of-quran|the compilation of the Quran]], [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]], [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]], [[person/hafsa|Hafsa bint Umar]] and [[concept/bida|innovation]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the report of the collection is given without a chain or reference) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:19:30}
- He says the early copy had no vowel marks because the Arabs judged the language by ear, and that mistakes appeared once non-Arabs entered, and he tells of a Bedouin who heard a reader end the verse on the thief with names of mercy and objected that mercy did not fit the cutting of the hand. This is his view. (needs_source: the story of the Bedouin has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:22:00}
- He says Abu al-Aswad al-Du'ali heard a verse on the polytheists read in a way that changed its meaning and so laid down the rules of Arabic grammar, and relates that Ali said how fine this way is, which is why the science was called nahw. See [[person/abu-al-aswad-al-duali|Abu al-Aswad al-Du'ali]], [[person/ali-ibn-abi-talib|Ali ibn Abi Talib]], [[concept/ibtida-al-nahw|the beginning of grammar]] and [[passage/quran-9-3|al-Tawba, verse 3]]. (needs_source: the report has no chain and part of the recording is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:24:30}
- He says the Quran was sent down in seven languages or seven readings, so a reader who differs from what one knows is not necessarily wrong, that one should ask first and advise in private, and that those who lead prayer should use readings the people know so that nobody is led to doubt, stressing that these differences belong to the science of readings and are no change in the Quran. See [[concept/qiraat|the readings]] and [[concept/al-ahruf-al-sabaa|the seven modes]]. This is his view. (needs_source: his statement that the Quran came in seven languages or readings is given without reference) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:26:30}
- He takes up the same question over taraweeh and relates that the Prophet prayed the night prayer in Ramadan for some nights and then stopped, fearing it would be made obligatory, and that the practice returned when that reason ended. See [[practice/taraweeh|taraweeh]]. (needs_source: the report is given without a chain and the recording is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:30:30}
- He cites a saying he attributes to Ibn Umar and the statement that every innovation is misguidance, and holds that since the religion was perfected nothing is to be added to it. See [[person/abdullah-ibn-umar|Abdullah ibn Umar]] and [[concept/bida|innovation]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying attributed to Ibn Umar has no chain and the recording is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:32:00}
- He relates that some Jews brought a case of adultery among them to the Prophet, that when he said the penalty was stoning they said it was flogging, that he answered that stoning is in their own Torah, and that they brought the Torah and one of them read from it. (needs_source: the story is given without a chain and the recording is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:37:00}
- He says a person is made of two parts, an earthly part tied to the ground and the love of life, and a part from the spirit of God that brings gentleness, humility, knowledge, repentance, uprightness and certainty, and that God had the angels prostrate to Adam only after breathing into him from His spirit. See [[concept/al-nafs-wal-ruh|the soul and the spirit]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:41:30}
- He says the Quran speaks of the self leaving the body at death, citing the angels telling the wrongdoers to bring out their selves, the call to the self at peace to return to its Lord, and God taking the selves at death and in sleep. See [[passage/quran-6-93|al-An'am, verse 93]], [[passage/quran-89-27|al-Fajr, verse 27]] and [[passage/quran-39-42|al-Zumar, verse 42]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:42:30}
- He says the spirit is a subtle divine matter that nobody fully grasps, and that its presence is known only by its signs. See [[concept/al-nafs-wal-ruh|the soul and the spirit]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:43:00}
- He says what a sleeper sees is of three kinds, a true vision, an ordinary dream and one that comes from the day's worries, and mentions by sense that a true vision is from the Merciful and a dream from Satan. See [[concept/taabir-al-ruya|dream interpretation]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the hadith is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:45:00}
- He says the Companions saw while awake what we see in sleep, and that the stronger a person's certainty in God and uprightness, the clearer his vision becomes. This is his view. (needs_source: his statement about the Companions has no reference and the recording is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:47:00}
- He says the spirits settled in the world of spirits before creation, mentions by sense that spirits are conscripted soldiers, those that knew one another agree and those that did not differ, and gives the example of a believer who enters a gathering of a hundred hypocrites and sits beside the one believer without knowing him. He adds that God took the covenant from the children of Adam and that every spirit testified to His oneness. See [[passage/quran-7-172|al-A'raf, verse 172]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the hadith is quoted by sense and the example has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:50:00}
- He mentions by sense that a true dream is one part of forty-six parts of prophecy, and relates that Umar saw three pecks in a dream and was then stabbed three times by a non-Arab man. See [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]]. (needs_source: the hadith is quoted by sense and the report of Umar's dream has no chain; the recording is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:55:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 00:56:00}
- He says the soul has three stations, the soul that commands evil with five signs, the self-reproaching soul with three and the soul at peace with seven, and names as signs of the commanding soul that it does not love people and always suspects them, and does not love for them what it loves for itself. See [[concept/maratib-al-nafs|the stations of the soul]] and [[concept/nafs-lawwama|the self-reproaching soul]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the number of signs is given without a reference) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 01:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 01:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 01:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 01:15:30}
- He relates by sense a divine saying in which God is the King of kings and the hearts of kings are in His hand, turning them to mercy when His servants obey Him and turning them against the people when they disobey, and tells his listeners not to busy themselves with cursing rulers. This is his view. (needs_source: the divine saying is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 01:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 01:14:30}
- He lists as further signs of the commanding soul slackness in obedience and a lack of certainty in what is with God, which shows as constant worry about tomorrow, saying what is in a person's life will come to him, and says the signs of the self-reproaching soul and of the soul at peace will follow in the next lesson. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 01:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 01:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 01:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 01:20:00}
- He advises the wronged not to pray against the one who wronged them, saying the prayer of the wronged is answered but that a person may himself be a wrongdoer to others, and that those wronged in this life will have their rights on the Day of Judgment. This is his view. (needs_source: the claim that the prayer of the wronged is answered is quoted without a chain and the recording is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 01:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep35 01:19:30}

## Episode 36: the three selves and the signs of the commanding, blaming and tranquil self
- He recalls that last time he spoke of the three selves the Qur'an mentions, the commanding self, the blaming self and the tranquil self, and says the commanding self has five signs, the blaming self three and the tranquil self seven, and that the last lesson ended on the five signs of the commanding self. See [[concept/nafs-lawwama|the blaming self]], [[passage/quran-12-53|Yusuf, verse 53]], [[passage/quran-75-2|al-Qiyama, verse 2]] and [[passage/quran-89-27|al-Fajr, verse 27]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:04:30}
- He recalls that the first sign of the commanding self is not loving people, while the tranquil self loves people and wishes them good, and relates that the Prophet asked whether he should tell them of the people of Paradise and named among them an imam, a ruler, whom he describes as just and granted success. He widens the word to anyone in authority: a head of state, a chairman, a director-general, a minister, a manager, and a father in his own home. This is his view. (needs_source: the hadith is quoted by sense without narrator or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:06:30}
- He says being just in dealings means not using different measures for different people, giving the example of how one may judge differently when a daughter complains to him and when a son complains to him, and calls this a lapse in the measure of dealings. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:07:00}
- He says every son of Adam errs and none is protected from error except the Messenger of God. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying is given by sense without narrator or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:11:30}
- He says cheating the measure is not only in the scale and the balance but also in dealings between people, and advises weighing every deed against the Book and the Sunna. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:13:30}
- He recalls that the first sign of the commanding self is not loving people and the second is not wishing them good, to the point of wishing that what is in their hands pass away, and names three great sins, of which he illustrates pride and greed. See [[concept/kibr|pride]] and [[concept/hirs|greed]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:14:30}
- He illustrates pride with Iblis: when God breathed of His spirit into Adam and told the angels to prostrate, all did except Iblis, who refused out of arrogance, and when asked what prevented him he answered that he was better, made of fire. See [[person/iblis|Iblis]], [[person/adam|Adam]], [[passage/quran-38-75|Sad, verse 75]] and [[passage/quran-38-76|Sad, verse 76]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:15:30}
- He illustrates greed with Adam: God told him to eat freely of Paradise except one tree, and Satan persuaded them that the prohibition was only so that they might become angels or immortal. He says Adam was eager for life, and that people are eager for life, positions and chairs. See [[person/adam|Adam]], [[passage/quran-7-20|al-A'raf, verse 20]] and [[concept/hirs|greed]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:22:00}
- He tells of a king who built a palace of the finest construction and promised the viziership to whoever could find one defect in it, and uses the story to say that the world does not last, citing the saying that if it had lasted for those before you it would not have reached you, and if it lasted for you it would not pass to those after you. This is his view. (needs_source: the parable and the saying are given without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:24:00}
- He says the Prophet taught how to love people, mentions by sense his counsel not to betray even one who betrayed you, and asks who the pious are, answering that they are those who spend in ease and in hardship. See [[passage/quran-3-134|Al Imran, verse 134]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the counsel is given by sense without narrator or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:26:30}
- He says anyone who explains the Qur'an needs study of the Arabic language, of the sciences of revelation, of exegesis and of the prophetic hadiths that explain the Qur'anic text. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:32:00}
- He says one must keep good manners with the people of knowledge. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:49:00}
- He says the Companions who gave legal opinions fall into three groups, those who gave many, a middle group and those who gave few, and that seven were among the many. (needs_source: the grouping and the number seven are given without reference) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:38:00}
- He recalls that the commanding self does not love people, does not wish them good, and does not love for them what it loves for itself nor dislike for them what it dislikes for itself. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:41:00}
- He asks where people's rights end up and answers that they are settled on the Day of Resurrection, and mentions by sense that even the hornless sheep will be given its due against the horned one. (needs_source: the hadith is quoted by sense without narrator or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:42:30}
- He turns to the blaming self, saying its signs are three and the first is self-accounting, and that it is of two kinds, one blamed and one not blamed. See [[concept/nafs-lawwama|the blaming self]] and [[concept/muhasabat-al-nafs|self-accounting]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:46:00}
- He tells of a man, not named in the audio, who said that his enemies could do him no harm: if they exiled him it would be travel for him, and if they killed him it would be martyrdom. (needs_source: the story and the saying are given without a name or source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:50:30}
- He says another sign of the blaming self that is not blamed is shortening hope, since death may come to a man young or old and no one knows his age, and that it rejoices in obedience and keeps away from disobedience. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:51:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:52:30}
- He tells that Ibn al-Qayyim was asked what scholars say of a man overcome by a sin to which he returns whenever he tries to repent, and that his book al-Jawab al-Kafi was the answer for one who asks about the healing remedy. See [[person/ibn-qayyim-al-jawziyya|Ibn al-Qayyim]] and [[work/al-jawab-al-kafi|al-Jawab al-Kafi]]. (needs_source: the account of the question and the book is given without reference) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:52:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:53:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:53:30}
- He speaks of how much a person can accomplish in a given span of life, citing al-Nawawi's many writings. See [[person/al-nawawi|al-Nawawi]]. This is his view. (needs_source: his remark on al-Nawawi's output is given without reference) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:54:00}
- He turns to the tranquil self, whose first sign is repentance and renewing it every day, and says that even one who resolves in the morning not to wrong anyone should still seek forgiveness, and that a sin is to be judged by the greatness of the One disobeyed and not by its smallness. See [[concept/tawba|repentance]] and [[passage/quran-89-27|al-Fajr, verse 27]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying on the smallness of a sin has no named speaker) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:55:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:55:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:56:00}
- He says the second sign is patience, in four kinds, of which he names patience in obedience, patience against disobedience and patience in trial. He holds that patience against disobedience is easier than patience in obedience, which only the patient are granted, and speaks of patience with God. See [[concept/sabr|patience]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:56:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:57:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:57:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:58:00}
- He gives the next sign of the tranquil self as enjoining the right and forbidding the wrong. See [[concept/amr-bil-maruf|enjoining the right]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:58:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 00:59:30}
- He speaks of entrusting one's affairs, one's family and one's wives to God, who does not lose what is entrusted to Him, and mentions by sense the counsel that what befell you would never have missed you and that if all people gathered to benefit you they could benefit you only by what God has written for you. See [[concept/tawakkul|reliance on God]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the counsel is given by sense without narrator or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 01:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 01:02:30}
- He lists next renunciation of the world, which he places in the hand and not in the heart, so that a man with millions may be a renouncer, quoting a supplication for the world in our hands and not in our hearts, and then fear of God together with hope in His mercy. See [[concept/zuhd|renunciation]], [[concept/khawf|fear]] and [[concept/raja|hope]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the supplication is given by sense without narrator or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 01:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 01:05:00}
- He cites a saying that the true stranger is not the stranger of the lands but the stranger of the grave and the shroud, and recites verses in which a dead man tells of his washing, his shrouding, the prayer over him without bowing, his lowering into the grave and the questioning with no mother, father or brother to help, ending with a warning to look at those who gathered the whole world and left it with only a shroud. (needs_source: the saying and the verses are given without a named author) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 01:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 01:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 01:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 01:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 01:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 01:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 01:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep36 01:09:00}

## Episode 37: the merits of the Companions and the best generations
- He resumes his explanation of the hadiths of Sahih al-Bukhari, saying the order of the book is not being followed strictly, and says this session takes up the section on the merits (manaqib) of the Companions; he explains that a manqiba is a good quality, a good character or a praised trait. See [[person/al-bukhari|al-Bukhari]] and [[work/sahih-al-bukhari|Sahih al-Bukhari]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:14:30}
- He argues that no revelation came to the Companions, so, he says, they were people whose faith could be strong or limited, and that God places people at degrees of nearness to Him according to their worship. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:09:30}
- He recalls that Abu Lahab, an uncle of the Prophet, and his wife are condemned in the Qur'an (he cites the surah by meaning, saying his wealth did not avail him), as a sign that kinship to the Prophet did not save a person. See [[person/abu-lahab|Abu Lahab]] and [[passage/quran-111-1|al-Masad, verse 1]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:10:30}
- He relates that the Prophet addressed members of his family, among them his daughter Fatima, telling each to act because he could not avail them anything before God, and that she could ask of his wealth whatever she wished. See [[person/fatima|Fatima]]. (needs_source: the hadith is given by sense, without narrator or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:12:00}
- He says that lineage counts through God-fearing piety, mentioning by meaning the Qur'anic account of Noah's son, who was told he was not of his family because of his conduct; he adds a warning, attributed to people of knowledge, against relying on the company of the righteous without doing good. See [[concept/taqwa|taqwa]], [[concept/nasab|lineage]], [[person/nuh|Noah]] and [[passage/quran-11-46|Hud, verse 46]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:12:30}
- He gives his definition of a Companion as a person who was with the Prophet, even for a moment. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:13:00}
- He relates that Umar ibn al-Khattab gathered six men, among them Ali and Abd al-Rahman ibn Awf, and said of them that the Prophet died pleased with them; he draws the point that nearness to God depends on piety, fear of God and walking His path, not on closeness to a person. See [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]], [[person/ali-ibn-abi-talib|Ali ibn Abi Talib]] and [[person/abd-al-rahman-ibn-awf|Abd al-Rahman ibn Awf]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the report on Umar and the six is given without chain, and most of the six names are garbled in both recordings) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:14:00}
- He tells a story of a Companion who took part in a battle: afterwards the Prophet looked for him among the wounded and the martyrs, then wept, and later smiled and turned his face away, and gave as a reason that the man had asked to be married and had not married. He draws the lesson that what a person wished for in this world and did not receive, God may make up for. This is his view. (needs_source: story given without chain; the man's name is garbled in both recordings) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:21:00}
- He relates that the Prophet spoke of people of his community whom no one knows, through whom God resolves problems of the Muslims, and describes a soul in the crop of a green bird circling the branches of the trees of Paradise. (needs_source: the reports are given by sense, without narrator or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:22:30}
- He says, citing the Qur'an by meaning, that the earth belongs to God, who gives it to whom He wills, and that the earlier scripture of David states that the righteous servants inherit it; he asks whether it is inherited by people of tawhid or of shirk and answers that it is for the people of tawhid, and says that in the Hereafter believers will praise God for having made them inherit. See [[passage/quran-7-128|al-A'raf, verse 128]], [[passage/quran-21-105|al-Anbiya, verse 105]] and [[passage/quran-39-74|al-Zumar, verse 74]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:27:30}
- He says that when a person prays for another, the angel says amen and wishes the same for the one praying, and advises praying well for others. See [[practice/dua|supplication]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying about the angel is quoted by sense, without chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:29:00}
- He relates a saying in which God calls Himself the king of kings, whose hand holds the hearts of rulers and who turns their hearts toward their subjects with mercy, and which tells people not to busy themselves with cursing rulers but with calling on Him; he advises praying for rulers rather than against them. See [[practice/dua|supplication]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying is quoted without chain or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:31:30}
- He turns to the first hadith of the section, whose chain names Jabir ibn Abdullah (the recordings garble the rest of the chain, which may include another Companion), in which the Prophet says a time will come when groups of people will set out and it will be asked whether there is among them a Companion of the Messenger of God; they will say yes and victory will be opened for them; then the same is asked of the generation after, and of the one after that. See [[person/jabir-ibn-abdullah|Jabir ibn Abdullah]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:35:30}
- He adds, by sense, the saying that the best generations are the Prophet's own, then those who follow them, then those who follow those, and that after them come people who bear witness without being asked; he lays out the generations of Companions, Followers (tabi'un) and the followers of the Followers. (needs_source: the saying is quoted by sense, without chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:36:30}
- He says most of the Companions were not buried in Mecca or Medina but spread through the lands to convey the words of God, and gives figures for their number. This is his view. (needs_source: the figures are given without reference and are garbled in both recordings) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:37:30}
- He tells the story of Sa'id ibn al-Musayyab's daughter: a ruler asked for her hand for his son, who was to become commander of the faithful, and Sa'id refused; instead she was married to Abdullah ibn Abi Wada'a, and the household had only oil, a little salt and dry barley bread, when a knock came at the door. See [[person/said-ibn-al-musayyab|Sa'id ibn al-Musayyab]]. (needs_source: story given without chain; names and details garbled in both recordings) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:51:30}
- He proposes that zakat funds be spent to help young Muslims who cannot afford it to marry, among the first uses of zakat. See [[practice/zakat|zakat]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:54:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:55:00}
- He turns to the generation of the followers of the Followers, noting that Abu Hanifa is counted by some as a Follower and by others as one of the followers of the Followers, since he is said not to have met a Companion; he adds that Imam al-Shafi'i came after, and that Imam Ahmad studied under al-Shafi'i, and recounts that al-Shafi'i as a young man memorised the Qur'an, went to learn among the Bedouin and then in Medina, and said he went on learning. See [[person/abu-hanifa|Abu Hanifa]], [[person/al-shafii|al-Shafi'i]] and [[person/ahmad-ibn-hanbal|Ahmad ibn Hanbal]]. (needs_source: the details of al-Shafi'i's early path are given without source and are garbled in both recordings) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:57:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:58:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:58:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 00:59:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 01:02:00}
- He explains the saying that people will come whose testimony precedes their oath and whose oath precedes their testimony, linking it to the weight of testimony and to warnings about false oaths. See [[concept/shahada|testimony]] and [[concept/yamin-fiqh|oaths]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying is quoted by sense, without chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 01:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 01:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 01:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 01:16:30}
- He tells the story of Khuzayma, whose testimony the Prophet counted as that of two: when a dispute arose between the Prophet and a man, Khuzayma bore witness for the Prophet and, asked how he could testify to what he had not seen, answered that he believed him in news from heaven; from this he draws the rule that one testifies only to what one has seen or heard. See [[person/khuzayma-ibn-thabit|Khuzayma ibn Thabit]]. This is his view. (needs_source: story given without chain; details garbled in both recordings) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 01:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 01:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 01:15:30}
- He relates that the Prophet likened his community to rain, of which one cannot say whether the good is in its beginning or its end, and that when the Companions asked whether any would be better than they, he said yes, those who come after him, believe in him and have not seen him, with glad tidings for those who saw him and believed and for those who did not see him and believed; he adds that the Prophet will know his community on the Day of Judgement by the marks of ablution. See [[concept/wudu-fiqh|ablution]]. (needs_source: the sayings are quoted by sense, without narrator or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 01:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 01:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 01:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 01:18:00}
- He says all the Companions are good yet differ in rank, mentioning the ten, and that after the Prophet the people followed Abu Bakr and Umar. See [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]] and [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 01:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 01:20:00}
- He relates that the Prophet said everyone had done him a favour that he had repaid, except Abu Bakr, whose favour only God could repay, and tells of a Companion who declined to put himself ahead of the one about whom the Prophet had said such a thing. See [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]]. (needs_source: the saying and the exchange are given without chain, and the names in the exchange are garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 01:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 01:21:30}
- He recalls, citing the Qur'an by meaning, that Abu Bakr is described as the second of two in the cave who is told not to grieve because God is with them, and relates that Gabriel came to the Prophet and told him to go to the door and give a man the good news of Paradise. See [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]] and [[passage/quran-9-40|al-Tawba, verse 40]]. (needs_source: the report of Gabriel's message is given without chain, and the man's name is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 01:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep37 01:24:30}

## Episode 38: the virtues of the Companions, the Hijra story of Abu Bakr and the Prophet's conduct with people
- He opens the thirty-eighth lesson by taking up the virtues of the Companions in Sahih al-Bukhari, and notes that al-Bukhari titled the section with the plural, virtues, rather than the virtue of the Companions, because each Companion has his own. See [[work/sahih-al-bukhari|Sahih al-Bukhari]] and [[person/al-bukhari|al-Bukhari]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:04:00}
- He says the Companions wrote the Qur'an down on palm stalks, skins, scraps, bones and stones, so that it would stand as God's proof until the end of the world, and that the Qur'an sent down in parts over twenty-three years is the same Qur'an we hold, with not one letter changed because God took charge of its preservation. He holds that whoever believes otherwise has left the faith. This is his view. (needs_source: the figure of twenty-three years is stated without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:05:00}
- He says the Sunna is preserved just as the Qur'an is, since the Qur'an is the text and the Sunna its explanation, and he answers those who say they take only the Qur'an: the Qur'an commands prayer but does not say how many units each prayer has or which are recited aloud, and it is the Prophet's words that explain this. He likens the Qur'an and the Sunna to a law and its explanatory memorandum, and says the Qur'an commands zakat while the Prophet's practice settles the due on farmland according to how it is watered and on trade goods. He adds that nothing of the Sunna should be denied, for to reject any of it would be to reject something from God and His Messenger. See [[concept/sunna|the Sunna]] and [[practice/zakat|zakat]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:08:30}
- He says the Companions' virtues are many and the hadiths al-Bukhari gives on them are many too, and that the Companions sat with the Messenger and learned a practical religion, not only words. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:09:00}
- He recites by sense the sacred hadith in which God says that His servant keeps drawing near to Him through voluntary acts, after the obligations such as paying zakat on time and performing the pilgrimage, until God loves him, and that then God is for him a hearing, a sight, a hand and a foot. He stresses that God is unlike anything He created. See [[concept/hadith-qudsi|sacred hadith]] and [[concept/nawafil|voluntary acts]]. (needs_source: the hadith is read without naming its narrator or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:10:30}
- He tells that a man came to Abu Hanifa and mentioned something he could neither see, hear, touch nor smell, and that Abu Hanifa asked him whether he had a mind and whether he had seen, heard or touched it, which he had not. He goes on that in everything there is a sign pointing to the One God, as dung points to the camel and footprints to the traveller, so the earth with its paths and the seas with their waves point to the Subtle, the All-Aware. See [[person/abu-hanifa|Abu Hanifa]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the exchange with Abu Hanifa is told without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:12:00}
- He says that whatever Umar ibn al-Khattab said, heaven confirmed, and that the Qur'an came down in agreement with Umar's view, as in the verse that forbids praying over any of them who dies or standing at his grave. He adds that when the verse on the stages of human creation reached the point of another creation, Umar exclaimed in admiration and praised God with the words that close the verse, and the Prophet told him it was revealed just so. He explains that Umar reached this because he drew near to God through voluntary acts, so that he heard what pleases God. See [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]], [[passage/quran-9-84|al-Tawba, verse 84]] and [[passage/quran-23-14|al-Mu'minun, verse 14]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the exchange between Umar and the Prophet is told without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:13:30}
- He explains the hadith to mean that the one whom God loves hears only what pleases God and does not hear what displeases Him. He advises parents that filling a child with faith works better than only telling him that songs are forbidden, which he says can make the child recoil, since faith will itself keep him from listening to anything but the Qur'an and what pleases God. He adds that God bars the door of disobedience to such a servant even if he walks toward it and opens the doors of obedience though he did not walk toward them. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:15:00}
- He speaks of a higher stage in the servant's religion: that a gift from a creature is a deprivation and withholding from God is a kindness. He recites a saying to this effect, spend on whom you wish and you will be his commander, do without whom you wish and you will be his equal, and ask of whom you wish and you will be his captive. He explains that asking a person costs one part of one's freedom, and that God's withholding, for instance of money, can itself be a kindness. He adds that the greatest blessing after faith in God is reason. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying is recited without naming its author) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:20:30}
- He says al-Bukhari gave the Emigrants a section of their own, and he cites the verse on the poor Emigrants who were driven from their homes and property while seeking God's bounty and pleasure. He points out that what they lost was withheld from them for a greater gift, God's pleasure, which he calls the greatest thing God grants a servant, greater than the delights of Paradise. See [[group/muhajirun|the Muhajirun]], [[passage/quran-59-8|al-Hashr, verse 8]] and [[concept/jannah|Paradise]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:24:00}
- He describes the people of Paradise as fed, clothed, drinking and free of fear of tomorrow, and relates by sense a report in which God addresses them, saying that God's pleasure is greater than all of this. See [[concept/jannah|Paradise]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the report of God addressing the people of Paradise is given by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:25:00}
- He turns to a hadith of Abu Bakr narrated by al-Bara ibn Azib: Abu Bakr bought a saddle from al-Bara's father Azib for thirteen dirhams and asked al-Bara to carry it to his house, but Azib would not let it go until Abu Bakr told what he and the Messenger did when they left Mecca with the polytheists searching for them. Abu Bakr says they travelled through the night and the next day until the noon sun stood at its height, when he looked for shelter, found a large rock with a strip of shade, smoothed a place for the Prophet and spread a bed for him, and asked him to lie down, and he did. See [[person/al-bara-ibn-azib|al-Bara' ibn Azib]], [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]] and [[event/hijra|the Hijra]]. (needs_source: the hadith is told without naming its collection, and the figure of thirteen dirhams is stated without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:30:00}
- Abu Bakr goes on that, while the Prophet rested, he looked around to see whether any pursuer was near and found a shepherd driving his sheep toward the rock for its shade. He asked whose servant he was, and the boy named a man of Quraysh whom Abu Bakr knew. Abu Bakr asked whether his sheep had milk and whether he would milk for them, and he said yes; he told him to tie up a ewe, to wipe the dust from its udder and to wipe his hands, and the boy milked a small amount. The lecturer remarks on the honesty of the Companions in reporting such detail. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:32:00}
- Abu Bakr says he had a container for the Prophet with a cloth over its mouth, poured water over the milk until its bottom cooled, took it to the Prophet and waited until he woke, and said drink, Messenger of God, and the Prophet drank until Abu Bakr was pleased. From this the lecturer recalls the Prophet's instruction to cover vessels even with a twig laid across, and says prevention is better than cure. This is his view. (needs_source: the instruction to cover vessels is recalled by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:36:00}
- Abu Bakr says he then told the Prophet it was time to leave, and they set out while the people were searching for them, and none caught up with them except Suraqa ibn Malik on his horse. Abu Bakr said the pursuer had caught up with them, and the Prophet reassured him that God was with them. The lecturer says this completes the account that Azib demanded as the price of the saddle. See [[person/suraqa-ibn-malik|Suraqa ibn Malik]] and [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:40:00}
- He recalls that when Abu Bakr was asked who the man with him was, he answered a man who shows me the way, and says that people took this to refer to the desert road. He then comments on the chain: al-Bara narrates from his father about Abu Bakr, who accompanied the Prophet on the Hijra, and he asks whether such people would lie about the Messenger. See [[person/al-bara-ibn-azib|al-Bara' ibn Azib]] and [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:40:00}
- He relates a hadith that people will be called to the gates of Paradise according to their deeds, the people of fasting from one gate, the people of prayer from another, and the people of knowledge and the fighters from others, and that Abu Bakr asked whether anyone is called from all the gates, to which the Prophet answered yes, and said Abu Bakr is one of them. See [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]] and [[concept/jannah|Paradise]]. (needs_source: the narrator and collection of the hadith are not named) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:40:30}
- He relates, from a son of Ali who says he asked his father Ali, a description of how the Prophet entered his house and went out. Ali answers that he entered for his own affairs, with permission to do so, and that he divided his time at home into three parts, one for God, one for his family and one for himself, and then divided his own part between himself and the people, keeping nothing back from the common or the select. Ali adds that he kept his tongue except for what concerned him, joined people's hearts rather than driving them away, and honoured the noble of each people and put him over them. See [[person/ali-ibn-abi-talib|Ali ibn Abi Talib]]. (needs_source: the report is read without naming its collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:50:30}
- He relates a report that the Prophet told people hurrying ahead of him in prayer, whom the lecturer calls some of the hypocrites, not to precede him in bowing or prostration, adding that God shows him what is behind him as what is before him. He also relates that Abu Hurayra asked the Prophet whether he should ask permission to enter on his mother, and that the Prophet asked whether he would like to see her uncovered and, when he said no, told him to ask permission; and that the Prophet on returning from a journey would first stop at the mosque and send word to his household that he had arrived. He presents these as lessons in manners. See [[person/abu-hurayra|Abu Hurayra]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the three reports are given without chains) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:44:30}
- He says the Prophet confirmed the leader of a people in the pre-Islamic era as their leader in Islam, and that when he sent a party he asked each man how much of the Qur'an he knew and made the one who knew the most, even a young man, their commander. He adds that the Prophet used to ask after his companions and ask people about the state of the people, praising what was good and strengthening it and disliking what was bad and weakening it. This is his view.
