(needs_source: the report of the young commander is given without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:51:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:53:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:53:30}
- He relates that the Prophet neither sat nor rose except with remembrance of God, did not reserve places and forbade reserving them, and the lecturer explains that in a mosque no one has a fixed place and the priority goes to whoever enters first. He adds that when the Prophet arrived at a gathering he sat where it ended, and that a stranger entering among his companions could not tell him apart at first glance and would ask which one was Muhammad. See [[practice/dhikr|remembrance of God]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:54:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:54:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:55:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:55:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:56:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:56:30}
- He relates that the Prophet gave each of those sitting with him his share of attention, so that none thought another more honoured, and that his gathering was one of forbearance, patience and trust. No sanctity was violated in it, its slips were not spread, and no one let out an obscene, coarse or foul word. He says the people most punished in hell are those who pour out foul speech. See [[concept/hilm|forbearance]], [[concept/sabr|patience]] and [[concept/amana|trust]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying about the most punished in hell is given by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 00:59:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 01:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 01:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 01:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 01:02:00}
- He relates that in the Prophet's gathering the old were honoured, the young shown mercy, the needy preferred and the stranger protected. He says the Prophet kept himself from three things, arguing, excess and what did not concern him, and that he did not overdo eating, speaking or sleeping. He relates a hadith by sense that a house is built in Paradise for whoever gives up arguing, the highest for one who gives it up while in the right. This is his view. (needs_source: the hadith of the house in Paradise is given by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 01:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 01:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 01:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 01:06:30}
- He relates that the Prophet did not blame anyone nor reproach him, and gives an incident in which those present said of a man that he stole and the Prophet told them to say that he took. He says the Prophet laughed at what his companions laughed at and marvelled at what they marvelled at, bore with a stranger's roughness of speech until the man calmed down, and did not cut off anyone's speech until he had finished. This is his view. (needs_source: the incident of say he took is given without a chain or detail) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 01:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 01:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 01:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 01:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 01:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 01:14:30}
- He relates that the Prophet's silence rested on four things, forbearance, caution, estimation and reflection, and explains caution as guarding against a wrong word, estimation as putting a word in its place and reflection as thinking about God's creation, adding that nothing angered him. He quotes the saying that the forbearing person is almost a prophet. See [[concept/hilm|forbearance]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the report of the Prophet's silence with its count of four, and the saying about the forbearing, are given without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 01:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 01:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 01:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 01:17:00}
- He ends by saying that the next lesson will take up the four greatest Companions, beginning with Abu Bakr, and reads a description of him that portrays a reciter of the Qur'an who forbade indecency and wrongdoing, feared God and stood in prayer at night. He holds that whoever speaks ill of Abu Bakr will be struck by God's curse on the Day of Judgement, since, he says, God curses those who curse the Prophet's Companions. See [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the author of the description of Abu Bakr cannot be recovered from the recording) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 01:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 01:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 01:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep38 01:21:00}

## Episode 39: a new chapter on the Prophet's battles, the world and the afterlife, and the trials of the Prophet
- He opens a new chapter of Sahih al-Bukhari, which he calls serious and important, and says the Book of God and the Sunna of the Prophet should be the constitution of the community, the way and the model for all of us. See [[work/sahih-al-bukhari|Sahih al-Bukhari]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:01:30}
- He says every report, movement, stillness, step, word, command or silence that reached us from the Prophet is a lesson for us, whether he moved forward, to the right or to the left, and that we should benefit from each. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:02:30}
- He says children are taught the history of Islam through the disputes of the early community, naming Mu'awiya, and asks whether they are taught the Prophet's conduct, his dealing with his Companions and his patience with those who harmed him. He says this hadith is an introduction to the chapter on the Prophet's battles and that he will not take up any single battle today. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:04:00}
- He says that when jihad is reduced to striving for a living, food for the children and a place at work, that is also striving, but he asks whether it is what we were sent for, and whether the Lord created people merely to look for food, which he calls the way of animals. See [[concept/jihad|jihad]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:05:00}
- He relates a counsel of Ali to al-Hasan that the world is a carcass and that whoever loves it is like the dogs, each taking his share, and says the Companions and the Prophet saw the world in that way. See [[person/ali-ibn-abi-talib|Ali ibn Abi Talib]], [[person/hasan-ibn-ali|al-Hasan ibn Ali]] and [[concept/dunya|the world]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the counsel of Ali is quoted without a chain and its wording is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:07:00}
- He relates by sense a report that the world appeared to the Prophet as an old woman covered in adornment who called out to him by name, that he did not turn to her, and that he was told, with Jibril named, that she was the world shown in her reality. See [[person/jibril|Jibril]] and [[concept/dunya|the world]]. (needs_source: the report is quoted without a chain or collection and part of the wording is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:08:00}
- He says the sign of the hypocrite is whether he likes or dislikes hearing the afterlife mentioned, and that when the afterlife is mentioned the heart of the believer softens and melts with longing. See [[concept/nifaq|hypocrisy]] and [[concept/akhira|the afterlife]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:11:30}
- He cites a saying that remembering death brings breadth and ease, whether in little or in hardship, and relates that the Companions used to say there is no true living but the living of the afterlife. See [[concept/akhira|the afterlife]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying on remembering death and the words of the Companions are quoted without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:13:00}
- He says that a person who goes out to earn lawful provision, to spare himself from asking, to feed his wife and children lawfully and to give from the surplus in zakat or charity, is as if in prayer until he returns from his work, so that God widens the world and the afterlife for him. See [[practice/zakat|zakat]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:15:00}
- He urges reconciliation with a brother who is upset, saying the Lord and the Messenger commanded keeping kin ties, and relates a letter from Muhammad ibn al-Hanafiyya to his brother al-Hasan ibn Ali: their father is the same Ali, al-Hasan's mother is Fatima, daughter of the Messenger, while he himself is of the Banu Hanifa line, so he asks al-Hasan to accept him, adding that whoever is first to take the step will be first to enter Paradise. See [[person/muhammad-ibn-al-hanafiyya|Muhammad ibn al-Hanafiyya]], [[person/hasan-ibn-ali|al-Hasan ibn Ali]] and [[person/ali-ibn-abi-talib|Ali ibn Abi Talib]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the letter is quoted without a chain and its closing words are garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:18:00}
- He relates that Umar set stipends from the public treasury by precedence in Islam, and that when he gave Ali's two sons the rate of the Badr veterans, his son Abdullah asked about it; Umar answered that the two were dearer to the Prophet's heart, that their father Ali was better than Abdullah's father and their mother better than his mother. See [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]], [[person/abdullah-ibn-umar|Abdullah ibn Umar]] and [[person/ali-ibn-abi-talib|Ali ibn Abi Talib]]. (needs_source: the report is quoted without a chain and the amounts are garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:21:00}
- He says he will make an introduction continuing last Friday's talk, about the difficulties the Prophet faced, and notes that every person meets many trials in the street, at home, with children, at work and in livelihood, each according to his measure, and that the illness of a child is a trial for those around the child too. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:26:00}
- He tells of a man whose leg was cut off, who said that if God took something He had left much, and if He had afflicted him He had also spared him, and that praise is God's, and says a person can say this to himself to hold firm in a trial. This is his view. (needs_source: the report is quoted without a chain and the man's name is not recoverable in either transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:24:30}
- He tells of a man who had lost his sight and used to say he saw the world bright around him night and day, that it was God's favour that all his days were daytime because his heart held the oneness of God, and then cites by sense a saying of God about taking a servant's two dear things, the reward being Paradise. (needs_source: the report is quoted without a chain, the man's name is not recoverable and the saying is quoted by sense) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:28:30}
- He says the intellect is what sets human beings apart from other creatures and that some scholars hold that the trust offered to the heavens, the earth and the mountains is the intellect; he asks whether it is the heart or the mind that thinks, answers that it is the mind, and cites the Qur'an on the hearts in the chests. See [[concept/aql|the intellect]], [[concept/amana|the trust]], [[passage/quran-33-72|al-Ahzab, verse 72]] and [[passage/quran-22-46|al-Hajj, verse 46]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:30:30}
- He says he turns to glimpses of the Prophet's jihad so that all of us draw strength from his history, since he is the example, and says everyone's words may be taken or rejected except the infallible Prophet's, citing the verse that a believing man or woman has no choice once God and His Messenger have decided a matter. See [[passage/quran-33-36|al-Ahzab, verse 36]] and [[concept/jihad|jihad]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:33:30}
- He says the pledge of al-Aqaba took place at the Great Jamra of al-Aqaba in Mina, and speaks of the two pledges there as great events in which men and women took part. See [[event/pledges-of-aqaba|the pledges of al-Aqaba]] and [[place/mina|Mina]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:46:30}
- He relates that a slave girl told a man that Amina had given birth to a boy whom his grandfather named Muhammad, that the man said if she was truthful she was free and freed her, and that it is said his punishment is lightened every Monday for freeing her out of joy at the Messenger's birth. See [[person/amina-bint-wahb|Amina bint Wahb]]. (needs_source: the account is quoted with it is said and without a chain, and the man's name is not recoverable in either transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:35:30}
- He relates by sense a report that Abu Talib will be placed in a shallow part of the Fire wearing two sandals of fire that make his brain boil, that he will be the least punished of the people of Hell, and that he thinks himself the most punished. See [[person/abu-talib|Abu Talib]]. (needs_source: the report is quoted by sense without a narrator or collection and the Qwen pass renders the name differently) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:37:30}
- He says the Quraysh agreed to speak with one voice against the Prophet, calling him a sorcerer, a soothsayer and mad, notes that the Qur'an records their calling him mad, and recalls the exchange between Moses and Pharaoh, in which Pharaoh calls Moses bewitched and Moses answers that Pharaoh is doomed. See [[passage/quran-15-6|al-Hijr, verse 6]], [[person/musa|Moses]] and [[person/firawn|Pharaoh]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:40:30}
- He relates that as a person reaches an advanced age the angels are told to stop writing all his sins, and at a later age to write only his good deeds; the two transcripts give different ages. (needs_source: the report is quoted without a chain and the ages differ between the two transcripts) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:44:00}
- He relates that the Quraysh agreed on an economic and social boycott of Muhammad and his family, under which no one was to buy from them, sell to them or give them food. See [[event/boycott-of-banu-hashim|the boycott of Banu Hashim]]. (needs_source: the account is quoted without a chain and the wording of the agreement is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:50:00}
- He says a person who is tried should remember that he is not better in God's sight than the Messenger of God, and refers back to last Friday's talk. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:59:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 00:59:30}
- He says that before Islam and before the verses of Hajj came down the Arabs came to the Kaaba, took off the clothes they had arrived in and wore clothes belonging to the people of the place, and that whoever could not get such clothes circled it naked, while they still associated others with God. (needs_source: the account of pre-Islamic practice is quoted without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 01:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 01:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 01:02:30}
- He says that taking from the Qur'an only what pleases one and leaving the rest is forbidden. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 01:03:30}
- He relates that some of those who had believed went to Abdullah al-Ansari, the father of Jabir, called him their elder and asked him to come with them to meet the Messenger. See [[person/jabir-ibn-abdullah|Jabir ibn Abdullah]]. (needs_source: the account is quoted without a chain and its setting is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 01:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 01:05:00}
- He relates that the Prophet told Jabir that God never spoke to anyone except from behind a veil but spoke to Jabir's father without a veil and told him to ask, that the father asked to be returned to the world, and that God answered that those who have died do not return. See [[person/jabir-ibn-abdullah|Jabir ibn Abdullah]]. (needs_source: the report is quoted without a chain or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 01:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 01:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 01:07:00}
- He relates that an imam, whose name is garbled in both transcripts, on seeing people weep over their dead said it was strange that travellers weep over a traveller who has reached his stop. (needs_source: the saying is quoted without a chain and the imam's name is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 01:08:00}
- He says a good ending points to the soundness of a person's deeds, that such a person must have had a clean record and a heart busy with remembering God, and tells of a man who fought against the Muslims, came to the Prophet, said he would become Muslim and fight with him, asked whether he would enter Paradise and was told yes, then said the testimony of faith, fought, was killed and entered Paradise. See [[concept/husn-al-khatima|a good ending]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the story is quoted without a chain and the man is not named) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 01:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 01:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep39 01:15:00}
